# Improvements list

The changes to make to the pipeline itself: what to change, why, and where the idea came from. Not to be confused with the upkeep (`upkeep.md`): what a person running the pipeline does, and when. An item that is done is deleted here, and the commit that does it says so; its *From:* credit moves first ([`upkeep.md`](upkeep.md#when-an-item-of-the-improvements-list-is-done)). Measured results that an item rests on are in [`findings.md`](findings.md).

## The whole pipeline

- **Batch processing for step 060 (done on 2026-11-17).** Step 060 has a new setting, `batch` (`false` by default). With `true`, stage 3 sends its model calls as batch jobs to the maker's batch API: Anthropic's Message Batches API, OpenAI's Batch API or Google's Gemini Batch API, each of which bills a batch request at half the price of a single request ([Anthropic](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/batch-processing), [OpenAI](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/batch), [Google](https://aistudio.google.com/docs/batch-api), read 2026-11-17). Why: the TP's runs go through the makers' own APIs (*The three makers' own APIs as services*, below), and none of them needs its answers at once. What the code does: each request is built, and each answer judged, by the code of a single request, and the answers are stored in the same cache under the same keys. The job ids are kept in the step's cache folder, so the next run collects the jobs of a stopped run instead of paying for them again. An errored or expired request counts as a failed call, as an error of a single request does, and is asked again on the next run. Anthropic's cached prompt start is marked for the 1-hour cache, as Anthropic advises for batches, and OpenAI is asked to keep a job's results for 7 days instead of 30. Through LiteLLM or Ask Sage the setting stops the step before any call. Only 060 has the setting: each later stage of 040 depends on the stage before it, and in 050 "batch" already names a drafted batch. In full: `060_extract/060_extract.md`, *Batch processing*. Verified: the tests, with each maker's batch API faked (`tests/test_batch.py`: sending, waiting, results out of order, an errored and an expired request, a job collected by the next run, the cache, the token counts, the early stop, a second ask, the refusals). Not verified: a real batch job. On 2026-11-17 Google's batch API refused every job from the Google project without billing (*400 FAILED_PRECONDITION. Precondition check failed.*), for each model tried and with or without `thinking_config`, so whether it accepts `thinking_config` in a batch request isn't known; no Anthropic or OpenAI key was available. The first paid batch run should therefore be small (two records, as in `060_extract/060_extract.md`, *How to run*), and its report read before a large one.
- **Recorded decisions and code brought into line (found and done on 2026-11-16).** An audit of the recorded decisions against the code found the mismatches below. (1) Step 060 can extract from the finished tuning records only: `extract_from` takes `tuning` beside `ground_truth` and `all`, for the runs of item (3b) of the entry below (`py 060_extract/run.py --extract_from tuning --schema annotations/ground_truth_schema.txt`). A note counts the finished records left aside, and step 070, evaluating such a run, says that the setting left the held-out records out, in place of advising to run 060 again. (2) Held-out results are kept per run. A run of step 070 that looks at the held-out part keeps its held-out records' counts in `held_out_evaluations/<run id>.json`, apart from the tuning file, with the look's line of `annotations/held_out_looks.csv`. `py helpers/held_out_results.py` summarizes the looks kept by recipe, pooled over the repeats as `py helpers/compare_runs.py` pools, with and without records left out on request (`--leave_out`), and says how many looks the log holds for each extraction and whether the fingerprints of the translation and of the held-out records' ground truth were the same in every look. It reads kept results only: no step is run, no look is added, and no model is called (`helpers/held_out_results.md`). (3) `py helpers/analysis/rescore_runs.py` gave step 070 three of its four inputs from step 060, so `extracted_triples_removed.csv` was read from 060's live folder, a later run's. It now gives all four, each found by the sha256 its run's manifest records (`common/common_helpers/step.py`, `output_of_run`, which `py helpers/partial_pairs.py` uses too), because a run's folder in `previous_runs/` holds only the files the next run changed; when no copy of a file is left it stops, saying which. The numbers of `docs/analysis/rescore_2026-11-13.md` stand: the live file has the sha256 that each of the six runs' manifests records (checked on 2026-11-16). (4) Docs corrected to what the code does: 040 draws 138 texts with the harvest of 2026-10-01, not 152 (*The sample of texts*, section *040_induce_schema*); 060's guide said repeat runs didn't exist; and four entries of this file, each with a dated note (the split by drafting model, the rule 19 marks in the second judge, the row of the second opinion table without an id, and the words "thing" and "one individual"). (5) Step 070 scores a kept run of step 060 (done later on 2026-11-16): the setting `extraction_run` takes 060's run id and finds that run's four files by the sha256 its manifest records (`common/common_helpers/step.py`, `outputs_of_run` and `gather_run`), copied with that manifest into `070_evaluate/extractions/<run id>/` when they aren't all in the run's own folder, since step 070 reads 060's manifest from the folder of the extracted triples. It stops, saying what to do, when the run isn't kept, when a file has no copy left, when one of the four files is given on the command line too, and when a file read names another run. `py helpers/analysis/rescore_runs.py` now gives step 070 its runs this way, after checking all six first, in place of copying the files itself. So the repeats of a recipe can all be extracted before they are scored, and each of the 15 extraction runs is scored once on the held-out part with `py 070_evaluate/run.py --extraction_run <060 run id> --evaluate_held_out true`, whose look's line names that run (`070_evaluate/070_evaluate.md`, *Scoring a kept run of 060*). (6) The held-out results by drafting model (done later on 2026-11-16): each evaluation step 070 keeps, tuning or held-out, now keeps each record's counts by provenance grouping, and `py helpers/held_out_results.py` gives, for each recipe, the numbers of 070's subsection *By who drafted the ground truth*: recall at both levels, the exact share of pairs and entity-class accuracy per drafting model, and precision and F1 as 070's attributed values (*What is reported* in the entry below), pooled over the repeats as its other numbers, with and without `--leave_out`. Both use 070's grouping code (`070_evaluate/070_evaluate_helpers/stats.py`, `group_parts`, which `by_group` now reads too, so the report's numbers are unchanged). A look kept before this change has no such counts, and the summary says so. Nothing any step computes changed: the same prompts, fingerprints and numbers. The tests: `tests/test_common.py` (*FairSampleByPart*, *ExtractWithoutGroundTruth*, *OutputOfRun*, with the setting `extraction_run` and its near-misses), `tests/test_evaluate_report.py` (*HeldOutLook*, *HeldOutResults*, with the numbers by drafting model worked out by hand) and `tests/test_pipeline.py` (`test_t10_held_out_look_logged`, `test_t10c_score_a_kept_extraction`: a kept run scored and its look logged, the live run named, a run id never kept, a file given twice and a kept file with no copy left). From: the audit of recorded decisions against the code, 2026-11-16.
- **The text the models receive, reworded for clarity (done on 2026-11-16).** Wording only, so that every model receives the clearest wording: no rule, part, case or example fact was added, removed or moved, no entity class or predicate was added or renamed, and no case is decided differently. What was unclear: vague verbs, pronouns with no clear antecedent, instructions buried in long sentences, elliptical sentences, and one concept named two ways. In the rules (`annotations/guidelines.md`, and so the model's copy, rebuilt with `py helpers/build_rules.py`): rule 1 (the one other use of what you know, which rule 23 now "allows"; *A name the record does not explain*; *Comparison*, now "without stating what it is compared with"; *Membership by name*), rule 2 (*A fact inside a name*), rule 8 (*A study a record tells*, now "Nothing else in the study is a fact of the record's dataset"), rule 9 (*A word that only says how another fact holds*; *What data are used for*), rule 11 (a word that a rule takes out but the record needs, and the exception for a name made of a code and a version), rule 17 (*A platform in an instrument's name*; *An instrument in a channel's name*), rule 19 (*A country*), rule 22 (*Lists joined by "/" or "+"*), rule 23 (the tie in test 1) and rule 24 (which instances lose the words for the input and the method). Three invented examples of the model's copy were reworded too (`annotations/rules_model_differences.txt`). In the ground truth schema, the definitions of Organization, Document, PhysicalQuantity, Country, MEASURES, HAS_VERSION, HAS_DOI, HAS_COORDINATE_SYSTEM, HAS_ACCESS_RIGHTS, HAS_ISSUE and METADATA_FOR. In code, the line under PREDICATES of every schema a prompt shows, and the line that explains the rule 19 marks (`common/common_helpers/schema_io.py`, `READS` and `MARKS_READ`). In the prompts of 040, 060 and 070 and in the parts that 040, 050 and 060 share: unclear pronouns, the keys of the data named as the data name them ("texts", "examples"), and each list a prompt points to named by its heading. Three wordings had two readings, and each is now worded as the ground truth and the rules already decide it. (1) MEASURES said "an Instrument's"; it now says that its subject is an Instrument, or a Platform that carries Instruments, as in the ground truth, whose MEASURES triples have 13 Instrument subjects and 10 Platform subjects (the NASA ER-2 and NASA DC-8 aircraft). (2) Country is a nation only as the place where a Person or Organization is based, and a nation that the data cover is a SpatialExtent, as rule 19 (*A country*) decides; the ground truth's 3 Country instances are all objects of BASED_IN. (3) Rule 23's tie in test 1 ended "the wording that says more is used", which repeats test 1 itself; it now says to use the wording that tells more about the entity, such as whose it is, the decision of the rule's own example ("BARREL PI" over "principal investigator, PI"), and the ground truth triples found with such a tie, the 4 triples "BARREL PI" SERVES_AS, follow it. For each changed rule, the ground truth triples whose `rules` cite it (`annotations/rules_by_triple.md`; rules 2, 11 and 17 apply to all 1,494 classed triples) and the items of `annotations/left_out.md` that quote or cite it were checked against the new wording, and none is decided differently. The quotes of the changed words in `annotations/rules_by_triple.md`, `annotations/left_out.md` and `annotations/questions_for_experts.md` follow the new wording. The rows of the translation table whose ground truth definitions changed are checked again when the table is rebuilt for the next induced schema. The changes were made by a model (Claude Opus 5.5) and are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. The text the models receive changed, so the fingerprints of the prompts of 040, 050, 060 and 070 change, and their next runs ask every call again. From: my request of 2026-11-16.
- **The Technical Publication's new baseline (decided on 2026-11-14; supersedes the plan of 2026-11-09 below and *Extraction with a model of a second maker (OpenAI)*).** The TP's runs set a new baseline, not a comparison with the TM: the rules, the ground truth (40 tuning and 40 held-out records) and every prompt changed since the TM, so no TM number is directly comparable. The plan: (1) schema induction (step 040) run again with the current rules, then a new translation table checked on tuning records only; (2) extraction (step 060) with one model of each of three makers, matched in capability and each available through NASA's LiteLLM or Ask Sage: Claude Opus 5.5, Gemini 4 Argon and GPT-6.1 Sol, all at reasoning "high" (Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index 52, 50 and 50 at that level, read 2026-11-14; Google's best model on these services sets the tier); (3) 5 repeats per model (*Repeat runs, to measure run-to-run noise*, below), each extracting from all 80 finished records (tuning and held-out), whose held-out scores stay unseen until (4); (3b) the same three models, 5 repeats each, extracting from the 40 finished tuning records (`--extract_from tuning`) with the ground truth schema in place of the induced schema, so that the TP's comparison of the two schemas (section 7.4) is measured on the new runs (decided on 2026-11-16; it about doubles the extraction cost); (4) the held-out evaluation, once, after the ground truth, the guidelines, the ground truth schema and the translation table are frozen in a tagged commit. The runs go through each maker's own API (Anthropic, Google, OpenAI), paid from our own accounts, not through LiteLLM or Ask Sage, whose budgets are used up (`docs/findings.md`, 2026-11-02 and 2026-11-03). [DOJA] Before the runs: the code gains the three makers' APIs as services, each sent its maker's documented reasoning setting (Claude's effort included, which the Anthropic API takes directly, so the LiteLLM effort check is no longer needed), the service recorded in every cache key and report as before; a one-call test of each; a cost estimate per run. Known bias to report: Claude Opus 5.5 wrote 1,315 of the 1,494 ground truth triples (count of `drafted_by`, 2026-11-14), so its extraction scores are favored. What is reported (made exact on 2026-11-16): each run of step 070 scores one extraction model, and within each part its report splits the ground truth triples by the model that drafted their row (`drafted_by`; subsection *By who drafted the ground truth*). For each drafting model it gives recall, the exact share of pairs and entity-class accuracy, which are numbers of that group's own ground truth triples and pairs. Precision can't be split the same way, because an extracted triple left without a pair has no drafting model. The report gives an attributed precision and F1 per group instead (the group's pairs over the counted occurrences less the other groups' pairs), in which every occurrence left without a pair counts against each group alike. The other metrics (schema fit, describes accuracy, the recall upper bounds, recall within reach) are reported by extraction model only. Held-out records #101, #119 and #125 nearly repeat other records' notes (similarity 0.81 to 0.89) without being caught by the identical-text check: report the held-out results with and without them (#N is the pool position, and all three are finished held-out records, checked on 2026-11-16; `py helpers/held_out_results.py --leave_out 101,119,125 <run ids>` gives both, from the looks kept). The audit of what the TM left open is in my TP folder (not in Git).
- **The three makers' own APIs as services (done on 2026-11-14).** The setting `service` now also takes `anthropic`, `openai` and `google`: each reaches the maker's own API through its official Python package (`anthropic`, `openai`, `google-genai`, pinned in `requirements.txt` and imported only when that service is used), with the key `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` or `GEMINI_API_KEY` from `.env`. Each request sends the maker's documented settings and no temperature: Claude's `max_tokens` and `output_config.effort` (streamed, with the shared prompt start marked for prompt caching), OpenAI's `max_completion_tokens` and `reasoning_effort`, Google's `max_output_tokens` and `thinking_level` (sources in `common/common_helpers/llm.py`, `DIRECT_SERVICES`). The guards are those of LiteLLM: the service, model and thinking level in the cache key, the served model version in the run's record, cut-offs by the maker's finish reason, token counts (cache reads and writes, thinking) in *Model calls*, the budget and rate-limit handling, the early stop, the test call, the free commands' guarantee and the question before paying. The SDKs' own retries are off, so every request is counted. Server-side refusal fallbacks (Anthropic's `fallbacks`) are not sent, since another model's answer would mix models within one run. Checked with the SDKs faked (`tests/test_model_calls.py`, *DirectServices*); not yet run against the real APIs. Still to do before the runs: `pip install -r requirements.txt` and the three keys on the laptop, one test call each (`docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *Choosing a model*), and a cost estimate per run. Not verified in the makers' docs: the Python type names of google-genai's timeout and connection errors (read from the error's type name), and whether Gemini's prompt token count includes its cached tokens.
- **Checks on every push, and the model client in parts (done on 2026-11-15).** GitHub now runs the linter (ruff), the type checker (pyright) and the tests on every push and pull request, on Windows with Python 3.14 (`.github/workflows/tests.yml`). The commands, to run on any computer, are in `README.md`, *Checks*. `pyproject.toml` describes the project and holds the settings of both tools. Ruff runs only rules that find defects: pyflakes, syntax errors, pylint's errors, and the checks of flake8-bugbear for code that does something other than it seems. Pyright runs its basic level, with the checks that the code's layout makes unreliable turned off, each with its reason in `pyproject.toml`. The packages are still listed once, in `requirements.txt`, which `pyproject.toml` reads; the checking tools are pinned in `requirements-dev.txt`. Besides tidying (36 imports never used, code written twice, type annotations that said the wrong type), setting them up found five defects that change what the code does, and four are fixed. (1) The test that compares the answer limit and the read timeout named in the docs with the code's matched nothing, since its patterns held a backspace character where `\b` was meant; it now checks a number of tokens only right after the limit's name, with near misses. (2) Checking the quotes of `annotations/questions_for_experts.md` against a file that isn't UTF-8 stopped with a NameError, since the check used `Stop` without importing it. (3) On a fresh checkout, `tests/run_all.py` reported that the tests had changed the repository, because the compiled files Python writes in the repository folder itself weren't left out of its comparison. (4) A string in a test held an escape sequence Python no longer accepts silently. The fifth is the next item. In the same change, `common/common_helpers/llm.py` (2,066 lines) became `llm.py`, which holds the run's model and every setting a person may change, and seven parts beside it, one per task: the errors and the two stops (`llm_errors.py`), what a reply is (`llm_replies.py`), each service's client (`llm_asksage.py`, `llm_litellm.py`, `llm_makers.py`), sending a prompt (`llm_calls.py`), and a paid run (`llm_paid.py`). Every name is still imported from `common.llm`, and each statement was moved unchanged, so the outputs, prompts and cache keys are the same. Mypy was considered instead of pyright and not used, because it can't check the steps' folders in one run: their helper files share names (`moves.py`, `records.py`). Still open: whether the tests pass on Linux, which hasn't been tried; GitHub runs them on Windows only.
- **Nothing discarded without a record (decided and done on 2026-11-15).** The rule: anything any stage of any step discards is recorded, item by item, never only dropped and never only counted. Every pipeline step now writes `discarded.jsonl` in its output folder on every run, one line per item a stage removed, filtered, skipped, capped, folded into another (a duplicate, a merge: with what it was folded into) or whose value it replaced, each with its stage, its reason (and the setting and its value, when a setting decided it), its own words and values, its record id and where it came from; its report gets the section *Items discarded*, counting them by stage and reason; and where a stage takes items in and keeps some, an accounting check (items in = items kept + items recorded) stops the run when an item vanishes without a record. One implementation for every step, `common/common_helpers/dropped.py` (beside the records left out after a failed model call, which it also records), written by `run_step`; the file's fields: `helpers/audit.md`, *Items discarded*; what each step records: the row `discarded.jsonl` of its guide's *Outputs*. Nothing the pipeline computes changed: the same kept outputs, prompts and numbers. Not recorded item by item, by choice: 020's edits inside one record's fields (the HTML markup note cleaning removes, the CKAN fields 020 doesn't keep, a format listed twice, a tag without a name), whose raw values stay in 010's batch files, reachable through each record's origin; a report's tables cut at their first rows, whose full lists are in the files they name; and the merging of one component class's spellings in 070's per-component-class rows, which keeps every count. A row of `annotations/second_opinion.csv` with values but no id was still skipped without an entry when this entry was written; step 070 records it as discarded since the same day (the next entry, item 7; noted on 2026-11-16). 040's stage 7 (definitions) records what it doesn't take but has no accounting check, since an entry sent may come back with no definition and is kept all the same (stage 8 marks it).
- **No patterns and no pins in any prompt, and code checks of what the model writes (decided and done on 2026-11-15).** Every prompt that shows a schema (060's extraction, with the induced schema or the ground truth schema; 050's drafting; the second opinion and the second judge, through 050; the worked examples' example schema) now shows its entity classes and predicates with their definitions and no pattern or pin, written one way (`common/common_helpers/schema_io.py`, `schema_text`; the line under PREDICATES says only "Each definition reads from the subject to the object."). The prompts that write or check the ground truth also show the rule 19 marks, since the next entry. The patterns and the `only:` lines stay in the files, and code checks every row against them as before (`pattern_not_in_schema`, `pattern_not_pinned`). Why the patterns: a schema's patterns list what a sample contained, so a labeling mistake becomes a listed pattern and a correct pattern the sample lacked is missing; and the ground truth schema's patterns include those of the finished tuning records' ground truth, which would show extraction evaluated on those records part of its answers. Why the pins: a predicate's definition is the one home of its meaning, so the seven pinned predicates whose definitions didn't name their pinned entity class now do (HAS_PURPOSE, HAS_SPATIAL_RESOLUTION, HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION, HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION, HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA, CLEANED_OF, HAS_CHANGE; their meaning unchanged, and the translation table's copies of the definitions updated with them), and a test (`tests/test_schema_io.py`, *DefinitionsNamePins*) keeps every entity class of an `only:` line in its predicate's definition, beside the older test of the converse. The induced schema of 2026-10-12 as 060 shows it: 58,123 characters with its 1,233 patterns, 24,588 without (measured on 2026-11-15). 050's prompt no longer shows the file as written either. Its header prose went with the patterns, and so did its rule 19 marks at first, until the next entry brought them back on a line under each entity class. The coined component classes still follow the schema. With it, the same day, the code checks that the audit of code against the model (2026-11-15) asked for: (1) rule 20's abbreviation test (Schwartz and Hearst, 2003), implemented once (`common/common_helpers/abbreviations.py`), flags a reply of 040's stage 2, 050 or 060 that disagrees with it (`abbreviation_fails_test`, `also_known_as_fails_test`, `identifier_passes_test`, `abbreviation_spelled_out`: 050's guide, *Checks on each row*); nothing is changed by it; (2) 040's defining (stage 7) sends each predicate's patterns from counting and the list of entity classes being defined, a definition may name them (optional), and code asks once more about an entry left out and about a definition naming a word shaped like an entity class that is none, as labeling asks again; (3) 040's splitting (stage 5) shows every predicate found, since code refuses any of their names for a new predicate, not only those of the predicates sent, and its prompt states the test for splitting once (a different verb for each group of triple instances by their object's entity class); (4) 040 folds label spellings before the merge call, and the running vocabulary of labeling shows one spelling of each label (the most frequent spelling still wins); (5) 070 names a proposed predicate row whose `swap_subject_and_object` disagrees with the patterns of both schemas, for the person checking it; (6) 040 takes the describes class the most text pieces named, as 050 and 060 do, and 050 writes entity classes and predicates in the ground truth schema's spelling, as 060 writes its schema's (`common/common_helpers/extraction.py`, `in_schema_spelling`); (7) a row of `annotations/second_opinion.csv` with values but no id is recorded as discarded by 070. Every prompt's fingerprint changes, so the new baseline is run after this. From: the audit of code against the model, 2026-11-15.
- **Rule 19 marks only where the ground truth is written or checked (decided and done on 2026-11-15).** The ground truth schema marks each entity class `rule 19: standalone` or `rule 19: describing`. The mark is my decision of where a qualifier goes under rule 19 of *Writing a classed triple* (`annotations/guidelines.md`, *Whose fact it is*). The change above (*No patterns and no pins in any prompt*) left the marks out of every prompt. They are now shown again, each on a line under its entity class as the file writes it, in the prompts whose job is to write or check the ground truth: 050's drafting, the second opinion (which drafts with 050's prompt to find the facts the ground truth lacks) and the second judge's facts task (`helpers/helpers_prompts/second_judge_facts.txt`, which shows the ground truth schema as 050's prompt shows it). The second judge's other task, on partial pairs (`helpers/helpers_prompts/second_judge_pairs.txt`), shows the record and the two triples and no schema, so it shows no marks (made exact on 2026-11-16). Drafting and the second opinion follow my decisions, and the second judge judges them; nothing any of them produces is scored against the ground truth. Extraction (060) shows no marks, with the induced schema or with the ground truth schema. Extraction is scored against the ground truth, and the marks are my answer key for where a qualifier goes, which the induced schema doesn't have. Showing them with the ground truth schema would therefore favour it in the comparison of the two schemas. Both extractions apply rule 19 by meaning, under the same rules. One switch does it (`common/common_helpers/schema_io.py`, `schema_text`, `rule_19_marks`). With the marks, one line before the entity classes says what a mark says, and that an entity class without one (a new entity class not marked yet, or a coined one) is to be decided by the rules. What standalone and describing mean stays in the rules alone (their words section, and `annotations/guidelines.md` for the second judge). The worked examples' example schema moved to a file of its own (`common/common_prompts/extraction_example_schema.txt`), marked, and each prompt shows it as it shows its own schema: 050's with the marks, 060's without, so 060's prompt text is unchanged. Its marks follow rule 19 and the examples' answers. Standalone: Dataset, Instrument, Spacecraft, Organization, Algorithm, MissionPhase and CelestialBody, as in the ground truth schema; Content, since example 2 gives "albedo maps" the target taken out of it, the maps having facts of their own; and Channel, a band of an instrument with facts of its own, such as its wavelength. Describing: PhysicalQuantity, Purpose (example 1 gives Halden the target taken out of its purpose), SpatialExtent, Version, Time and ProcessingLevel, as in the ground truth schema; and TemporalResolution, SpatialResolution and AngleRange, amounts like the ground truth schema's Value. The marks stay in the file, and code uses them as before. 050's prompt changes, so the next runs of 050, the second opinion and the second judge's facts task ask the model again; 060's prompt and the second judge's partial pairs prompt don't change. The tests: `tests/test_schema_io.py`, *RuleNineteenMarksInPrompts*, and `tests/test_common.py`, *WorkedExamples*.
- **Three control characters in the model's copy of the rules (found on 2026-11-15; done on 2026-11-15).** When a replace entry of `annotations/rules_model_differences.txt` leaves words out, `common/common_helpers/model_rules.py` tidies the punctuation after them with a regular expression whose replacement is the control character U+0001, where `\1` (the punctuation matched) was meant. As a result, `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`, which steps 040, 050 and 060 send to the model, holds U+0001 in three places where a punctuation mark belongs: after "in the title's wording", after "in the record's order", and after "unless a rule leaves it out". The fix is one character in the code and a rebuild (`py helpers/build_rules.py`). It changes the prompt's text, so the fingerprints of the prompts of 040, 050 and 060 change and their next runs ask again. Fixed on 2026-11-15 before any run used the rules of 2026-11-14: the replacement is now ``, and the rebuilt copy has a full stop after "in the title's wording" and after "unless a rule leaves it out", and a comma after "in the record's order". From: the code review behind the checks above, 2026-11-15.
- **The Technical Publication's experiments (from 2026-11-09).** The TM (NASA/TM–20260010350) is being extended into a Technical Publication, to be submitted by 2026-11-20 (STI review takes 10 business days and can finish after my NASA access ends). What it adds: extraction by model: a second model of another developer (Gemini, through Ask Sage) on the 20 finished tuning records, 3 repeats with the induced schema and 3 with the ground truth schema, so that the metrics by drafting model show whether each model scores higher on the ground truth triples its own family drafted. Rule for every new draft: one drafting model only, Gemini 2.5 Pro (050's default), so that a drafting model's influence has one source; the 20 finished records keep their mixed drafts (263 Claude Opus 5.5, 149 Gemini 2.5 Pro, 4 Gemini 4 Argon), reported by drafting model. Measured cost: one extraction run on the 20 tuning records sends about 0.86 million tokens (849,000 of them the cached 42,450-token prefix, 20 calls) and receives about 0.09 million (060 reports of 2026-11-01 and 2026-11-02); so one model's six runs need about 5.7 million tokens. Ask Sage grants more tokens on request when they run out, about 15 minutes later, and a stopped run restarts from its stored answers. A held-out evaluation (held-out records annotated by hand, guidelines, ground truth schema and translation table frozen first) was planned and set aside on 2026-11-09 for the time it needs; it stays to do. From: the decisions of 2026-11-09.

- **Repeat runs, to measure run-to-run noise (the setting is done; this entry brought into line with the code on 2026-11-16, when it still read as a to-do).** What exists: the setting `repeat` of steps 040, 050, 060 and 070 and of `py helpers/second_judge.py`: `1` (the default) reuses the saved answers, and `2`, `3`, ... ask again, each repeat into cached answers of its own, since the repeat is part of the cache key from 2 on (`common/common_helpers/llm.py`, `REPEAT`; `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *Settings several steps share*). `py helpers/second_opinion.py` drafts each record in several repeats of its own (its setting `repeats`, 3 by default). `py helpers/compare_runs.py` pools the repeats of a recipe and gives their spread, and so does `py helpers/held_out_results.py` for the held-out looks; step 070 scores any kept run of step 060 (setting `extraction_run`), so the repeats can all be extracted before they are scored. Still to do: the repeats themselves, 5 for each recipe the Technical Publication reports (*The Technical Publication's new baseline*, items (3) and (3b)), before model access ends on 2026-11-20. Why repeats: one run isn't a baseline: two runs of step 040 with the same settings, differing only by one rule of the prompt (rule 8 of *What counts as a fact*), spelled 41 of the 63 entity classes and 67 of the 126 predicates the same. From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-12. Extended on 2026-11-01: **for the papers, 5 repeats of the baseline** (3 for each change). Why: the standard deviation of *k* repeats has *k* − 1 degrees of freedom, so from 3 repeats it is known only to within a factor of about 6 upward (95% interval 0.52 to 6.3 times the value measured), from 5 to within about 3 (0.60 to 2.9 times), by the chi-square interval for a standard deviation (NIST/SEMATECH e-Handbook of Statistical Methods, [section 1.3.5.8](https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda358.htm)); the decision rule averages each recipe's repeats, so a baseline of 5 and a change of 3 compare fairly (`helpers/compare_runs.md`, *Did a change help?*). The smallest version planned then (repeats 4 and 5 of the baseline recipe after the 3 planned) is superseded by the 5 repeats per model of the new baseline (noted on 2026-11-16). From (extension): the research of 2026-10-31 on repeated runs, section 4.
- **Repeats that LiteLLM may answer from its own cache (2026-10-31).** On 2026-10-31, `py helpers/check_effort.py`, run through NASA's LiteLLM with `claude-opus-5.5`, sent each setting the same request 3 times and got exactly the same completion-token count each time: 717, 717, 717 (no effort sent); 814, 814, 814 (`low`); 802, 802, 802 (`high`), from a model sent no temperature and with adaptive thinking. That suggests the proxy answers a repeated identical request from a cache of its own ([LiteLLM caching](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/caching): "The second response is served from the cache", with the header `x-litellm-cache-key`). If so, the runs of a recipe's repeats (setting `repeat`) are copies of one run, and their spread says nothing about the model's own variation. Done on 2026-10-31: `py helpers/check_answer_cache.py` (4 tiny paid calls, asked about before sending; `running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *Checking LiteLLM's answer cache*) sends one question twice as it is and twice with LiteLLM's per-request control `{"cache": {"no-cache": true, "no-store": true}}` ([LiteLLM cache controls](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/caching_controls)), and decides by a rule printed before the calls: "LiteLLM answers from its cache" if the second plain call carries `x-litellm-cache-key` or returns a byte-identical answer with identical usage, while the no-cache calls differ from each other or carry no `x-litellm-cache-key`; "no answer cache seen" if no call carries the header and the two plain answers differ; "not shown" otherwise, with the reason; and separately whether the proxy accepted the control (no HTTP 4xx). The bypass itself is written (`common/common_helpers/llm.py`, `ANSWER_CACHE_CONTROL`): with `LITELLM_BYPASS_ANSWER_CACHE=1` in `.env`, every LiteLLM request of 040, 050, 060, 070 and the checks carries the control, never one through Ask Sage; the words the model gets, the cache keys and the fingerprints don't change; each run's record (`answer_cache`) and report (*Model calls*) say "LiteLLM answer cache: bypassed" or "not bypassed", and 070's recipe carries it, so `py helpers/compare_runs.py` lists runs with and without it under *What differs*. It is off by default because LiteLLM's docs don't say whether a proxy with its cache off accepts the control, so it is switched on by the check's evidence. Done on 2026-11-01: the check said "LiteLLM answers from its cache", the proxy accepted the control, and `LITELLM_BYPASS_ANSWER_CACHE=1` is in the NASA laptop's `.env` since that day (`findings.md`, 2026-11-01); no repeat had been run through LiteLLM before then. From: `findings.md`, 2026-10-31 (`check_effort.py`'s counts).
- **Records left out, and replies asked again, counted in every report (done on 2026-10-30, prompt audit F17).** A record whose model call fails is left out of everything a run computes, so if such records cluster (e.g. the model's safety filter refusing one maintainer's biology records) the numbers describe a sample without them. Steps 040, 050 and 060 now count the records they leave out by reason (refused, cut off, malformed, service error, other) and by maintainer, in the report's section *Records left out* and the details file's key `left_out`; 070 does the same for the finished records 060's calls failed for, apart from those 060 didn't choose. Every model-calling step's report (subsection *Replies asked again*) and manifest also count the replies that came back unusable the first time (not JSON, or JSON of the wrong shape), how many asking again fixed, and how many stayed unusable. One implementation, `common/common_helpers/dropped.py`, with `tests/test_dropped.py`. From: the prompt audit of 2026-10-30.
- **The prompts revised for the baseline (done on 2026-10-30, prompt audit F5, F7, F8, F10, F15, F16, F19).** For 050, 060 and 070 (not 040, which is not rerun: `tests/allowed_differences.txt`): F5, the order schema, rules, worked examples, task, reply format, record, then a closing line after the record (050's and 060's guides, *Prompts*); F7, two invented worked examples, each record with its complete answer and a small example schema, shared by 050 and 060 (`common/common_prompts/extraction_examples.txt`), checked by `tests/test_common.py`, *WorkedExamples*; F8, the model's copy of the rules numbered and ordered like the guidelines (rules 1 to 24), long rules split into the rule, its test and one example, repeats and stray line pieces removed, every other difference listed in `tests/allowed_differences.txt` and checked by `tests/test_docs.py`, *RulesInStep*; F10, every reply format valid JSON as written, with placeholders such as "<subject>", and one header style (a header line in capitals, no colon); F15, plain wording instead of capitals for emphasis; F16, the rules say once that predicate words in examples describe a fact, not a predicate's name, and that an unconnected triple is kept when the fact connecting it can't be written with the predicates allowed; F19, every run record says what the service may do to a request that the record can't see (`common/common_helpers/llm.py`, `SERVICE_NOTES`). The fingerprints of 050's, 060's and 070's prompts changed, so their next runs ask again (070: a few calls).
- **Drift guards: things that should be the same across the steps (done on 2026-10-30, from the test audit).** `tests/test_parallel.py` compares, member by member, the prompts' data guard and JSON line (070's two prompts gained the guard), the blocks 050 and 060 share and their layout, the settings 040, 050, 060 and 070 share (050's and 060's `max_chars` must be equal, no exception), how each stage calls the model (a declared table: a new stage, or one that calls differently, fails), and how 040 reads replies (it now reads a number as text, as 050 and 060 do). Every difference is listed, with its reason, in one file, `tests/allowed_differences.txt` (it replaces the rules-only list of the same day); a difference not listed fails, and so does a listed one that no longer exists. Also tested now: what a run records about its model calls is what the request sent, for each service and model (Ask Sage takes no answer limit, so its runs now record the limit as not sent and say a cut-off can't be detected there); each step's manifest names its prompt files by the fingerprint of their text, also after a rerun answered from the cache; the numbers the docs restate from the code (the answer limit, the read timeout, the attempts); `tests/README.md` lists every test file; and 070's recipe for `helpers/compare_runs.py` now carries how 060's calls were sent (the exact model, the answer limit, temperature, thinking, effort). 070's report names its translation calls by batch, not as one call, and the per-text tables of 040, 050 and 060 label their first row the same way.
- **One selection of the ground truth (done on 2026-10-31, edge-case audit, group G1).** Which ground truth records count was decided again by each of eleven consumers (050's prompt and checks, 060's choice of records and its suggested sources, 070's records and its stop on coined component classes, the status, `run_pipeline.py`, the expert questions, the annotation tool's suggestions, pattern flags and `source:` lines, and three readers of a `source:` line), and they disagreed. Measured on 2026-10-31: 32 coined component classes (6 entity classes, 26 predicates) and 48 patterns came only from the unfinished batches 4 and 5, so step 050's prompt showed the model its own uncorrected drafts as the ground truth schema, and its pattern check stayed silent for them; a record in two files crashed `py run_pipeline.py --status` (KeyError). Now `common/common_helpers/ground_truth.py` is the one place: each record has one state (absent, in two files, a bad mark, unfinished, finished), its part comes from the pool, and every consumer asks `gt.select(purpose)` for its rows: *schema evidence* (finished tuning records: everything that shapes the ground truth schema a model or a check sees, through `ground_truth_schema_view`), *evaluable* (finished records, by part: 060 and 070), *annotated* (every record in a file: 050 never drafts one again; the tool's rename, merge, overview and held-out guard) and *to fix*. No code outside that file reads the rows itself (a test guards it). One reader of a `source:` line, `common/common_helpers/schema_io.parse_source`: a position counts as held-out by its part in the pool, whatever word stands before it ("ground truth tuning #12, #8" names held-out #8; 070's note used to look for the word "held-out"). The annotation tool writes a `source:` line naming only finished tuning records, and the tuning record shown when its rows use the entry, since the person adding it from there has read that use (none when none does; added with G2 on the same day, since a record can no longer be ticked finished while a component class it uses is missing from the ground truth schema, so the record being annotated is never finished when its component classes are added). Patterns are frozen for held-out records too: a held-out record's pattern that no finished tuning record uses never joins the ground truth schema's patterns, and the tool says so (`annotations/guidelines.md`, *Before you start*). Step 060 given the ground truth schema (or a copy with the same content) checks the same patterns as 050. The status lists only what finished records coin (step 070 stops on it), never a model's draft. In the expert questions, "in the ground truth" means finished, both for a record's quoted triples and in *Affects*. A ground truth file is read only when named `batch_` and three digits or more, the rule 050 numbers by. What changes for a run: 050's prompt now shows no component class or pattern from the unfinished batches 4 and 5 (its fingerprint and cache keys change for the drafts that follow); 070's numbers don't change (it already evaluated finished records only).
- **One set of keys, and one tidying of every row (done on 2026-10-31, edge-case audit, groups G3 and G5).** "The same" was decided by several keys that disagreed. Now `common/common_helpers/triples_io.py` and `text_match.py` are the only homes: `component_class_key` (a component class: letters and digits of any script, case folded, accents, Unicode variants and invisible format characters evened out; it used to drop every letter outside a to z, so "Δ" counted as having no letter and "ΔT" was "t"), `instance_key` (a component instance), `triple_key` and `classed_key` (the same fact, and the same classed triple: the instances by `instance_key`, the predicate by `component_class_key`, a symmetric predicate either way round), `is_describes` and `is_undecided` (by `component_class_key`), and `passages` (the passages of a source text, the join " … " read in any of its ways: "…" or "...", with or without spaces). Every evened-out text (`text_match.even`) now drops invisible format characters (a soft hyphen, a zero-width space, a word joiner, a byte-order mark), so "Terra" + soft hyphen + "Aqua" is found as "TerraAqua" and step 060 no longer removes such a triple for its source text. Their users: the ground truth's repeat check (now the key of 070's exact pairs, so "1 km" and "1000 m" in one record are the same classed triple and 070 stops until one is deleted), 050's and 060's duplicate check, the partial pair reviews, the expert questions (triples, entity classes and predicates compared by the keys, not word for word), the translation table ("(None)" in any spelling read as `(none)`; shared targets by `component_class_key`), the schema readers (a repeated entry in any spelling is a repeat, in both shapes, the first kept), 060's list of component classes outside the schema and 070's describes classes in its report (counted by `component_class_key`), the annotation tool, and the annotation page's own key, `looseKey`, now the same rule (tested in headless Chrome on the same table, `tests/key_variants.py`). One tidying step, `canonical_row`, runs on every row before any check: a model's (one line, spaces evened out, a JSON number written as the record's text writes it, so 1.5 is "1.50" where the text says "1.50") and the ground truth's, as read. A describes triple of the model's own among its classed triples is removed with the new reason `describes_triple` (it used to be kept as a second describes triple, and its CatalogEntry and DESCRIBES listed as component classes to add). Measured on 2026-10-31 on the ground truth: no repeat under the new key, no invisible format character in a finished record's text, and no component class whose key changes, so 070's numbers on the finished records don't move from these keys; text_match's `MATCHING_VERSION` is 3, so `py helpers/compare_runs.py` shows runs before and after as different. Considered and not changed: a leading "a" in an instance is still dropped ("A Train" is "train"; the rule is the documented one of *evened out*); a partial pair review is still filed under subject, predicate and object without the entity classes (two such ground truth triples with other entity classes are already a problem of the ground truth).
- **Evaluation runs only on checked rows, and its pairs don't depend on row order (done on 2026-10-31, edge-case audit, groups G2 and G4).** Step 070 trusted a record ticked finished as clean, but never checked its rows: an incomplete row was dropped without a word, a source text not in the record's text was ignored, an undecided describes class took the record out of describes accuracy, and two describes rows were flagged nowhere (070 kept the first, so describes accuracy followed row order). Now one gate, `common/common_helpers/ground_truth.py`'s `evaluable_problems`, runs the row checks on every finished record (tuning and held-out), a second describes triple among them (a new error, `several_describes`), the component classes the ground truth schema lacks, and every row of `annotations/pending_triples.csv` (a fact that waits on an expert with an entity class or predicate the ground truth schema lacks could never be matched, so the occurrence it waits for counted against precision); step 070 stops on any of them, naming file, line, problem and what to do. The annotation tool refuses to tick *All facts extracted* while a row of the record has a ✖ (the page unticks it, and the server saves such a record as not finished). The ground truth's repeat check uses the key 070's exact pairs use (G3). Pairing broke ties by row order: of two equally good partners (one with the wrong object class, one with the wrong subject class), the first row won, so subject-class and object-class accuracy changed when the rows were reordered. Now, after the most pairs, the most strict pairs and the fewest pairs matched with subject and object swapped, pairing takes the most entity classes that agree slot by slot, and a tie left is broken by the triples' contents (instances by `instance_key`, component classes by `component_class_key`, then as written), never by their order (`070_evaluate/metrics/pairs.md`); a test evaluates every test record with its rows shuffled in every order (60 random orders of the larger ones) and gets the same numbers. When text pieces of one record name different describes classes, the one named by the most pieces is kept (on a tie, the first) and the describes triple is flagged `describes_classes_differ`, no longer resolved without a word. Measured on 2026-10-31 on the ground truth: no finished record has a row error, a second describes triple, or a repeat under the new key, and no fact waits on an expert, so step 070 doesn't stop on the current data; whether its numbers move (ties now broken by contents, the keys and the tidied rows of G3 and G5) shows only on 060's extraction, which isn't on this computer: rerun `py 070_evaluate/run.py` on the laptop and compare with the last run (`py helpers/compare_runs.py`). Runs before and after are not comparable: 070's recipe now records `evaluation_version` 2 (and text_match's `matching_version` 3), so `py helpers/compare_runs.py` lists both under *What differs*.
- **One judgment of every model reply (done on 2026-11-01, edge-case audit, group G6).** A reply was judged only by LiteLLM's finish reason and by whether it parsed as JSON, so through Ask Sage (no finish reason, no answer limit) a cut-off answer counted as "not JSON": asked once more, paid twice on every rerun, and never kept as cut off (`docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`'s *A cut-off isn't paid for twice* held for LiteLLM only); a model declining in prose as HTTP 200 ("I'm sorry, but I can't …", finish reason `stop`) was asked once more and counted as *malformed*, not *refused*; a complete answer under a finish reason the code didn't know (`end_turn`) failed; the fence regex stripped ``` at any line start, also inside a value, and missed ```JSON; and `helpers/check_effort.py` read any HTTP 4xx (401, 404, 429) as "the setting is read". Now `classify_reply` in `common/common_helpers/llm_replies.py` is the one place that says what a reply is (complete, cut off, refused, empty, malformed), from the finish reason (the service's and the model maker's: the sets `COMPLETE_REASONS`, `CUT_OFF_REASONS`, `REFUSAL_REASONS`) and the content together (`find_json`: a fence only around the whole answer, the tag in any case; the first object in prose; an object left open at the end; several objects), and both service clients and `call_llm_json` go through it: a cut-off through Ask Sage (an answer stopping inside an unfinished JSON object, with no finish reason saying the model stopped itself) is `CutOff`, kept in the cache as cut off (the marker records the answer limit the service was sent: `MAX_ANSWER_TOKENS` through LiteLLM, none through Ask Sage) and never paid twice, with advice fit for Ask Sage (no limit to raise: lower `max_chars`, another model, or LiteLLM); a refusal, by a filter's reason or in prose, is `Refused`: never asked once more, never stored, counted as *refused* in the failed-call record and in *Records left out* (`dropped.kind`); a complete object under an unknown finish reason is complete; an object the model left unfinished under `stop` is malformed (asked once more). A reply of `{"triples": []}` can't be told from a record with no facts and still counts as one. `check_effort`'s step A reads only a 400 or 422 whose error text names the setting as "the setting is read"; a 401, 403, 404 or 429 gives no verdict and says what it is about. Tests enumerate every variant for both services, near-misses included (refusal words before a JSON object are not a refusal; a fence inside a value is kept; "I can help" is not "I can't"). Home: `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, section 5.
- **One interval, and a compared set that stays a fair sample (done on 2026-11-01, edge-case audit, group G7).** `py helpers/compare_runs.py` dropped the records whose ground truth changed and compared the rest, which were then no longer the first records of the pool (the bootstrap ran on a non-random subset); a change of `annotations/pending_triples.csv` or of the translation table between runs showed only as a recipe fingerprint, the affected records staying in; the same run given twice was compared with itself; the middle 95% was computed twice (`stats.py` and an inline copy), the low index rounded down and the high index rounded to nearest (positions 24 and 974 of 1,000: 24 values below, 25 above), and redraws skipped as undefined weren't reported; a held-out look was logged with 0 held-out records evaluated, two runs at once could log the same count, and the kept evaluation's fallback run id had one-second resolution with no refusal to overwrite. Now `stats.interval` is the one rule (positions 0.025·(n − 1) rounded down and 0.975·(n − 1) rounded up: 24 below, 24 above; `used` and `skipped` redraws), used by 070's margins, its per-component-class tables and `compare_runs`; the report's table of the margin's assumptions gains the row *Every redraw gives a value*, and a range from fewer redraws says from how many. Each kept evaluation records, per record, its ground truth, its facts waiting on an expert and the translation of its classed triples' shapes (`_translation_of`); `compare_runs` leaves out a record differing in any of them (comparing the translation on the shapes the record has in every run, so an extraction difference keeps the record), cuts the compared set before the first record left out (the rest named *after a record left out, so no longer the first records of the pool*), says what a run before 2026-11-01 can't be checked for, and refuses the same run twice. A look is logged only when a held-out record was evaluated, under `common/common_helpers/files.py`'s `locked` (a lock file beside the file, shared with the saves of G8), and the kept evaluation is written with `new=True`. Homes: `070_evaluate/metrics/approximately.md`, `helpers/compare_runs.md`, 070's guide.
- **One check of every setting, and one definition of "has text" (done on 2026-11-01, edge-case audit, groups G9 and G10).** Settings were checked in several places with minimums only: `check_settings` knew no upper bound or kind (`True` passed as 1; `--page_size 2000` on a server capped at 1000 would have skipped 1,000 records per page, silently, with a rerun advised that repeated it), and `part`, `extract_from`, `service`, `model`, `thinking` and `repeat` were each refused by code of their own, each worded differently, after the inputs were read. Now `common/common_helpers/step.py`'s `Spec` (kind, lowest, highest, values, empty allowed) and `check_settings` are the one rule; each step's table is `SPECS` in its helpers (`010_harvest_helpers/moves.py`, `020_clean_helpers/moves.py`, `030_split_helpers/moves.py`, `040_induce_schema_helpers/texts.py`, 050's, 060's and 070's `records.py`), the shared settings' rules are `shared_specs()`, `run_step` checks them before any input is read, the stage that uses them checks the same table, `llm.paid_calls` too, and every message has one shape (*The setting <name> must be <what it allows>, but is <value> …*); a value from a list is read without case or surrounding spaces. 010 also stops when a page comes back shorter than asked for while the catalog has more (the server's cap is below `page_size`), naming the cap to give, instead of saving the page and skipping records. "Has text" was decided two ways: `has_text` read title and notes only with `str.strip()` (a zero-width space or a byte-order mark counted as text), while 020's count and the text a model reads (`full_text`) took the extra text fields too, so a record whose only text was in an extra field counted as with text in 020 and was skipped as without text by 050 and 060. Now `common/common_helpers/records_io.py` holds `text_fields`, `full_text` and `has_text` (the same fields; blank once evened out by `text_match.even`), `chunking.py` imports them, 020's count uses `has_text`, and 070's advice for the first record missing from the fair sample says when that record has no text and so can never be finished or extracted. Tests run every step's settings at each bound, just outside it, as true/false, as a fraction and with a near miss of each listed value; a short page with and without more records in the catalog; and the text variants (an extra field only, invisible characters, a letter beside one). Homes: `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, section 4; each step's guide.
- **One rule for several rows about one thing, and it reports (done on 2026-11-01, edge-case audit, group G12).** A partial pair with two different verdicts in `annotations/partial_pair_reviews.csv` took the last row's, silently; the reviewers' comments dropped empty comments before choosing the latest, so a comment couldn't be withdrawn, and chose the latest by comparing timestamp texts, so a spelling could sort above a later moment. Now `common/common_helpers/latest.py`'s `latest_by_key` is the rule both readers use: with timestamps (read as moments) the latest wins, a withdrawal (an empty value) too; without them, or at one moment, the rows must agree, else the conflict is reported, naming both rows, and nothing is chosen. 070's warning on the verdicts file and `py helpers/review_page.py --read` list the conflicts and the timestamps that can't be read. The JSON schema reader's last-wins was already replaced by the first-kept rule, reported, on 2026-10-31 (G3). Homes: 070's guide, `helpers/review_page.md`.
- **Read, then write only if unchanged (done on 2026-11-01, edge-case audit, group G8).** The annotation tool rewrote a whole file from the page with no check that the file was still what the page had loaded, so two tabs on one batch, a hand or Excel edit while the page was open, or a rename in the Ground truth schema view followed by a save from a tab loaded before it, silently lost the other change; the translation table's save checked only the rows' kinds and names, so a row 070 appended in between was lost; a step hashed its inputs before `main()` read them, so a manifest could describe files the run never read (the tool saving while 070 waited at its question); the status counted a person's file given in place of a step's input (`--schema annotations/ground_truth_schema.txt`) under the input's name, so an edit never made 060 out of date; the hash of an input of several files was over their bytes alone, so a renamed batch file hashed the same; two processes on one cache file overwrote each other's paid answers. Now `common/common_helpers/files.py` holds the one mechanism: `locked` (a lock file beside the file, one process and thread at a time), `fingerprint`, `read_snapshot` and `write_if_unchanged` (write only if the file still has the fingerprint it was read with, else `Changed`: nothing written, reload). Every view of the tool sends the fingerprint it loaded with every save, and the server writes through `write_if_unchanged` (the ground truth file, the translation table, the additions file); a verdict replaces its pair's row under the lock; `run_step` hashes the inputs again after `main()` and stops the run, with no manifest, when one changed; each manifest input says whether it was `given` on the command line, and the status counts such a file; `audit.sha256` of several files hashes each file's name and length with its bytes (so every step with a many-file input shows as out of date once after this change: run it again); the cache merges what another process wrote before writing. Tests: a save after an on-disk edit, two tabs, a rename then a stale save, the same bytes with CRLF, an edit during a run, a file given in place of a default, a renamed batch, two caches on one file. Home: `annotations/README.md`, *How a save is checked*; `helpers/audit.md`; `docs/upkeep.md`.
- **One reader of every file, and one set-up of the console (done on 2026-11-01, edge-case audit, group G11).** `py run_pipeline.py --status` under a cp1252 console or a redirected output died with `UnicodeEncodeError` on ✖ (only a step's log set the console up); the pipeline report, the copy of a manifest in `previous_runs/` and the pipeline log were written with Windows line endings; prompt files and `records.jsonl` were read as UTF-8 without dropping a byte-order mark (Notepad's reached the model and changed the prompt's fingerprint; one in `records.jsonl` broke its first line with a raw `JSONDecodeError`); a bad line of `records.jsonl` gave a traceback, not a message; a drafted batch saved in cp1252 by Excel crashed 050; `run_pipeline.py` read a manifest unguarded; `report.cell` kept `\\r`. Now `common/common_helpers/files.py` holds the readers (`read_text`, `read_json`, `read_jsonl`, `read_csv`, `read_csv_table`, `append_text`: UTF-8 with the byte-order mark dropped, Windows line endings read as plain, a `Stop` naming the file and line on a file that isn't UTF-8 or isn't in its format), every step, helper and the annotation tool read and write through it (a guard test allows no `open`, `read_text` or `write_text` outside it, binary reads aside), and `common/common_helpers/console.py`'s `setup()` is called first by every program a person runs. Not changed: the status's reading of `git status --porcelain` still shows a renamed or non-ASCII path garbled (cosmetic). Home: `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, section 4, *Files and the console*.
- **A used-up budget stops the run at once (done on 2026-11-02).** NASA's LiteLLM answered HTTP 429 with "Budget has been exceeded! User=iiordane in Team=trial-moderate Current cost: 25.0, Max budget: 25.0", and `common/common_helpers/llm.py` treated it like a passing rate limit: each call was tried 4 times, in every worker, so a run kept sending calls. Now a used-up budget or quota is never retried, whatever its HTTP status: LiteLLM's budget answers (its `BudgetExceededError`, "Budget has been exceeded! … Current cost: …, Max budget: …"; `budget_exceeded`, "ExceededBudget", "exceeded budget for", "over budget": [LiteLLM, budgets](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/users)) and the providers' (OpenAI's `insufficient_quota`, "exceeded your current quota"; Anthropic's "credit balance is too low"), decided in one place beside the retried statuses (`budget_exhausted`). The first such answer stops the whole run: no worker sends another call (`call_llm` and `litellm_post` refuse every later request of the process, so every step and helper is covered: 040 to 070, `helpers/second_opinion.py`, `helpers/second_judge.py`, `helpers/models.py --try`, `helpers/check_effort.py`, `helpers/check_answer_cache.py`), the calls already on their way are waited for and their answers kept in the cache, and one message names the service, its words and numbers, and what to do (`docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *The budget is used up*). An ordinary rate limit still retries. Tests: `tests/test_model_calls.py`, `Budget` (a plain 429, a 429 whose text speaks of a budget in another sense, Anthropic's `budget_tokens`, a 400 budget answer, Ask Sage's error forms). From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-11-02.
- **Ask Sage's used-up monthly token limit stops the run at once (done on 2026-11-03).** Ask Sage says the limit is reached in an ordinary answer, HTTP 200, in prose ("Sorry, you are above your monthly token limit (1011372 out of 1000000), please contact your organization admin to get it increased."), and `common/common_helpers/llm.py` took it for a refusal: a second-judge run sent all its calls, and 67 failed one after another. Now the one recognizer of a used-up budget (`budget_exhausted`) also reads such an answer (`budget_answer`: short, not JSON, with those words), before an answer is judged a refusal and before any retry, so the first one stops the run as LiteLLM's budget answer does. Its message gives the tokens used and the monthly limit, and nothing of it is kept in the cache. Docs: `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md` (*The budget is used up*, and its row of the stops table), `docs/findings.md`. Tests: `tests/test_model_calls.py`, Budget (both answers word for word, near-misses).
- **Applying the second opinion calls no model, and every free command is free by code (done on 2026-11-02).** `py helpers/second_opinion.py --apply true` was documented as free, but it drafted every record again and counted on the answer cache; after ground truth triples were added, the drafting prompt's ground truth schema view gained two patterns, every cache key changed, and the apply paid for 60 model calls ([`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-11-02). Now the helper's two jobs are apart: drafting (paid; it asks before paying) saves each record's draft per repeat in `saved_drafts/<model>.json` of its output folder, and applying only matches those saved drafts against the ground truth as it is now, building no prompt and reading no cached answer; with no saved drafts for the model and repeats asked, it stops, naming the drafting command (`helpers/second_opinion.md`, *Drafting pays, applying is free*). Every command documented as free first forbids model calls for its process (`common/common_helpers/llm_errors.py`, `forbid_model_calls`: `call_llm` and `litellm_post` then stop the run before a request leaves): applying, `helpers/partial_pairs.py`, `helpers/compare_runs.py`, `helpers/review_page.py`, and `helpers/models.py` without `--try`. The question before paying now gives the count to pay (`PaidCalls.start`, `to_pay`: 050, 060, the second opinion, the [second judge](terminology.md#4-ground-truth-and-samples)), and the docs say a rerun is free only when it doesn't stop to ask (`docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *When a rerun is free*). Tests: `tests/test_model_calls.py`, `FreeCommands`; `tests/test_second_opinion.py`, `WholeRun` (apply after a ground truth and schema change: 0 calls, the cache and the saved drafts untouched, the new triple found; drafting the same asks first with the count; another model or more repeats than saved: stops, naming the drafting command; fewer repeats: applied, 0 calls). From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-11-02.
- **The question before paying in 040's one-prompt-at-a-time stages gives no count (from 2026-11-02).** 040's labeling, merging and defining ask through `common/common_helpers/cache.py`, `ask_cached`, which starts paying at the first prompt the cache can't answer, so their question says what they ask ("label component instances, 80 per call") but not how many calls are to pay. Count the batches each stage still has to ask before its first call, and pass the count to `PaidCalls.start` (`to_pay`), as 050, 060 and the helpers do. 070's translation question already names its number of calls in its plan. From: the check of every paid-calls question, 2026-11-02.
- **Future experiments on the prompts (decided on 2026-10-30, from the prompt audit):**
  - *Native structured output (F9)*: ask the service for its JSON-schema output mode instead of parsing the reply. Only if the counts of *Replies asked again* show unusable replies are frequent; format restrictions can reduce reasoning quality (Tam et al., "Let Me Speak Freely? A Study on the Impact of Format Restrictions on Performance of Large Language Models", EMNLP 2024 Industry Track, arXiv:2408.02442).
  - *Quote first (F11)*: each triple's `source_text` before its subject and object instances in the reply, so the model copies the passage before writing the instances. Its effect is unknown; it needs a paid comparison with repeats.
  - *A vote over the baseline's repeats (F18)*: keep a triple when a majority of the 3 repeat runs extracted it, after self-consistency (Wang et al., "Self-Consistency Improves Chain of Thought Reasoning in Language Models", ICLR 2023, arXiv:2203.11171). Planned with the baseline: computed afterwards from the repeats' outputs, at no extra model cost. The code can come later, beside the comparison of runs (`helpers/compare_runs.py`) or in step 070.
- **Cost per run.** Each report already gives the tokens sent and received (section *Model calls*); a price per token would turn them into dollars. From: the literature review of 2026-10-07. Extended on 2026-11-01: **also time and scale.** Why: the catalog has about 36,000 records and the pipeline has extracted from 20 at most; no tokens, seconds or dollars per record are in Git (*Prompt size and cost*, under 060; *Measure the step times on the NASA laptop*, below), while comparable papers report a cost per document (SynthKG, "Scaling Knowledge Graph Construction through Synthetic Data Generation and Distillation", arXiv:2410.16597: about $0.031 and about 14,500 tokens per document), and ESWC's In-Use track lists "scalability studies for real deployments" and "Cost-benefit assessments of implementing and managing semantic technologies" among its topics ([call](https://2026.eswc-conferences.org/calls/papers-in-use-track)). Smallest version: (1) from the laptop's 060 reports, the input, cached-input and output tokens and the seconds per record, and the schema's share of the prompt, times the service's list prices, and times the catalog's record count for the whole catalog, into `findings.md` (no model; about 2 hours); (2) one 060 run on a stratified random 1% of the catalog (about 360 records; drawing it fairly is part of the work, since 030 splits the pool, not the catalog), with no ground truth, for the rates a full run would meet: records refused by the content filter (*The content filter*, under 060), answers cut at the length limit, triples removed by the source-text check, triples per record by stratum, and the time. Effort: about 1 day. Model cost: about 360 record extractions, the largest planned spend (a few dollars by the review's estimate, not measured). Needs the models before 2026-11-20: (2) yes; (1) no, but its reports are on the NASA laptop. From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap C.
- **Cost per run from the service's own spend (from 2026-11-02).** Why: my LiteLLM budget of $25 was used up on 2026-11-02 ([`findings.md`](findings.md)), and no run records what it cost, so I can't say which runs spent it, nor plan the runs left before 2026-11-20 against what remains. LiteLLM keeps a spend per key, user and team: its docs show `/user/info` returning the user's `spend` and each key's `spend` and `max_budget` ([LiteLLM, cost tracking](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/cost_tracking)), and `/key/info`, `/user/info` and `/team/info` returning the use of budgets set per model ([LiteLLM, budgets](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/users)); whether a user's own key may call them isn't documented (question 9 for the administrators, above). Smallest version, once that is known: each run reads the spend once before its first paid call and once after its last (free requests: not model calls), and its record and report give the difference as the run's cost, and the budget left. Until then, *Cost per run* (above) estimates from tokens. Not yet called: checked in the docs only. From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-11-02.
- **Thinking the service doesn't report, bounded** (from the research of 2026-10-31, F-3): Anthropic counts thinking inside the billed output tokens, and LiteLLM reports them as completion tokens. So the thinking of one answer is at most its completion tokens minus the tokens of its visible text. Each report could give that bound where it now says the thinking is unknown, and the same bound can be computed from the log of run `040_induce_schema_2026-10-12_1629` for its two stopped answers. From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-12, *Step 040 · What the content filter stopped on*.
- **Set Claude's effort explicitly, once a check shows LiteLLM passes it on (decided on 2026-10-30, prompt audit F1).** On Claude Opus 5.5 adaptive thinking is always on: the model decides whether and how much to think, and effort is the main control of how much (Anthropic, [Effort](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/effort): "Adaptive thinking is always on and can't be turned off, so effort is the primary control for how much the model reasons"). The pipeline sends it no effort, so it runs at its default, `medium` ([What's new in Claude Opus 5.5](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/opus-5-5/whats-new-opus-5-5)); each run's record says `effort: not sent (model default, medium)`. On the NASA laptop, run `py helpers/check_effort.py` (step A, two requests with an invalid effort value, expected to cost nothing; step B, nine paid calls; each step asks before sending; [`running_on_nasa_laptop.md`](running_on_nasa_laptop.md), *Checking that LiteLLM passes Claude's effort on*) and put what it prints in [`findings.md`](findings.md). If either step shows that the setting is passed on (step A: an invalid value is refused; step B: the decision rule the check prints before its calls), add an effort setting sent to Claude models (as `thinking` is sent to Gemini), part of the cache key and of each run's record, and choose its level by comparing runs: Anthropic advises re-running an effort sweep rather than carrying a setting over. If LiteLLM doesn't pass it on, the runs stay at `medium`, and the reports say so. Checked on 2026-10-31: not shown either way, since the question was too easy to need thinking at any effort (`findings.md`); to settle it, `check_effort.py` should ask a question that needs several steps of reasoning. Done on 2026-10-31 (research F-6 of that day): Anthropic says that with adaptive thinking "at lower effort settings it might skip thinking entirely on easy inputs" ([Extended thinking](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/extended-thinking)), so the easy question could not tell. The check now has two steps: step A sends `reasoning_effort` and `output_config` effort "not_a_level" (refused: the setting is read; answered: dropped or ignored); step B asks a counting puzzle with a checked answer (168) 3 times each with no effort, at `low` and at `high`, and decides by a rule fixed before the calls: passed on if every high call uses more completion tokens than every low call, or the mean of high is at least 1.5 times the mean of low. Completion tokens are the measure, since Anthropic counts thinking inside the billed output tokens. Run on 2026-11-01 ([`findings.md`](findings.md)): before the answer cache's bypass its counts were cache copies, and its verdict void; after it, "not shown" (means 758 with no effort, 819 at `low`, 873 at `high`, overlapping), and step A shows LiteLLM reads and validates the effort value. So the runs stay at `medium`. To do, before choosing an effort level: a check with an effect large enough to show (a harder question, or more requests per setting). Also (prompt audit F4): a thinking-token count the service doesn't report is unknown, not zero; `findings.md` says so since 2026-10-30.
- **A batch API at NASA's LiteLLM**, if it has one: many calls sent together, at a discount. Ask NASA's LiteLLM administrators (question 6 of *Questions for NASA's LiteLLM administrators*, below). Checked on 2026-10-31 (research F-7): LiteLLM's batch endpoint lists provider-native batch APIs for OpenAI, Azure OpenAI, Vertex AI, Amazon Bedrock, Mistral, vLLM and xAI, and not Anthropic's own ([LiteLLM batches](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/batches)), so it depends on which provider serves `claude-opus-5.5` there. From: 2026-10-13.
- **Questions for NASA's LiteLLM administrators** (from the research of 2026-10-31, F-1 to F-7): what the service does to a request depends on its LiteLLM version and its configuration, which are not public. One message, nine questions:
  1. Which LiteLLM version runs the proxy?
  2. For `claude-opus-5-5` and `gemini-2.5-pro`: which provider and route serves each (Anthropic's API, Amazon Bedrock or its us-gov region, Vertex AI, Google AI Studio)? How many deployments share each model name, and with which routing strategy? And the Claude model's two names: the proxy lists `claude-opus-5-5` and also accepts `claude-opus-5.5` without listing it (both answered on 2026-11-01; the pipeline's default is the listed spelling since that day, after `claude-opus-5.5` until then) — do both names reach the same deployment? ([`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-11-01).
  3. Is `drop_params` set? If so, is an unsupported `reasoning_effort` or `output_config` dropped without an error?
  4. Is session affinity on (`optional_pre_call_checks: ["session_affinity"]`), so that the requests of one run, sent with one `x-litellm-session-id`, reach one deployment and its prompt cache? Step 060 sends that header since 2026-10-31.
  5. Are guardrails or content filters of the proxy's own configured? Does the proxy pass Anthropic's `stop_details` (the refusal category) or the raw stop reason on to clients?
  6. Is the batch endpoint (`/v1/files` and `/v1/batches`) enabled for either model, and at what price?
  7. Does the proxy report `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` for Claude, from Anthropic's `output_tokens_details.thinking_tokens`?
  8. For Gemini: does a `cache_control` mark create an explicit Google cache, which is billed for storage, and does the usage the proxy returns show implicit caching?
  9. (added 2026-11-02) May my own key call `/key/info` or `/user/info` to read its spend and budget, so each run can record its cost? And can my budget of $25 (user `iiordane`, team `trial-moderate`, used up on 2026-11-02) be raised or reset, and to how much?
  From: the research of 2026-10-31 ([`findings.md`](findings.md), the entries of 2026-10-31 on LiteLLM).
- **Keep the saved answers safe:** every model answer behind a reported number is in the steps' `cache/` folders under `outputs/`, which Git doesn't carry, so they exist only on the computer that ran the step (`previous_runs/` keeps each step's earlier output files, not its `cache/`). Archive them with each run that numbers are reported from. From: the methods review of 2026-10-11, item 7. Extended on 2026-11-01: **before 2026-11-20, and as a release.** Why: the model answers behind every number exist only on the NASA laptop, and nothing has a license, a DOI or a release; ESWC's Resources track asks whether a resource has a persistent identifier and a license ([call](https://2026.eswc-conferences.org/calls/papers-resource-track)), and Text2KGBench's release includes the raw model answers (Zenodo record 7916716).
