Smallest version: (1) before 2026-11-20, copy every `cache/` folder, run report and log of the reported runs off the laptop, without the `.env` key; (2) after: one Zenodo deposit (the ground truth, the ground truth schema, the translation table, `annotations/guidelines.md`, the reported runs' extracted triples and 070 outputs, and the archived answers with their request fingerprints), under a license NASA's release process (NPR 2200.2E) allows, with *How to reproduce* in the README (below) and a version tag; the release paperwork takes weeks, so it starts now. Effort: 1 day, plus the paperwork. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: no, but the copy is time-bound. From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap H.
- **Extract and draft, before model access ends, every record that could ever be evaluated (from 2026-11-01).** Why: after 2026-11-20 I have no model access, while step 070, the annotation tool and `helpers/compare_runs.py` need none; so a record that step 060 hasn't extracted (and step 050 hasn't drafted) by then can never enter an evaluation, tuning or held-out, and several items here (a held-out number, the graph's questions, the kinds of mistakes) depend on such records. Smallest version: in one session, 050 drafts and 060 extractions with the baseline recipe for pool positions 0 to about 99, tuning and held-out alike (`py 060_extract/run.py --ids <the file of their ids>`; `annotations/held_out_looks.csv` still guards against looking at held-out numbers), the baseline's repeats on the finished records, and the runs of the items here that need the model; then the copy of *Keep the saved answers safe*, above. A later 060 run on those records reuses the saved answers at no cost only while the prompts and the schema stay the same. Effort: half a day at the keyboard; the runs take hours. Model cost: about 100 records × (1 draft + 1 extraction), plus the repeats. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: yes, first of all. From: the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap A.
- **Record the first real evaluation in [`findings.md`](findings.md):** 070 run 2026-10-22_1615 evaluated a 060 run made with the real model; their numbers and tokens are in their reports on the NASA laptop, and `findings.md` has no entry for them yet. From: 2026-10-22.
- **Measure the step times on the NASA laptop** from the run reports: the table of steps in [`running_on_nasa_laptop.md`](running_on_nasa_laptop.md), section 4, says "not yet measured" for 040, 050, 060, and 070. From: 2026-10-24.
- **`tests/run_all.py` on the NASA laptop:** its first run there reported that the tests changed files, and the next run passed. Find why. From: 2026-10-12.
- **A test file that once failed in `tests/run_all.py` and passed alone:** on 2026-10-26, `test_status.py` was stopped at the 300-second limit in one full run (it takes about 20 seconds; alone, right after, it passed in 22), and `test_evaluate_pairing.py` failed in another full run and passed alone (12 seconds). The second run overlapped with edits to 070's code, which may explain it; the first had no such edit, and its output didn't say which test was running. Now `run_all.py` names each test as it starts and, at the time limit, prints what the file had written, and `test_status.py` gives each status read 120 seconds; if it happens again, the output names the test. From: 2026-10-26.
- **README, *How to reproduce*:** setup and running are in [`running_on_nasa_laptop.md`](running_on_nasa_laptop.md); building the graph waits for step 080. From: my old to-do list.
- **Ask data.nasa.gov about its harvest jobs** (from the research of 2026-10-31, F-11): the catalog runs CKAN's harvest extensions, and its count swings by thousands of records within days. Ask its administrators the harvest schedule and sources, and whether a record that vanishes is deleted or made private (both answer an anonymous caller with 403). From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-31, *data.nasa.gov · Records that vanish, and the count today*.
- **A deployment path, stated by the people who would deploy it (from 2026-11-01).** Why: ESWC's In-Use track scores "Adoption by domain practitioners" and "Impact of the solution / use case" ([call](https://2026.eswc-conferences.org/calls/papers-in-use-track)), and no one who runs data.nasa.gov or the Science Discovery Engine has said they want the knowledge graph, nor in what shape (search filters by mission and instrument, which the Science Discovery Engine already offers; an extension of CKAN; an export). Smallest version: one conversation, through my mentors, with the data.nasa.gov or Science Discovery Engine team, written up as half a page: which predicates match which of their filters, what they would need (precision above some level, the source text of each edge, a refresh at each harvest), and an acknowledgment; asked together with *Ask data.nasa.gov about its harvest jobs*, above. Effort: hours, plus waiting. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: no. From: the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap K.
- **Other catalogs:** some things fit only data.nasa.gov. The catalog's address is fixed in the code (`010_harvest/010_harvest_helpers/ckan_client.py`); "undefined" is treated as a maintainer (`020_clean/020_clean_helpers/maintainers.py`); splitting into strata needs a maintainer field that other catalogs may not fill; step 040's prompts say "a catalog of NASA datasets". From: the methods review of 2026-10-11, item 11. Extended on 2026-11-01: **first a design review of catalog independence, then a transfer test.** Why: what this project can claim about other catalogs is today nothing beyond data.nasa.gov, and a reviewer of a journal will ask. (1) The design review: list every data.nasa.gov and NASA assumption in the code, the prompts, the rules and the docs (those above, and the others the review is to find; candidates: rule 1's *Names on an official NASA list*, 080's NASA reference lists), propose a configuration point for each that can have one, and assess whether one pipeline for many catalogs is achievable at all: the ground truth, the ground truth schema, the translation table and the rules were written for this catalog's records, so another catalog needs at least a ground truth of its own to be evaluated; and write down what this project can and cannot claim about generality. (2) Then a transfer test on catalog.data.gov, which runs CKAN with the same `package_search` API ([Data.gov CKAN API](https://catalog-old.data.gov/dataset/data-gov-ckan-api)): 010 with the catalog's address a setting, one harvest page, 060 with the induced schema on 20 records of another agency, reporting how many classed triples it gives and how many the checks remove, with no ground truth (so no precision or recall). Effort: about 1 day for the review; 1 to 2 days for the test. Model cost: 20 record extractions. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: the review no; the test yes, if done at all (for a journal only). From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap L; my request of 2026-11-01, for the design review.
- **Competency questions:** 10 to 20 questions the knowledge graph should answer, to judge it by. From: the literature review of 2026-10-07; the method is Grüninger & Fox (1995), "Methodology for the Design and Evaluation of Ontologies", IJCAI-95 Workshop on Basic Ontological Issues in Knowledge Sharing. Extended on 2026-11-01: **answered from a small graph, and compared with the catalog's own search.** Why: the project's motivation, finding datasets by a connection no single record states (e.g. the datasets acquired by instruments aboard the Aqua spacecraft, when the two facts are in two records), is never tested; dataset search today "relies mainly on keyword matching over published metadata" (Chapman, Simperl, Koesten et al., "Dataset search: a survey", *The VLDB Journal* 29, 2020, [doi:10.1007/s00778-019-00564-x](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00778-019-00564-x)), and ESWC's In-Use track asks a submission to "demonstrate the fitness for purpose" of its approach ([call](https://2026.eswc-conferences.org/calls/papers-in-use-track)). Smallest version: a small graph of the extracted triples of the 1% run (*Cost per run*, above) and of the finished records, one node per record and one per subject instance or object instance, joined when they are the same evened out; 10 questions, each needing a step across two records; for each, the answers from the graph and those from data.nasa.gov's search with the question's words, every answer judged right or wrong by me; per question, how many right answers each route finds, and how many questions each route can answer at all. Effort: 3 to 4 days. Model cost: none beyond the 1% run. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: only for that run. From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap D. Extended on 2026-11-02: **a third route, semantic search** (records retrieved by the meaning of their text, not its exact words), since it also handles different words for one thing, the drawback a reviewer would raise first; the questions should include ones that need a step across two records, a role, a count or a value (e.g. a resolution finer than 1 km), the drawbacks of keyword search that semantic search doesn't remove. The NASA TM's limitations say that retrieval is not yet shown.
- **An entity class hierarchy for the schemas:** the ground truth schema is flat (no entity class is a kind of another). An umbrella entity class with subclasses is standard in knowledge graph schemas (subClassOf in W3C, "RDF Schema 1.1", Recommendation, 25 February 2014, [page](https://www.w3.org/TR/rdf-schema/)); for people and groups it has a standard name, Agent: FOAF puts Person, Organization and Group under it (Brickley and Miller, "FOAF Vocabulary Specification 0.99", 14 January 2014, [page](http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/)), and PROV-O puts Person, Organization and SoftwareAgent under it (W3C, "PROV-O: The PROV Ontology", Recommendation, 30 April 2013, [page](https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-o/)). For what carries instruments it has one too, a platform: W3C's sensor ontology says "A Platform is an entity [a thing] that hosts other entities [things], particularly Sensors, Actuators, Samplers, and other Platforms" (W3C, "Semantic Sensor Network Ontology", [page](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-ssn/)), and NASA's GCMD Platforms keywords group space-, air-, water- and land-based platforms (checked on 2026-10-31; Q4 and Q25 of annotations/questions_for_experts.md no longer ask it). With it, a pin could say any -> Agent instead of listing Organization and Person (DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY, SPONSORED_BY_PARTY), and the graph could answer questions across the kinds. It touches the schema reader and its file format, the pin check, step 040's induced schema, and step 070's translation table, so it is a design change for after the first run. Some entity classes only ever appear as the object of one predicate, and their definitions just restate it: Purpose (HAS_PURPOSE), Task (RESPONSIBLE_FOR), Role (SERVES_AS), ProcessingLevel (HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL), SpatialRepresentation (HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION). They cause no disagreement today, unlike Value and Time, which were merged for that reason on 2026-10-20; with a hierarchy they could sit under one umbrella entity class (e.g. Label). Evidence that an induced schema is often finer than one written by people, so that a hierarchy would let the two meet: in TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3), 48.1% of the entity classes of their benchmark's hand-built schemas (weighted by use, all scopes together) were matched only by a narrower entity class of the induced schema, against 50.2% matched by an equivalent one (Table 2, *Combined*), and of its accepted matches for entity classes, 13.8% were best at the coarsest level of its hierarchy (appendix C.2, Table 6). From: 2026-10-20; TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3), for the evidence.
- **A number cut from its unit in a lookup:** a letter next to a digit counts as a break between words (so "67P" is found in "Comet67P"), so "1 km" is also found in a text that says "1 km2" (an area), and the check of a subject instance or object instance against the record's text (`common/common_helpers/text_match.py`, `Text.contains`) raises no flag for it. Pairing is not affected: "1 km" and "1 km2" never pair. From: 2026-10-24.
- **Ideas from TRACE-KG not to adopt, and why** (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3):
  - *Prefer recall over precision*, inferred facts included: its extraction prompts say "Prefer recall over precision" (`TKG_Prompts.py`, lines 28 and 883) and keep a fact rated "Plausible inference; partial support (still include)" (line 77). That conflicts with guidelines 1 and 7 of *What counts as a fact*, "Only what the record states" and "Not uncertain facts".
  - Subject instances and object instances in the model's words: its prompt has the model name each thing in a short, reusable form, which may not occur in the text as written (line 865). That conflicts with rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple* (the record's own words, as written) and with the source text check (`common/common_helpers/validate.py`).
  - A qualifier dictionary on each edge (section 4.4, appendix B.5): there, a "qualifier" is a condition a triple holds under (when, where, how certain), kept with the edge, not a word inside a subject or object instance as in this project (see *qualifier* in `terminology.md`). Its "UncertaintyQualifier" keeps hedged facts, against guideline 7, "Not uncertain facts". Their ablation found qualifiers mattered most on their benchmark, judged by a model (appendix C.4).
  - Never more than one edge between the same subject and object instances: its prompt says to merge them all (line 1211). That contradicts its own section 4.5, which merges only two edges between the same things that mean the same, and would drop distinct facts. Suppose a record says NASA developed and sponsored a mission; then DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY and SPONSORED_BY_PARTY (ground truth schema) join the same two instances, and one would be lost.
  - Its "Leakage" metric, the overlap of 4-word runs between the text and the names of things (section 5.1, appendix C.1), counts copying the record's words as a fault: the opposite of rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple*.
  - A model judge, as the evaluation, of whether a fact "can be supported or inferred" from the graph (`TKG_Experiment_1.py`, line 1359; its section 5.1 says the judge allows "no inferred edges"): inferred facts would count as found, and the judge's own errors go unmeasured. Step 070 compares with a ground truth a person checked.
  - A model's own confidence as a gate (appendices C.2 and D: below 0.88 goes to a person): the paper gives no evidence that it is calibrated. A person checks every row of the translation table instead.

## 020_clean

- **DCAT 3.0** (the W3C's vocabulary for data catalogs): enforce it. What that would change isn't worked out yet. From: my old to-do list.

## 040_induce_schema

- **Splitting a broad predicate by its object's entity class (done on 2026-11-14: stage 5, splitting, `040_induce_schema/040_induce_schema_helpers/split.py`; 040's guide, *How it works*, stage 5, and *Settings*, `max_new_predicates`, default 50, chosen from the schema of 2026-10-12: 75 of its 104 predicates reach two or more object entity classes, and each added predicate takes about 175 characters, 0.13% of the median step 060 prompt; whether it helps is measured by running 040, 060 and 070 again).** As proposed: a stage after stage 4, checked by code. For each predicate whose patterns reach several object entity classes, the model may propose splits, each a new predicate with the object entity classes it takes; code checks that each object entity class named is among that predicate's patterns and goes to only one split, reassigns the triple instances by their pattern, and lists every predicate split it rejects, with why. Suppose SOURCED_FROM (current schema) has patterns with a Mission object and with a Spacecraft object; then it would split into one predicate for each, which can translate to FROM_MISSION and FROM_PLATFORM (ground truth schema). This fixes the cause behind *Gaps in the translation table* (1), under 070, and makes *Translation that depends on the entity classes* a fallback for the predicates not split. Cost: a few paid calls in 040, then running 060 and 070 again. Risk: more predicates, so a longer schema text in every 060 prompt (see *Patterns in or out of the prompt*). From: TRACE-KG (Abolhasani, Ba, He & Pan, "Traceable Context-Enriched Knowledge Graph Generation with Induced Schemas", arXiv:2604.03496v3, 18 Oct 2026, [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03496v3)): its model may split an entity class into several (`split_class`, appendix B.4.2), and its code keeps only the split entity class's own members, each in one split only (`execute_split_class`, `TKG_Main.py`, line 4796); it sets each triple instance's predicate with its subject's and object's entity classes in view (section 4.5); and its code carries out only actions it has checked (section 4.6).
- **Splitting by subject, object or both, with a minimum support, merging again and pins (decided and done on 2026-11-15; extends the item above).** Stage 5 of step 040 (`040_induce_schema/040_induce_schema_helpers/split.py`; 040's guide, *How it works*, stages 5, 7 and 8, and *Settings*) changed in five ways. (1) A predicate is split by the entity class of its subject, of its object, or of both (its pattern), one grouping per predicate in a run, and it goes to the model when its triple instances have more than one subject entity class or more than one object entity class. Why: a predicate can stand for relationships that differ by its subject, or by its subject and object together, which grouping by the object alone cannot separate. One grouping per predicate keeps each triple instance in one new predicate, and the pattern grouping can express any mix of subject groups and object groups. (2) A new predicate needs triple instances in at least `min_split_texts` texts (default 2). Why: when the evidence cannot tell a wrong split from a rare true one, not splitting loses no fact, since the original predicate still expresses it, whereas a wrong split makes extraction choose between two predicates on one text's evidence. (3) After the cap, every predicate is merged again with stage 4's predicate merge prompt and code, and a split whose parts all end in one predicate is undone. Why: a new predicate can mean the same as an existing predicate, or two new predicates of one split can mean the same, and only a merge over all predicates sees it. The cap comes first because a merge can only lower the number of predicates added. A merge of two predicates that were both there at stage 4 is set aside, since stage 4 already judged that pair. (4) Code pins each new predicate to the groups its split took, and the induced schema writes the pins (`pinned`), so step 060's check `pattern_not_pinned` flags an extracted triple that breaks one; the flagged triples are kept and counted. A predicate merged from several keeps the union of their pins only when each of them is pinned. Why: the groups a split took are what its new predicate means, and a predicate with no pin may join any entity classes, so a union with one has no bound. (5) Defining (stage 7) asks each pinned predicate's definition to name the entity classes of its pin, with the check the ground truth schema's pins pass (`common/common_helpers/schema_io.py`, `unnamed_pinned`), and asks once more when one is missing. Why: no prompt shows a pin, so the definition is the only place a model reading the schema learns it. (6) The predicate merge call (stage 4, and merging again) shows each predicate label with up to 3 example triple instances, each instance with its entity class, in place of its predicate instances alone, and its prompt states the verb test: one verb for both means one predicate whatever the entity classes, and the same predicate instance alone is no reason to merge. Merging again shows each new predicate with the triple instances its split took. Why: the parts of one split share their words ("produced by"), so a listing of words alone would push the model to merge them back and undo correct splits; the entity classes show the difference the split was made for. Stage 4's prompt changed, so its answers are asked again on the next run. `max_new_predicates` stays 50. Whether the changes help is measured by running 040, 060 and 070 again.
- **The sample of texts (done on 2026-11-14).** It was 10 maintainers × 15 texts, which could never sample the records of the other 412 maintainers (8.4% of the catalog's records; the methods review of 2026-10-11, item 3). Stage 1 (selection) now draws up to `texts_per_stratum` texts (default 8) from each of the 19 strata of the ground truth candidates pool, equal allocation, since the aim is discovery, not estimation, as the TM's design was; only induction candidates, each stratum in a fixed random order (030's for a maintainer's own stratum; "(small maintainers)" shuffled the same way, with 030's seed), in rounds so that taking more keeps what was taken, and read in a random order fixed by the same seed. The old settings (`induction_maintainers`, `texts_per_maintainer`) stop a run with a message naming the new one; the schema of 2026-10-12 stays documented as made with them (040's guide, *Known limits*). Why now: *Inducing the schema from a larger or more varied sample*, under *Considered and not changed*, reversed. Corrected on 2026-11-16: this entry first gave 152 texts (19 × 8), but a stratum with fewer than 8 induction candidates that aren't twins of a text already drawn gives all it holds, and with the harvest of 2026-10-01 three strata do (Scott Goodwin 2, John M. Kusterer 7, Jennifer Blue 1), so the default draws 138 texts, as the code's note says. Decided on 2026-11-16: keep 138. The 14 texts those strata lack are not drawn from the other strata. Survey sampling has a take-all rule for a stratum smaller than its allocation, which takes every unit of that stratum and recomputes the allocation for the others (Tillé and Wilhelm, "Probability Sampling Designs: Principles for Choice of Design and Balancing", Statistical Science 32(2), 2017, pp. 176–189, [doi:10.1214/16-STS606](https://doi.org/10.1214/16-STS606), section 3: "When the population is skewed, Equation (2) often gives values nh > Nh ... In this case, all the units of the corresponding stratum are selected (take-all stratum) and the optimal allocation is recomputed on the other strata."). That rule serves an allocation that minimizes the variance of an estimate for a fixed total sample size. This design fixes the number of texts per stratum instead (equal allocation, so that no stratum is read more than another), and the induction texts estimate no metric, so drawing the 14 elsewhere would read some strata more than the rest. Still open: whether 8 per stratum, 138 texts, is enough (read *Saturation*; compare designs with `helpers/compare_runs.py`). From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-12.
- **`min_support`** above 1 (it is 1, which keeps everything found): try other values once runs can be compared. From: 040's guide, *Settings*. The minimum is a count of texts, so the same value is a stricter or looser rule depending on how many texts are read: 3 of 150 texts is 2%, 3 of 1,500 is 0.2%. When samples of different sizes are compared (*The sample of texts*, above), state the minimum as a proportion of the texts read, or the comparison mixes the sample size with the threshold. From: the TM review, 2026-11-04.
- **Near-duplicate texts inflate support (in part done on 2026-11-14).** If a maintainer's 15 texts are nearly the same text, every schema entry found in them counts 15 times. Measure how alike each maintainer's texts are; then drop the near-duplicates (taking others from the same maintainer), or count that maintainer once. It matters once `min_support` is above 1. 040's report lists twins between the texts it learned from and the pool, not near-duplicates among its own texts. From: my old to-do list. Done: stage 1 skips a text that is a whole-text or notes-only twin of a text already drawn (the same `notes`, evened out; `common/common_helpers/ground_truth.py`, `twin_kinds`) and takes the next in its stratum; a title-only twin is kept, since its text differs; the report counts each, per stratum. Not done, on purpose: near-duplicates (the same `notes` but for a granule number or a date) are still drawn. Catching them needs a similarity measure and a threshold to choose and justify, while exact twins need neither, are defined once for the whole pipeline, and with `min_support` 1 support decides nothing (040's guide, *Known limits*, *Only exact twins are skipped*).
- **Nothing filters junk from stage 2:** e.g. "dataset" as a bare subject instance, or "VNP43D66 is the BSA" read as an "is a" statement, both gaining real support. A list of generic subject instances to drop would cost nothing. From: my old to-do list.
- **Show the source text when labeling (stage 3) (done on 2026-11-14: each component instance, of both kinds, is sent with its shortest triple instance and that triple instance's source text; 040's guide, *How it works*, stage 3, cites TRACE-KG):** the proposal was: stage 3 labels a subject or object instance by the shortest triple instance it appears in (040's guide, *How it works*), which may not say what it is. Suppose the shortest triple instance with "Dawn" is "Dawn" – "carries" – "GRaND"; then Dawn could be a mission or a spacecraft, while a source text such as "the GRaND instrument aboard the Dawn spacecraft" says which (see *Mission or spacecraft, when they share a name*, under 050). Sending each instance's source text too makes the label prompts longer. From: TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3), whose prompt for entity classes gives the model, for each thing, 20 to 120 words of context as its "PRIMARY evidence for meaning" (`TKG_Prompts.py`, line 419); that context is the model's own words, while this would be the record's.
- **040's statistics (done on 2026-11-14).** The report (and `induction_evidence.json`, key `statistics`) gives triple instances per text (min, max, median, mean, standard deviation), also per stratum; the texts with no triple instance; what each code check of stages 2 to 8 removed, with the texts it removed wholly (record id and stratum); the model calls failed or cut off, by stage; the twins skipped while drawing; and the schema text's length per added predicate (040's guide, *How it works*, *Statistics*). The per-text table every step shares gained the standard deviation. From: the TP's plan, 2026-11-14.
- **Stage 2 (extraction of triple instances) as 060 extracts (decided and done on 2026-11-14).** 040's `extract.txt` now has 060's layout and order, the shared two passes and closing line, prompt caching for Anthropic's models, and 050's and 060's duplicate count (`triple_key`); its outputs keep their shape. `tests/allowed_differences.txt` lists only what the task requires: no SCHEMA section and no worked examples (they come with an example schema that would steer the schema 040 learns), and `tests/test_parallel.py` (*Forty*) checks the layout. From: the 040 changes of 2026-11-14.
- **One set of defined words in the prompts of 040 and 070 (done on 2026-11-14).** Each of those prompts (040's `extract.txt` aside, which carries the rules and their words) starts with a WORDS section that defines every term it uses, from one file, `common/common_prompts/words.txt`, worded as the extraction rules word the terms they also define; "thing" and "kind" in their plain sense are gone (kind is entity class or predicate). `tests/test_parallel.py` (*Words*) checks all three. From: the review of the 040 changes, 2026-11-14.
- **Alternatives to stage 4 (merging synonymous labels):** grouping by similarity, passing the chosen labels forward from batch to batch, or another approach entirely. From: my old to-do list.
- **Which text fields to learn from:** 040 uses `title` and `notes` and any field 020's `extra_text_fields` adds; which other fields help is untested. First candidate: the names of the files attached to a record (e.g. `…L3m.8D.SST4…nc`), which can say what a dataset holds when its notes describe several (2026-10-19, record #4). From: my old to-do list.
- **Ideas to compare the induced schema with:** the Purview glossary, Neo4j's capabilities, the five science domains of NASA's Science Mission Directorate (SMD) site, and the filters data.nasa.gov's search offers. From: my old to-do list. Extended on 2026-11-01: **first the schemas reviewers know**: DCAT 3 (*DCAT 3.0*, under 020), SOSA/SSN (the W3C's vocabulary of sensors, platforms and observations, [its page](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-ssn/)) and GCMD's keyword categories. A table of the induced schema's entity classes and predicates against them, each judged as TRACE-KG judges (Equivalent, Narrower, Broader or Unrelated: *Kinds of translation*, under 070), says how much of the induced schema is standard and how much is the catalog's own. Effort: 1 day, by hand. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: no. From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap J.

## 050_annotate

- **Fifth round of rule fixes (decided on 2026-11-13; done).** A fourth pair of independent audits of the rules and the ground truth was made after the entry below. I decided the nine points that follow, and each was checked against the record's text, the rule's words and the ground truth schema before it was applied. The audits and the changes were made by a model (Claude Opus 5.5) and are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. One ground truth triple was added and one was deleted, so the finished records keep 432 ground truth triples. Both copies of the rules changed (`annotations/guidelines.md`, and `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt` with invented examples), and the first worked example of the prompts (`common/common_prompts/extraction_examples.txt`) follows point 6, so the fingerprint of every prompt that holds the shared rules or the worked examples changes, and the next drafting or extraction run is a new recipe. This entry ran no step of the pipeline.
  - 1. Record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0": "COMET ESCORT 3" (MissionPhase) HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko 1 (1969 R1)" (CelestialBody) is added, with the source text "COMET ESCORT 3 … comet 67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko 1 (1969 R1)" and the rules 12, 19, 20, 21 and 23. The phase's name holds the word "COMET", the record names one comet, and rule 23 writes it in the wording that says more, the wording of the record's dataset's HAS_TARGET. The source text follows rule 17's *A name's facts*: the name as written, joined by " … " to the passage where the record writes the comet's wording, as "COMET ESCORT 4 … the comet 67P" does. Rule 20 is listed because the instance holds its own code in parentheses. The two parallel triples are "COMET ESCORT 4" HAS_TARGET "67P" (record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0") and "comet escort 1" HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko" (record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0"). Every mission phase of the finished records was then read for the same triple. "approach" and "encounter" (record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0") already have HAS_TARGET "9P/Tempel 1", from words of their own ("approach to and encounter with comet 9P/Tempel 1"), and "cruise" is named with no body. No other phase is named with its target.
  - 2. The column `rules`, made uniform. (a) Rule 19 is listed on every row whose object is a qualifier that rule 19 takes out, a title's qualifiers and a name's included. Seven rows gained it: record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0", HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 3" and OBSERVED_AT_TIME_OF_DAY "night"; record "CH1-ORB MOON M3 2 L0 RAW NEAR-IR SPECTRAL IMAGES V1.0", HAS_TARGET "Moon" of the record's dataset and of "Chandrayaan-1" ("Chandrayaan-1 mission to the Moon", as "approach to … comet 9P/Tempel 1" already lists it); record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0", HAS_TARGET "67P" ("a Global Gravity measurement at the comet 67P"); record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006", "MOD11_L2" HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "L2", read from a name like "Moon Mineralogy Mapper (M3)" HAS_TARGET "Moon"; record "HIRDLS/Aura Level 3 Geopotential Height 1deg Lat Zonal Fourier Coefficients V007 (H3ZFCGPH)", HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "from -64 to 80 degrees", taken out of the same phrase as HAS_SPATIAL_RESOLUTION "1 degree latitude", which lists it. (b) Rule 7 is listed on every approximate value ("about", "~", "over", "approximately"). Three rows gained it: OBSERVED_AT_ALTITUDE "about 30 km" (record "BARREL 4G Rate Counter (RCNT) NaI Scintillator Diagnostics, Level 2, 4 s Data"), and HAS_DURATION and HAS_GRANULE_DURATION "~3 years" (the HIRDLS record). (c) Rows of one kind list the rules of that kind. The three "gridded" rows list rules 15 and 24 (rule 24, *Words that name no method*, gives the fact, and each phrase states several facts), and keep the rules that decide their own wording: HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION "Gridded" (record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005") "15, 16" → "15, 16, 23, 24"; "spatially gridded" (the SST record) "15, 19, 20" → "15, 20, 24", since "gridded" is none of rule 19's kinds and rule 20 takes "(L3)" out; "gridded" (record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid") "24" → "15, 24". The two "produced daily" rows both list "16, 23, 24": the adverb "daily" qualifies the verb, not an instance, so rule 19 takes nothing out, and rule 24's example ("is produced daily using 16 days of data") is the rule that gives the fact. HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION "Daily" of the record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid" changed from "16, 19, 23". The two rows of the sentence "For complete information about known issues please refer to" both list "9, 20": DOCUMENTED_AT of the record "VIIRS/NPP BRDF/Albedo Parameter 3 Band M4 Daily L3 Global 30 ArcSec CMG V002" gained rule 9, as `annotations/left_out.md` already says of "known issues". (d) The SST record's DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY "NASA Ocean Biology Processing Group (OBPG)", written from "The NASA MODIS L3 SST data products are generated by", lists rule 1 (*Membership by name*), like the other rows written from a statement about a group. (e) HAS_TARGET "Comet Borrelly" (record "Solar Orbiter Ephemeris, Heliocentric Trajectories, Heliographic, Heliographic Inertial, and Solar Ecliptic Coordinates, HelioWeb, Daily Data") no longer lists rule 1. `annotations/left_out.md` has no reference note for it, none could be looked up in this round, and the word "Comet" inside the name gives its entity class (point 3). Changed in all: rule 19 on seven rows added and two taken off, rule 7 on three added, rule 24 on three added, rule 15 on one added, rule 23 on one added, rule 9 on one added, and rule 1 on one added and two taken off (the second is in point 3).
  - 3. Rule 1 now says: "A name whose own words say what kind of thing it is ("Moderate Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer (MODIS)", "Satellite Needs Working Group (SNWG)") is explained by the record's own words, since the kind word inside the name is the record's own word, when that word fits the definition of exactly one entity class." The condition at its end is the one rule 1 sets for a reference's entry, and it decides point 8. Such names need no reference note, and a row lists rule 1 for them only when another part of rule 1 decides it (a reference for another instance, a convention, a test). Every row that lists rule 1 was read against this. One more row no longer lists it: record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0", ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT "OSIRIS Narrow Angle Camera" "1, 16, 23" → "16, 23" (the reference note on "OSIRIS" serves "OSIRIS Narrow Angle Camera" PART_OF "OSIRIS", which keeps rule 1). ON_GRID "Climate Modeling Grid (CMG)" (the VIIRS record) keeps rule 1, since it is written by *Membership by name*. The rows of "Moderate Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer (MODIS)", "Visible Infrared Imaging Radiometer Suite (VIIRS)" and the names with "Program", "Group", "Government" or "School" list rule 1 only where another part of rule 1 decides them (a reference note for "NASA", "JPL", "OPERA", "Earth", "U.S." or the Suomi NPP platform, or *Membership by name*), and needed no change.
  - 4. Rule 2's list of what naming states has a new entry: "a Mission named in a Dataset's name gives FROM_MISSION". FROM_MISSION is "was collected during this Mission" (Dataset -> Mission), the one predicate of the ground truth schema that says that data come from a mission, as FROM_PLATFORM says it of a Platform. The record "BARREL 4G Rate Counter (RCNT) NaI Scintillator Diagnostics, Level 2, 4 s Data", FROM_MISSION "BARREL" with the title's "BARREL" as source text, rests on the list now. No row changed.
  - 5. A title's words and the name test. Rule 17's *A title's facts* now says: "A title's words give a fact only where a predicate of the ground truth schema fits what they state, by the test of rule 2 of *What counts as a fact* (*A fact inside a name*): the title's "Solar Orbiter Ephemeris" names an ephemeris of the craft Solar Orbiter, and FROM_PLATFORM ("was collected from this Platform") is false of it, so "Solar Orbiter" gives no ground truth triple." Rule 2's example of "Solar Orbiter Ephemeris" gives the same reason and points to rule 17. A title's "DAWN" still gives FROM_PLATFORM, since there the Platform is named in a Dataset's name. No row changed: the Solar Orbiter record has HAS_VARIABLE "Solar Orbiter Ephemeris" and no FROM_PLATFORM, and its item of `annotations/left_out.md` already gives the reason. The second opinion's FROM_PLATFORM "Solar Orbiter" in `annotations/second_opinion_candidates.csv` now cites rule 2 of *What counts as a fact*, as that item does; it cited rule 1.
  - 6. The source text of a qualifier's fact. Rule 17's *The thing the title names* now says: "A fact that a qualifier states with no words for the predicate therefore has the qualifier's words alone as its source text, the shortest passage (case 1), when its subject is the thing the title names. When its subject is another thing, the passage also holds the words for that subject." Its example gives "provides daily snow cover" to HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "snow cover", and "daily" and "global" to the two qualifiers; it gave "provides daily snow cover" to HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION "daily". A second example gives "HIRDLS profiles" HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 2" the source text "HIRDLS Level 2 profiles". Eight of the ten rows that the audits listed changed (the other two already had the qualifier alone), and fifteen more, each before → after. Record "MODIS/Aqua Snow Cover 5-Min L2 Swath 500m V061": HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "global", "This global Level-2 (L2) data set" → "global"; HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level-2 (L2)", "Level-2 (L2) data set" → "Level-2 (L2)"; HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION "daily", "provides daily snow cover" → "daily"; HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION "Swath", "5 minutes of swath data" → "swath". Record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005": "weekly gridded Level-3 products" → "weekly", and "gridded Level-3 products" → "gridded" and "Level-3". Record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid": HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Land", "Land Aerosol Optical Depth" → "Land"; "gridded Level 2 product" → "Level 2" and "gridded". Record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006": HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION "Daily", "provides daily per-pixel" → "daily"; "the daytime and nighttime surface temperature bands" → "daytime" and "nighttime". Record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0": the record's dataset's HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 2", "This data set contains Level 2" → "Level 2", and "Level 3", "This data set contains Level 2/3" → "Level 2/3". Record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0": "This data set contains CODMAC level 2 data" → "CODMAC level 2". Record "OPERA Dynamic Surface Water Extent from Sentinel-1 (Version 1)": HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "near-global", "DSWx-S1 provides near-global" → "near-global". Not changed: the rows that already had the qualifier alone ("Polar", "Near Real Time (NRT)", the OPERA record's "Level-3", the title's words); the rows whose subject is another thing, which hold that thing's words ("HIRDLS Level 2 profiles", "the L2 pixels", the rows of "EDR (raw) sensor data"); and the rows whose qualifier is joined to its noun by a word for the predicate ("at the comet 67P", "for Comet Borrelly", "produced daily"). The first worked example of the prompts follows: the source text of its HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION "daily" is "daily". `annotations/questions_for_experts.md`: the "source text" fields of the example records of Q1, Q5, Q6 and Q11 follow.

"Earth" inside "version 5 Hierarchical Data Format for the Earth Observing System (HDF-EOS5)" (the HIRDLS record) gives no ground truth triple, and `annotations/left_out.md` has an item that says why. The fact that the inner name states would need "Earth Observing System" as an instance, and its kind word "System" fits no single entity class (point 3); no public reference was looked up for it. In "Earth Observation Satellites (EOS)" (the SST record), "Earth" qualifies the satellites, which are the instance itself, so the name states HAS_TARGET "Earth". The two cases differ in which thing the word qualifies.

- **Fourth round of rule fixes (decided on 2026-11-12; done).** A third pair of independent audits of the rules and the ground truth was made on 2026-11-12, after the entry below. I decided the fifteen points that follow, and each was checked against the record's text, the rule's words and the ground truth schema before it was applied. The audits and the changes were made by a model (Claude Fable 5.2) and are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. Six ground truth triples were added and four were deleted, so the finished records have 432 ground truth triples (430 before). Both copies of the rules changed (`annotations/guidelines.md`, and `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt` with invented examples), so the fingerprint of every prompt that holds the shared rules changes, and the next drafting or extraction run is a new recipe. This entry ran no step of the pipeline.
  - 1. Record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3": "SDSS Quasar Catalog" (Dataset) HAS_TARGET "Quasar" (CelestialBody) is a ground truth triple again, with the source text "SDSS Quasar Catalog" and the rules 12, 19 and 21. It was dropped earlier on 2026-11-12 for the reason that "Quasar" names a kind of body. That reason was wrong: the ground truth schema defines CelestialBody as "a natural object in space, or a kind of such objects, e.g. Vesta; active galactic nuclei", and the same record has HAS_TARGET "active galactic nuclei (AGN)", a kind. HAS_TARGET is the one predicate of the ground truth schema that joins a thing to a CelestialBody ("only: any -> CelestialBody"), so the name states it (rule 2, *A fact inside a name*; rule 19, a target body), as "Moon Mineralogy Mapper (M3)" HAS_TARGET "Moon" does. The same reading holds for a mission phase named with its comet. Record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0": "COMET ESCORT 4" (MissionPhase) HAS_TARGET "67P" (CelestialBody) is added, with the source text "COMET ESCORT 4 … the comet 67P" and the rules 12, 19, 21 and 23. The comet of the phase's name is the one comet the record names, and rule 23 writes it in the record's wording for it, "67P" ("comet" is its type word). The record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0" has the parallel triple, "comet escort 1" HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko". `annotations/left_out.md` had no item for the word; the second opinion's "COMET ESCORT 4" HAS_TARGET "comet" was marked left out by rule 11, a reason from before rule 2's *A fact inside a name*, and is now "already in the ground truth in other words".
  - 2. Record "Flight Data For Tail 655": the record's dataset HAS_VARIABLE "sensor recordings" (Variable) is added, with the source text that ends at the list's first item and the rules 9 and 22, like its four siblings. `annotations/left_out.md` left the item out by rule 4, whose words are about a sentence that says what kind of thing something is; the record has no such sentence, since it says that the data structure "contains" the recordings. The predicate follows the definitions of the ground truth schema. The recordings are the measured values themselves, so they are not "supporting information about its values" (HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA), which the four other items are. "sensor recordings" names no single quantity, so HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF, which is pinned to a PhysicalQuantity ("a bare property that can be measured"), does not fit. HAS_VARIABLE fits: it is "holds this Variable in its files", and the recordings are what each parameter's structure holds, narrowed by the sensor that made them. The second opinion drafted the same classed triple. The item is deleted from `annotations/left_out.md`.
  - 3. The column `rules`, made uniform. (a) Rule 18 is listed on every row whose object is an amount, and rule 18 now says that a count is one: "A count with the noun for what is counted is such an amount: the object instance is the number and the noun, as written." Fifteen HAS_NUMBER_OF rows gained it ("353,191 detections", "262,902 unique X-ray sources", "412 quasars", "1376 observations", "16 frequency channels", "one data layer", "Seven balloon launches", "four BARREL campaigns", "over 50 small, approximately 20 kg, stratospheric balloons", "186 parameters", "up to 7 sine coefficients", "up to 7 cosine coefficients", "4 waves" twice, "7 waves"). (b) Rule 1 is listed on every row whose processing level or version rests on rule 1's convention ("L" or "Level", "V" or "Version", followed by a number). Five rows gained it: HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 2" (three records), "Level 3" (one record) and HAS_VERSION "Version 6". (c) Rule 20 is listed on every row with an instance that rule 20 writes (a name with its abbreviation or a short note in parentheses). Sixteen rows gained it; the same instances already listed it in their other rows. (d) "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3" COMPUTED_FROM "2XMMi-DR3", whose sentence has the subject "the authors", lists rule 12 like the two rows beside it. (e) Two numbers that decided nothing were taken off: rule 4 on the four HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA rows of "Flight Data For Tail 655", and rule 24 on HAS_VARIABLE "Ensemble X-Ray Variability".

Rule 17, *A title's facts*, now says which passage is the source text when the title and the notes both hold the words: "The source text is the passage that states the fact about its subject. 1. When a passage of the notes states the fact about the record's dataset itself, that passage is the source text; the title's words are joined before it only when rule 23 writes the instance in the title's wording". "2. A noun phrase of the notes that only names the instance, inside a statement about another thing, does not state the title's fact". The rows that the audits listed were read against it. Record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0": the HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION row ends at "image data", without "in W/m^2/sr/nm", the words of the HAS_UNIT row.
  - 7. Record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3": "Serendipitous Source Catalog" (Dataset) HAS_RELEASE "second version of the catalogue" (Dataset) is added (rules 12, 21 and 23), with the source text from "Serendipitous Source Catalog" to "the second version of the catalogue". It is the same fact of a catalog and one of its versions as "SDSS Quasar Catalog" HAS_RELEASE "DR7 edition of the SDSS Quasar Catalog", and it joins the two Datasets that "2XMMi-DR3" is a release of.
  - 8. Rule 19, *Whose fact it is*, now says: "A qualifier of the "data" or "products" that a dataset "contains" or "consists of" is that dataset's fact, since those words name no other thing. Record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0", "This data set contains Level 2/3 (EDR/RDR) data": the record's dataset HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 2" and HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 3" are added (rules 1, 19, 20 and 22), as the record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0" has HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "CODMAC level 2" from "This data set contains CODMAC level 2 data". The three campaigns' other ground truth triples are no longer connected to the record's id by a chain of ground truth triples; they stay (rule 21).

The three items of `annotations/left_out.md` that cite rule 3 for a heading quote this sentence; two of them quoted words the rule did not have.

A title's "BRDF/Albedo" when the notes show one name. 7. An address attached to a thing with no words for a predicate (rule 20, *A page named, or given as an address*, case 3). 9. Whether "from -64 to 80 degrees" (HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE) is an amount in rule 18's sense; it does not list rule 18. 10. - **Third round of rule fixes (decided on 2026-11-12; done).** Two more audits of the rules and the ground truth were made independently on 2026-11-12, after the two entries below. I decided the ten points that follow, and each was checked against the record's text and the rule's words before it was applied. The audits and the changes were made by a model (Claude Fable 5.2) and are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. No ground truth triple was added or deleted, so the finished records keep 430 ground truth triples. Both copies of the rules changed (`annotations/guidelines.md`, and `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt` with invented examples), so the fingerprint of every prompt that holds the shared rules changes, and the next drafting or extraction run is a new recipe.
  - 1. Which fact a name's word states (rule 2, *A fact inside a name*). The sentence "A word that names another thing without saying how the two things are related gives no ground truth triple either" allowed two readings of every name that begins with another thing's name. The rule now gives the test: such a word "states a fact only when the entity classes of the two things leave one reading", that is, when "the ground truth schema has exactly one predicate whose definition fits a thing of the one entity class that carries the name of a thing of the other". The rule lists what the test gives with the ground truth schema as it is: "an Instrument named in a Dataset's name gives ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT, a Platform named in a Dataset's name gives FROM_PLATFORM, a Mission named in the name of one of its parts (an instrument, a craft, a campaign, a team) gives PART_OF, and an Organization named before a group's own name gives PART_OF". "When more than one predicate fits, or none, the name's words give no ground truth triple". The example of "NASA/NOAA" is replaced by one example with both outcomes ("Sentinel-1 radar observations" FROM_PLATFORM "Sentinel-1"; no ground truth triple for "Copernicus" in "modified Copernicus Sentinel data", since the programme could have provided, sponsored or made the data). Each predicate was checked against its definition in `annotations/ground_truth_schema.txt`. Every row written from a name's words alone passes the test, and no row changed: ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT "GRaND" and "HIRDLS" (the one predicate that fits a Dataset and an Instrument), FROM_PLATFORM "Sentinel-1", PART_OF "BARREL" of "BARREL instrumentation", "BARREL balloon", "BARREL team" and "August 2015 BARREL campaign", PART_OF "NASA" of the five groups named with it, and PART_OF "GRaND" and "OSIRIS" of the sensor and the camera named with them. The three items of `annotations/left_out.md` that cite this reason keep their outcome and quote the new sentence: "NASA/NOAA" (a Platform and an Organization: LAUNCHED_BY_PARTY, DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY and SPONSORED_BY_PARTY all fit), "Copernicus" and "Sentinel", and "OPERA" as the developer (a Dataset and an Organization: PROVIDED_BY_PARTY, SPONSORED_BY_PARTY and DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY all fit). Decided the same day (*Settled the same day*, 1, below): the test asks which predicate the naming itself states, and naming a unit with an organization's name before its own states that the unit belongs to the organization, so PART_OF stays; SPONSORED_BY_PARTY and DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY also join two Organizations, and a name never states them. To decide: "Rosetta Orbiter" PART_OF "Rosetta" would pass the test if "Rosetta" inside the platform's name is the mission, which the entry *A fact counts wherever its words are, even inside a name* left open; and the item on "NASA" in "global NASA skin sea surface temperature (SST) products" (record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0") still reads the word as who made the products and leaves it out by rule 16, where the test would give no fact (more than one predicate fits). Its outcome is the same.
  - 2. Record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0", from "the L2 pixels are binned into an integerized sinusoidal area grid (ISEAG) and mapped into an equidistant cylindrical": "pixels" (Dataset) ON_GRID "integerized sinusoidal area grid (ISEAG)" and "pixels" (Dataset) MAPPED_INTO "equidistant cylindrical" now have the record's dataset as their subject. ON_GRID is "its values are given on the cells or points of this Grid" and MAPPED_INTO is "its values were laid onto a map drawn with this MapProjection": the values on the grid and on the map are those of the L3 products, the step's output, and the L2 pixels are its input (COMPUTED_FROM "pixels" and "pixels" HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "L2" stay). The two rows no longer list rules 12 and 21 in the column `rules`.
  - 3. Rule 2's list of the rules that leave a fact out is complete now. Every "Rule:" line of `annotations/left_out.md` was counted: the items cite rules 1 to 10 of *What counts as a fact* and rules 16, 20, 21, 22, 23 and 24 of *Writing a classed triple*. The list lacked rule 2's own part *A fact inside a name* (4 items with this point), rule 22 (3 items: an item of a list that names no specific thing, and the abbreviation of a list of several things) and rule 23 (13 items: a type word), and names them now.   - Settled the same day (2026-11-12). I decided the two points left to decide above and one more, and the rules, the ground truth and the files that follow them were changed. No ground truth triple was added or deleted, and only source texts changed (23 rows), so the finished records keep 430 ground truth triples.
    - 1. The name test (rule 2, *A fact inside a name*) asks which predicate a name states: "The predicate must be one that the naming itself states, which the entity classes of the two things and the naming pattern (which of the two names stands inside the other's, and where) decide, and the ground truth schema must have exactly one such predicate." An Organization named before a group's own name keeps PART_OF: "naming a unit with an organization's name before its own states that the unit belongs to that organization, and states neither sponsorship nor development". The rule adds: "A relationship that naming does not state (that one thing funded, developed, provided or sponsored the other) is never read from a name, and is a fact only when the record has words that say so." "Copernicus" still gives no ground truth triple: "a programme's name before "Sentinel data" could mean that the programme provided the data, funded them or collected them, and naming does not settle which". No row changed. The four items of `annotations/left_out.md` that quote the test ("NASA/NOAA", "Copernicus" and "Sentinel", "OPERA" as the developer, and "Solar Orbiter") quote the new sentences and give the new reason.

The rule adds: "An abbreviation alone, the name without its abbreviation, or any other wording shorter than the instance names the thing in other words (rule 23), although its words are all in the instance". The source text then "also holds the passage that shows the two wordings name one thing, usually the instance where the record spells it out", as one passage when no whole sentence lies between (case 2) and joined by " … " in the record's order otherwise (case 3). *A name's facts* says the same of a name that holds only an abbreviation, and the examples of the Van Allen Probes and of "VIIRS band M4 (0.555 μm)" follow. 23 source texts changed. Each is given here as what was added, and the earlier source text stays inside the new one.

- **Rules made clear after the two audits (decided on 2026-11-12; done).** The two audits of 2026-11-12 found places where a rule allowed two readings, and rows of the same kind that were written differently. I decided each of the nine points below, and the rules, the ground truth and the files that follow them were changed the same day. The changes were made by a model (Claude Fable 5.2) and are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. The finished records have 430 ground truth triples now (431 before). Both copies of the rules changed (`annotations/guidelines.md`, and `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt` with invented examples), so the fingerprint of every prompt that holds the shared rules changes, and the next drafting or extraction run is a new recipe.
  - 1. A list joined by "/" or "+" (rule 22, *Lists joined by "/" or "+"*, the one home of the test). In a title, a "/" or "+" "always separates two items of the list of facts of the record's dataset (rule 17, *A title's facts*), and never joins the words of one name". In the notes, the list is split "only when every item is also named alone somewhere in the record, outside the "/" or "+" phrase and not only inside another name; otherwise it stays whole, as written". Rule 17 points to rule 22 in *A title's facts* and in *A platform in an instrument's name*, item 3, and no longer says it in its own words. Two sentences keep what the ground truth already has: "A title's word is named alone", and "Two such lists that the record matches item by item split together" (in "Level 2/3 (EDR/RDR)" only "EDR" and "RDR" are named alone, and the two levels are written apart). The rule's example of a title's "RSI 1/2/3" that stays whole is replaced by four examples from the records. No ground truth triple changed, since every row already follows the rule. Record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0": the title's "67P/CAL" is a list, and its item "67P" gives HAS_TARGET. Record "HIRDLS/Aura Level 3 Geopotential Height 1deg Lat Zonal Fourier Coefficients V007 (H3ZFCGPH)": the title's "HIRDLS/Aura" is a list. Record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005": the notes' "Aquarius/Satélite de Aplicaciones Científicas (SAC-D)" stays whole, since its second item is named nowhere else. Record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0": the title's "9P/TEMPEL 1" is a list whose item is written in the notes' wording, "9P/Tempel 1" (rule 23), so the row is as it was and now lists rule 22. The annotation tool's flag `slash_list_split` follows the rule (`helpers/annotator/server.py`, `050_annotate/050_annotate.md`): it flags an item of a list of the notes only, never an item of a title's list.
  - 2. A processing step on the record's dataset's own values (rule 24, a new part, *The record's dataset's own values*): "When a step's output is the record's dataset's own values, the output is the record's dataset: it is the subject of COMPUTED_FROM and PRODUCED_USING_ALGORITHM, also when the record words the output as one of the dataset's Variables or as its values". One sentence keeps the rows whose output is one Variable among several: "A Variable that the record tells apart from the dataset's other Variables, with a step of its own, is the output of that step" ("precision values" in the same record; "SST4" and "skin SST" in the record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0"; the two band emissivities in the record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006"). Record "HIRDLS/Aura Level 3 Geopotential Height 1deg Lat Zonal Fourier Coefficients V007 (H3ZFCGPH)": the subject of COMPUTED_FROM "HIRDLS profiles" and of PRODUCED_USING_ALGORITHM "Kalman filter using both forward and backward passes in time" was "Geopotential Height Zonal Fourier Coefficients" (Variable) and is the record's dataset now, as in the records "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006", "BARREL 4G Rate Counter (RCNT) NaI Scintillator Diagnostics, Level 2, 4 s Data" and "MODIS/Aqua Snow Cover 5-Min L2 Swath 500m V061".
  - 3. Facts that no chain of ground truth triples connects to the record's id (rule 21, one sentence added to its heading): "A fact the record states is a ground truth triple whether or not a chain of ground truth triples connects it to the record's id (rule 2 of *What counts as a fact*), and this rule leaves out only what its two parts name: a statement that only explains a concept, and a fact about another dataset." *Finishing a record* now says that such a triple "stays in the ground truth in either case", and the model's copy says the same in its *Connect your triples*. Record "HIRDLS/Aura Level 3 Geopotential Height 1deg Lat Zonal Fourier Coefficients V007 (H3ZFCGPH)": HAS_IDENTIFIER has the source text "product (H3ZFCGPH)", so the three HAS_IDENTIFIER rows read from a product's code in parentheses are cut alike.
  - 7. The type words of a Dataset (rule 23, *Type words*). The rule decided every row but one kind, and two sentences were added. The first widens the test for a type word that stays: "The type word stays in the instance only when the words left once it is taken out aren't a name, or are the name of another thing" ("VNP43MA1 product page" stays whole, since "VNP43MA1" alone is the product). The second states what most rows already did: "The type words of a Dataset include "product", "data product", "data set", "data record", "catalogue" and "file". The word "data" is not a type word: it stays in the instance, as written, whatever words stand beside it." Every Dataset instance and every Document instance of the finished records was read against the two sentences, and no row changed ("MOD11_L2", "2XMMi-DR3" and "table1.dat" are written without their type word; "RDR data", "EDR (raw) sensor data", "radiance data", "encounter data", "original trajectory data" and "modified Copernicus Sentinel data" keep "data").
  - 8. Programs. A program that an organization runs is an Organization, one of its named parts (the ground truth schema's Organization: "a named institution or one of its named parts, not a Mission"). `annotations/left_out.md` says so in the item on "NASA Living with a Star", and has a new item for "Copernicus" and "Sentinel" in "modified Copernicus Sentinel data": they give no ground truth triple because the name does not say how the data and the two things are related (rule 2, *A fact inside a name*), not because a programme has no entity class. The entry *An entity class may come from a public reference*, below, and two decisions in `annotations/second_opinion_candidates.csv` (they now cite rule 2 of *What counts as a fact*, where they cited rule 1) follow. Rule 1 of the guidelines has no wording against it, and no rule changed.

FROM_PLATFORM "Aqua" of the record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0" had no value and lists rule 23. HAS_TARGET "9P/Tempel 1" of the record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0" lists rule 22 too (point 1), and COMPUTED_FROM "2XMMi-DR3" lists rule 8 too (point 6). - **One copy of the rules, built for the model; one name per concept (decided and done on 2026-11-14).** The rules have one home, `annotations/guidelines.md`. The model's copy, `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`, is now built from it by `py helpers/build_rules.py` (`common/common_helpers/model_rules.py`), and `annotations/rules_model_differences.txt` lists every way in which the two differ, each with its reason; `tests/test_model_rules.py` fails while the model's copy is not what the build gives. The rules family of `tests/allowed_differences.txt` moved into that file. The guidelines now give each rule's text first and its examples as bullets after it, and write each fact by its meaning with the ground truth schema's name in parentheses ("collected by the instrument (ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT)"), so that the model, whose schema differs by step, reads the same sentences without the names. Each statement is now made once, in its home rule, and other rules point to it by number and short name ("rule 22, a list"): the source text of facts from several passages (rule 1 and rule 17, now only *Words in different places*), a title's words as a list of facts (rule 17's *A title's facts*; three other parts point to it), a period or range as one instance (rule 22; rule 11 points to it), the unexplained code (rule 1; rule 2 points to it), what data are used for (rule 9; the membership test no longer lists it), and rule 11's list of exceptions (the numbers of the rules that take words out). The words section, shared by both copies, defines every term the model's copy uses, among them **entity** (one individual that an instance's words refer to) and **the title's instance** (it replaces "the thing the title names"); "thing" is gone from both copies, from `docs/terminology.md`, and from four definitions of the ground truth schema (Identifier, DesignFeature, Taxon, ALSO_KNOWN_AS), which models see. "A fact the record does not state in one passage" now reads "a fact that the record does not state may be written only when it follows from what the record states by one of the three tests", since the replacement test's own source text is one passage. Wording only: no ground truth case is decided differently. The quotes of the rules in `annotations/questions_for_experts.md` and `annotations/left_out.md` follow, and a new test checks the latter's quotes as the review page checks the former's. The fingerprints of steps 040, 050 and 060 change. The prompts of schema induction's labeling, merging and defining stages and of 070's translation suggestions still say "thing" and "kind of thing" in their plain sense; they were left as they are, since changing them would make those stages ask again for answers already cached. Note of 2026-11-16, two statements of this entry that later changes overtook: (1) those prompts no longer say "thing" or "kind of thing" in their plain sense, since *One set of defined words in the prompts of 040 and 070* (section *040_induce_schema*, done on 2026-11-14); (2) an entity is now defined as what an instance's words refer to, "one particular instrument, spacecraft, mission, organization, or the like" (2026-11-15), no longer "one individual", in `docs/terminology.md`, the guidelines, the model's copy of the rules and the prompts' shared words.
- **The model's copy no longer names the public references; "kind" replaced in both copies (decided and done on 2026-11-14).** The model's copy of rule 1 told the model to give a name an entity class "only when a public reference would confirm" it, a reference it cannot consult: its calls have no tools, so that each answer depends on the prompt alone. The part is now *A name the record does not explain*: the model gives such a name an entity class from its own knowledge only when it is certain of it, never writes a long form the record doesn't give, never chooses an entity class from the verb that joins the name to another instance, and, when not certain, writes no triple whose meaning depends on what the name refers to (the name may still stay inside a longer instance, as "QX7" stays in "QX7 Wide Field Camera"). The lookup stays the annotator's job, a difference between the two copies (`tests/allowed_differences.txt`, rules, rule 1). Wording only, in both copies: "kind" in the sense of an entity class or a type of thing, which clashed with the term **kind** of `docs/terminology.md` (entity class or predicate), is now "entity class" or "type"; rule 1 says that an unexplained code gets no entity class "unless" the part on names gives it one, as the rule already decided; the cross-references "(names you recognize)" follow the new name. No case is decided differently: every ground truth triple whose `rules` cite rule 1 and every reference note in `annotations/left_out.md` was checked against the new wording. The model's copy changed, so the fingerprints of the prompts of 040, 050 and 060 change, and their next runs ask every call again; the induced schema in use (040 run of 2026-10-12) was made with an earlier version of the rules. From: the owner's review of 2026-11-14.
- **An entity class may come from a public reference (decided and done on 2026-11-12).** Until now rule 1 of *What counts as a fact* allowed one source outside the record for a name's entity class: the official NASA lists of its part *Names on an official NASA list*. I decided that a name's entity class may come from the record's own words or from a named public reference that anyone can look up: the official NASA lists first, then Wikidata. The reference gives the entity class only, and every ground truth triple must still be a fact the record states. The lookups and the changes below were made by a model (Claude Fable 5.2) and are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. The finished records have 431 ground truth triples now (428 before).
  - The rule (`annotations/guidelines.md`, rule 1): its part is now called *Names in a public reference*. The references are, in this order, an official NASA list (the GCMD Instruments, Platforms or Providers keywords, or the PDS4 context products; the Providers keywords are new) and Wikidata. The reference's entry "must clearly be the thing the record names": the record's wording is its name or one of its other names, and the entry fits what the record is about. What the entry says the thing is (the kind of thing the NASA list holds, or Wikidata's "instance of" statement and the "subclass of" statements above it) "must fit the definition of exactly one entity class of the ground truth schema"; otherwise "the name stays unexplained". "The entity class is never guessed from the sentence's verb". The note in `annotations/left_out.md` now holds the reference, its entry, its address and the date it was checked. The rule has a second example, the record's "412 quasars that were observed by XMM-Newton". Rule 17 names the part twice, and both places follow. Rule 2's example of an unexplained word of a name is now the "SIN" of "SIN Grid", since "OSIRIS" has an entity class (below). `README.md` (what fits only data.nasa.gov) and *Considered and not changed* (*The model's own knowledge in the ground truth or extraction*) follow.
  - The model's copy (`common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`, rule 1, *Names you recognize*): the model may give a name the entity class of its well-known kind "only when a public reference would confirm that kind: one of NASA's lists (the GCMD keywords for instruments, platforms and providers, or the Planetary Data System's context products) or Wikidata", must not guess the kind from the sentence's verb, and leaves the name out when it isn't sure. Its rule 2 says the same as the guidelines' about an unexplained word of a name. `tests/allowed_differences.txt` (family rules, rule 1) states the difference that remains: the annotator looks the name up and writes the entry down, and the model looks nothing up. The model's copy of the rules changed, so the fingerprint of every prompt that holds the shared rules changes (040's, 050's and 060's), and the next run of each of these steps asks every call again.

- **Fixes from the two audits (done on 2026-11-12).** Two audits of the ground truth (`annotations/ground_truth/batch_000.csv` to `batch_005.csv`) against `annotations/guidelines.md` were made independently on 2026-11-12. The fixes below are the findings that both audits made, or that I decided, each checked against the record's text and the rule's words before it was applied. The audits and the fixes were made by a model (Claude Fable 5.2) and are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. The finished records have 428 ground truth triples now (431 before).
  - Record "OPERA Dynamic Surface Water Extent from Sentinel-1 (Version 1)": the record's dataset DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY "OPERA" (Organization), source text "OPERA", is deleted. Rule 2, *A fact inside a name*: "A word that names another thing without saying how the two things are related gives no ground truth triple either." `annotations/left_out.md` has the item. The six ground truth triples about OPERA and the Satellite Needs Working Group stay, since the record states them ("OPERA" INFORMED_BY "Satellite Needs Working Group (SNWG)"; the group's PART_OF "U.S. Government", two HAS_PURPOSE and HAS_TARGET "Earth"; "U.S. Government" BASED_IN "U.S."). No chain of ground truth triples links them to the record's id any more, so the annotation tool flags them (⚑ not connected), as it flags the four ground truth triples about "Peter Minnett" in the record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0". The record states no fact that connects OPERA to its dataset (*Finishing a record*: "add that fact only if the record states it").

They now have the title's form. "swath" → "Swath" (record "MODIS/Aqua Snow Cover 5-Min L2 Swath 500m V061", HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION); "weekly" → "Weekly" (record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005", HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION); "daily" → "Daily" (HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION of the records "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid", "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006" and "VIIRS/NPP BRDF/Albedo Parameter 3 Band M4 Daily L3 Global 30 ArcSec CMG V002"); "Heliocentric trajectories" → "Heliocentric Trajectories" (record "Solar Orbiter Ephemeris, Heliocentric Trajectories, Heliographic, Heliographic Inertial, and Solar Ecliptic Coordinates, HelioWeb, Daily Data", HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF). The source texts stay the notes' passages. The ground truth now agrees with *Considered and not changed*, *Title-case ties in rule 23*, which did not adopt the lowercase forms. No score changes, since step 070's pairing ignores capitals.
