They name no ground truth triple now, so the second opinion is to be applied again (`helpers/second_opinion.md`) and the two pairs reviewed again after the next run of step 070. The model's copy of the rules changed, so the fingerprint of every prompt that holds the shared rules changes once more.
- **One wording for each concept in the rules (wording pass of 2026-11-12; no meaning changed).** I found the rules hard to apply: one concept had several wordings, and "left out" had two meanings. Both copies of the rules (`annotations/guidelines.md` and `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`) now use one wording for each concept. No rule was merged, moved, renumbered or changed in meaning, and every cross-reference between rules is kept.
  - A word that is not written in an instance is *taken out*. The rules also said "left out", "leaves out", "is not part of the instance" and "removed" for it.
  - A fact that is not written as a ground truth triple is *left out*, and its words *give no ground truth triple*. The rules also said "gets no ground truth triple", "is not written" and, in the model's copy, "gives nothing".
  - A word that is not taken out *stays in the instance*. The rules also said "stays" alone and "keep it".
  - A name, a list or a phrase that is written as one instance, with no word taken out, *stays whole*. The rules also said "kept whole", "keep it whole" and "the instance stays".
  - The record *states* a fact, and "a record says" only introduces a record's words in an example. The rules also said "the record says" for a statement.
  - The words rule 19 decides about are *qualifiers* in each of its parts (two parts said "a word"). Rule 4 says "kind" throughout (it also said "type"). Rule 12 says "the thing the title names", as rules 17, 20 and 23 do. Rule 16 says which wording to "use", as rule 23 does. In rule 17, the words for the thing the title names "need not be in the source text" (they "may be left out of" it before).
  - Each of these is stated once, in a new section of the guidelines, *How the rules use their words*, which also says how the thing the title names differs from the record's dataset. The model's copy has the same list at its top, under WORDS THE RULES USE, with the terms it uses defined in plain words (the thing the title names, the record's dataset, subject or object, qualifier). The explanations under the worked examples (`common/common_prompts/extraction_examples.txt`) follow in four places.
  - The quotes of the rules follow, in the words that changed only: `annotations/left_out.md` and `annotations/questions_for_experts.md`. Dated entries in this file and `docs/analysis/rules_audit.csv` keep the rules' words as they were then. `annotations/rules_by_triple.md` is regenerated.
  - The fingerprint of every prompt that holds the shared rules changes, so the next drafting or extraction run answers from no cached reply and is a new recipe.
  - Not changed, because the sentence could mean either of two concepts. 1. Rule 11's "A word that rules 5, 9, 10 or 19 take out stays, as written, when the record doesn't state the fact without it" and rule 9's "Such a word may stay": a word that stays by rule 9 can become part of what the predicate says, so "stays" there could mean "stays in the instance" or "stays in the fact". Resolved the same day, in both copies: rule 11 now says such a word "is not taken out when the record doesn't state the fact without it", and rule 9 says that such a word "is then part of what the predicate says when the object is an amount (rule 18), and it stays in the instance otherwise". 2. In the model's copy, rule 1's "If you don't know what kind of thing it is, leave it out": "it" may be the entity class or every triple that holds the name. 3. Rule 21 says "include" for writing a fact as a ground truth triple, in its title too, and rule 4 says "a classed triple of its own" where rules 11, 19 and 20 say "a ground truth triple of its own". Both are left as they are, since the titles are compared between the two copies and the second wording carries the term's only link in its section.
- **To do: more records for tuning the rules, and a held-out set (planned on 2026-11-12, after the audits of the rules).** The rules and the ground truth must be complete (every case in a record is decided by some rule), unambiguous (each case is decided one way only), consistent (no two rules decide the same case differently) and applied uniformly (every ground truth triple is checked against every rule), and the ground truth must be reproducible: two careful annotators reach the same ground truth. Two audits of every finished record, made independently, check the four properties, and the cases where they disagree show which rules are still unclear.
  - Tuning: new records from the sample, none in the ground truth, annotated under the rules in batches of 5. Every case no rule decides becomes a rule change, followed by the audits of all records again. Tuning stops when a batch of 5 shows no such case, or at 20 records.
  - Held-out: 10 more new records, none among the tuning records or the ground truth, annotated only after the tuning stops, with the rules fixed, by two annotators working independently (a Claude model and, if available, a model by another maker). Their agreement measures reproducibility, and the cases no rule decides measure whether the rules hold on records they were not tuned on. Both sets are preliminary model annotations, still to be reviewed by a person.
  - Held-out, done on 2026-11-13. The 10 held-out records are the first 10 of the pool's held-out part that were not finished, in pool order (pool positions 8, 11, 14, 17, 20, 23, 26, 29, 32 and 35). A model (Claude Opus 5.5) annotated them alone, with the rules frozen: no rule, prompt or existing entry of the ground truth schema changed, and the six entries it coined are marked held-out only. I dropped the second annotator. The same model already polished the tuning records, so a second annotator of the same family would measure little, and the check on a drafting model's bias is extraction by models of other makers, scored against the same ground truth. The cases that no rule decided, or that the rules decided two ways, were annotated by the closest rule and listed for a later round of rule fixes; none changed a rule. I review 2 of the 10 records in full. The annotations are preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. The tuning part (more records, in batches of 5) is not done before the deadline. On the same day, 10 more held-out records were annotated the same way, 20 in all: the next 10 of the held-out part in pool order (pool positions 38, 41, 44, 47, 50, 53, 56, 59, 62 and 65), in `annotations/ground_truth/batch_007.csv`, with their sections in `annotations/left_out.md`. They hold 176 ground truth triples, and the record "Privacy Preserving Distributed Data Mining" states no facts. The model coined five more held-out-only predicates (HAS_CHANGE, HAS_PARTNER, ORBIT_LOWERING_BEGAN_ON, HAS_ORBIT_LOWERING and ROUGHLY_PROPORTIONAL_TO) and used LOCATED_AT again. Here too, the cases that no rule decided were annotated by the closest rule and listed for a later round of rule fixes, and none changed a rule. These annotations are preliminary as well, still to be reviewed by a person. Later that day, 10 more held-out records were annotated the same way, 30 in all: the next 10 of the held-out part in pool order (pool positions 68, 71, 74, 77, 80, 83, 86, 89, 92 and 95), in `annotations/ground_truth/batch_008.csv`, with their sections in `annotations/left_out.md`. They hold 188 ground truth triples, and the 30 held-out records hold 522, 24 of them with held-out-only entries. The model coined two more held-out-only predicates, HAS_ENERGY_RANGE and DISPLAYS. As before, the cases that no rule decided were annotated by the closest rule and listed for a later round of rule fixes, and none changed a rule. These annotations are preliminary too, still to be reviewed by a person. On the same day, 20 more tuning records were annotated the same way, with the rules frozen, so the tuning part has 40 finished records in all: the next 20 of the tuning part in pool order, pool positions 27, 28, 30, 31, 33, 34, 36, 37, 39 and 40 in `annotations/ground_truth/batch_009.csv` (127 ground truth triples) and pool positions 42, 43, 45, 46, 48, 49, 51, 52, 54 and 55 in `annotations/ground_truth/batch_010.csv` (173 ground truth triples), with their sections in `annotations/left_out.md`. The 40 finished tuning records hold 732 ground truth triples. Later that day, 10 more held-out records were annotated the same way, 40 in all: the next 10 of the held-out part in pool order (pool positions 98, 101, 104, 107, 110, 113, 116, 119, 122 and 125), in `annotations/ground_truth/batch_011.csv`, with their sections in `annotations/left_out.md`. They hold 232 ground truth triples, and the 40 held-out records hold 754, 31 of them with held-out-only entries. The model coined one more held-out-only entity class, Taxon, and one more held-out-only predicate, FROM_CAMPAIGN, and used CollectionStatus, HAS_COLLECTION_STATUS, LOCATED_AT, USES_PLATFORM and OBTAINED_FROM again, whose `source:` lines now name these records too. Three of the records repeat the text of finished records, and their decisions are copied: pool position 101 repeats pool position 39, 119 repeats 35, and 125 joins the texts of 17 and 37. As before, the cases that no rule decided were annotated by the closest rule and listed for a later round of rule fixes, and none changed a rule. These annotations are preliminary too, still to be reviewed by a person. Later that day, nine facts of four finished tuning records (pool positions 40, 49, 51 and 54) that had been left out only because the one entry fitting them was marked held-out only became ground truth triples, since the mark keeps out only entries that held-out records alone motivate: LOCATED_AT, USES_PLATFORM, HAS_COLLECTION_STATUS, CollectionStatus and HAS_ENERGY_RANGE lost the mark, their `source:` lines and examples now come from those tuning records only, and the entity class Place was coined for the places of pool position 40. The annotation tool's *Unmark* keeps the held-out positions in the entry's `source:` line on purpose, so that step 070 asks for the definition, written from a held-out record, to be read again before it reaches every model; here the definitions and their examples were read again, the two examples that quoted held-out records were replaced, and the held-out positions were then removed by hand. A check of the last audits' findings of a ground truth triple that breaks a rule found one not yet corrected: "Aqua" FLIES_IN "Earth Observation Satellites (EOS)" (record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0"), since the record says that Aqua is one of those satellites, not that they fly as a formation of that name. It was deleted, and left_out.md records the membership as an "is a" fact (rule 4), an open case.
- **A fact counts wherever its words are, even inside a name (decided on 2026-11-12; done, the added triples to review).** Rule 17's *A name's facts* (*Writing a classed triple*) said how to quote a fact that a name states by its words alone, and no rule said which such facts count. The ground truth was uneven: it had "GRaND plus Z boron loaded sensor" PART_OF "GRaND", "GRaND epithermal neutron map" ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT "GRaND" and "Sentinel-1 radar observations" USES_TECHNIQUE "radar", and it lacked "radiance data" HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "radiance", the mission phase of "Tempel 1 encounter data" and the "map" of "GRaND epithermal neutron map". I rejected a closed list of kinds. The one test: every fact a name's words state is a fact (rule 2 of *What counts as a fact*: "Every fact the record states, unless a rule leaves it out"), the name stays whole (rule 11: "A name stays whole"), and the source text is the name as written (rule 17, *A name's facts*). Every other rule applies to such a fact as to any other: rule 1 (an unexplained code, nothing known from elsewhere), rule 9, rule 16, rule 21, and rule 19's list, which decides what is taken out of an instance and not whether a fact counts.
  - Rules text (`annotations/guidelines.md`): rule 2 has a new part, *A fact inside a name*, the one home of the test, with four examples ("radiance data"; "NASA Ocean Biology Processing Group (OBPG)" PART_OF "NASA"; "OSIRIS Narrow Angle Camera", where the unexplained "OSIRIS" gives nothing; "NASA/NOAA Suomi National Polar-orbiting Partnership (Suomi NPP)", where the words don't say how the agencies and the platform are related). Rule 11, rule 17's *A name's facts* and rule 19's *What stays in the instance* point to it and don't restate it; rule 19 now says that staying in the instance decides only how the instance is written, with "Argentina's space agency" as its example. Rule 2's list of the rules that leave a fact out never named rule 11 or rule 19, so it is unchanged. The model's copy (`common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`) has the same four changes with invented examples, so the extraction prompt's fingerprint changes (the prompts of 040, 050 and 060 all include the rules). `tests/allowed_differences.txt` needs no new line: both copies decide the same.
  - Ground truth: all 20 finished records read again against the test, every instance and every name of the title and the notes. 15 ground truth triples added, none changed or deleted (finished records 416 → 431 ground truth triples). Each is a draft by a model that applied the test (Claude Fable 5.2; 12 of the 15 were first proposed by the second opinion, `gemini-4-argon`, and name it in `drafted_by`; the other 3 name `claude-fable-5.2`), preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person. Record "NASA Standards and and Technical Assistance Resource Tool": "NASA developed standards" DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY "NASA"; "NASA developed handbooks" DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY "NASA"; "NASA Technical Standards Program" PART_OF "NASA". Record "MODIS/Aqua Snow Cover 5-Min L2 Swath 500m V061": "radiance data" HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "radiance". Record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005": "Comisión Nacional de Actividades Espaciales (CONAE)" BASED_IN "Argentina" (from "Argentina's space agency", which stays whole as the Role). Record "DAWN GRAND MAP VESTA FAST NEUTRON RESIDUAL V1.0": "GRaND epithermal neutron map" HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION "map". Record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006": "MOD11_L2" HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "L2". Record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0": "encounter data" HAS_MISSION_PHASE "encounter" (source text "Tempel 1 encounter data"). Record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3": "NASA HEASARC" PART_OF "NASA". A drafted "SDSS Quasar Catalog" HAS_TARGET "Quasar" was not kept then, for the reason that "Quasar" names a kind of body, not a specific one. That reason was wrong, since the ground truth schema's CelestialBody is "a natural object in space, or a kind of such objects", and the triple was restored the same day (*Fourth round of rule fixes*, above). Record "Solar Orbiter Ephemeris, Heliocentric Trajectories, Heliographic, Heliographic Inertial, and Solar Ecliptic Coordinates, HelioWeb, Daily Data": "original trajectory data" HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "trajectory". Record "BARREL 4G Rate Counter (RCNT) NaI Scintillator Diagnostics, Level 2, 4 s Data": "NASA Living with a Star" PART_OF "NASA". Record "CH1-ORB MOON M3 2 L0 RAW NEAR-IR SPECTRAL IMAGES V1.0": "Moon Mineralogy Mapper (M3)" HAS_TARGET "Moon". Record "OPERA Dynamic Surface Water Extent from Sentinel-1 (Version 1)": "U.S. Government" BASED_IN "U.S.". The rows were inserted next to the related rows of their record with the annotation tool's CSV writer; the files differ from before by the 15 added lines only. `annotations/rules_by_triple.md` is generated again.
  - `annotations/left_out.md`: two items deleted, since their facts are ground truth triples now ("L2" inside "MOD11_L2"; "U.S." inside "U.S. Government"). Three items keep their statement left out and no longer rest on "A name stays whole": "NASA/NOAA" (rule 1: the words don't say how the agencies and the platform are related), "South African" (rule 9: the record names no country) and "NaI Scintillator" (rule 1: that it is an instrument would come from outside the record).

Eight of the 50 facts of 2026-11-02 are decided this way; 42 stay undecided.

Four decisions of `annotations/second_opinion_candidates.csv` still cite rule 11 for such a fact (the comet, "Global", "epithermal neutron", the Association) and wait on these.

No reported number changes until the runs are scored again: the ground truth changed, so a free re-score of the six runs follows (`py 070_evaluate/run.py --suggest false` after each `py 060_extract/run.py --repeat N`), and the changed rules change 060's prompt, so a new extraction is a new recipe. From: my decision of 2026-11-12.
- **The rules each ground truth triple uses, recorded per triple (added on 2026-11-11; the values to review).** Every `annotations/ground_truth/batch_*.csv` has a last column `rules`: the numbers of the rules of `annotations/guidelines.md` the ground truth triple uses, ascending (`1, 19`); the rules' words stay in the guidelines alone. The annotation tool keeps the column, shows each triple's rules with the rule's words on a click, and adds or removes numbers; `py helpers/rules_by_triple.py`, and the tool on each save, write `annotations/rules_by_triple.md`, one section per record in `left_out.md`'s order with each rule quoted once per record, and `tests/test_rules_by_triple.py` fails when the doc or a value is out of step. Rules 2, 11, 13, 14 and 17 apply to every ground truth triple and aren't recorded; a describes triple follows the guidelines' section *The describes triple* instead. The values are drafts by a model (Claude Fable 5.1, 2026-11-11) for the 426 ground truth triples of the 21 records annotated so far, which I review in the tool record by record; until then the doc says they are preliminary. The use: when a rule's wording changes, the ground truth triples that cite it are the ones to check again. The same drafting pass also checked every ground truth triple against all the rules, not only its own: 78 ground truth triples may break a rule, and 20 statements of the records' text may be facts with neither a ground truth triple nor an item in `annotations/left_out.md` (rule 2). Both lists are in `docs/analysis/rules_audit.csv` (one row each, with the rule's words; preliminary, still to be reviewed by a person). Some point at the guidelines rather than the ground truth. The first, decided on 2026-11-12: rule 10 left out "current", but rule 22's example keeps "heritage and current NASA sensors" whole; rule 11 now states once, for every word that rules 5, 9, 10 or 19 take out, that it stays when the record doesn't state the fact without it (the test rules 9 and 19 each stated for their own words before), in `annotations/guidelines.md` and in the model's copy (`common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`, with an invented example), so the extraction prompt's fingerprint changes. Six more such conflicts are to be decided, and six ground truth triples that the rules as written say should change are mine to change. To do: I check each row against its record, fix the source texts and `left_out.md` notes myself, and decide the rest one by one; no reported number changes until the runs are scored again.
- **A second opinion on every finished record (done on 2026-11-01).** Many ground truth triples were first proposed by a model of the same family as the extraction model and carried no `drafted_by` (they name `claude-opus-5.5` since 2026-11-01: *Every ground truth row names its drafting model*, below); the rest were drafted by `gemini-2.5-pro` or written by hand. Whoever drafted a record, the facts the drafter missed were added by one person alone, and a person correcting a draft tends to keep what is there, so recall is likely overstated (`070_evaluate/metrics/recall.md`, *Assumes and can't see*). The field's practice is a second annotator from another source, whose annotation shows what the first missed: Tan et al., "Revisiting DocRED – Addressing the False Negative Problem in Relation Extraction", EMNLP 2022, re-annotated a benchmark and found its labels incomplete. Now `py helpers/second_opinion.py` has another model (`gemini-4-argon` by default) draft every finished record again, with 050's prompt and checks, matches each ground truth triple against that draft with 070's pairing, lists the draft's facts the ground truth lacks (tuning records only) for review, and with `--apply true` records, per model, in how many repeats it found each triple in `annotations/second_opinion.csv`; step 070 then reports recall and partial F1 for the triples found in at least one repeat and for those found in none (`helpers/second_opinion.md`). Three repeats (decided on 2026-11-01): one draft is one answer of the model, which may state a fact on one answer and leave it out on the next, so a triple one draft misses may be a miss of that answer alone. `--repeats` (3 by default; first named `--runs`, renamed the same day, since a run is one execution of a step and these are repeats within one) drafts every record as the repeats 1 to 3 of the recipe in one command, asks once before paying for all of them, and records `found_in_repeats` of `repeats` per ground truth triple and model; a triple counts as found when at least one repeat found it, and 070 also shows how many triples were found in 0, 1, 2 or 3 repeats. Why 3: the fewest repeats that tell a triple found every time from one found now and then, at 3 times the cost of one, and the same number of repeats as the baseline (`docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *Repeats: how much the numbers vary on their own*). Not done: changing `drafted_by` on rows with none (whether to write the model that first proposed them waits on a decision); a vote across several second opinions. From: the decision of 2026-11-01.
- **The second opinion's facts to review, in Git (done on 2026-11-01).** The draft's facts the ground truth lacks (tuning records only, the union over the repeats) were only in `outputs/intermediate_results/second_opinion/second_opinion_matches.csv`, which is never committed, so the run on the NASA laptop left them out of reach for review. Now `py helpers/second_opinion.py --apply true` also writes them to `annotations/second_opinion_candidates.csv`, their one home in Git: one row per classed triple and model (`id`, `title`, the classed triple, `source_text`, `model`, `proposed_in_repeats`, `repeats`) and an empty column `decision` that I fill when I review the row against the record and the rules: `added`, `left out (<section>, rule <n>)` (the rule named as `annotations/guidelines.md` writes it, checked against it), or `already in the ground truth in other words`; any other value stops the reading, naming the line. Held-out records' are never written, only counted. A rerun of apply replaces the model's own rows on the records drafted again and keeps every decision already filled for the same record and classed triple, also a decided row the rerun no longer proposes, so no decision is lost. The commands after the drafting run: `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *A second opinion on the ground truth*; the file: `helpers/second_opinion.md`, *The draft's facts to review*. Reviewed on 2026-11-01 (next entry). From: the decision of 2026-11-01.
- **The second opinion's results, and its facts reviewed (decided on 2026-11-01).** `py helpers/second_opinion.py --apply true` with `gemini-4-argon` and 3 repeats, on the 20 finished tuning records: 358 of the 412 ground truth triples were found in at least one of the 3 repeats (`annotations/second_opinion.csv`). I reviewed the draft's 150 facts the ground truth lacked (`annotations/second_opinion_candidates.csv`, its column `decision`) one by one against the record and the rules: 75 are already in the ground truth in other words, 70 are left out by a rule, and 5 are added (four judgment calls, below). No systematic miss of the ground truth shows.
  - The pattern: the 75 are ground truth facts under another subject, wording or entity class (the record's dataset in place of its part or its variable; "NSST" for "skin SST"; PhysicalQuantity for Variable). What the draft states beyond the ground truth comes in three kinds, each left out by a rule: facts read off the inside of a name (PART_OF "NASA" from "NASA HEASARC", MAPPED_INTO "SIN" from "SIN Grid", "OSIRIS" from "OSIRIS Narrow Angle Camera", "NaI Scintillator" from a title), which rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple* keeps whole; facts of other datasets in the record (the VNP43D family, the BRDF/Albedo product, MCD19A1N), rule 21 of *Writing a classed triple*; and title codes ("4G", "CH1-ORB", "Tail 655", "L2G", "67P/CAL"), rule 1 of *What counts as a fact*. The two facts worth adding outright (B and D) are cases of one record each.
  - Left out, by the rule named in `decision`: *What counts as a fact*, rule 1: 13; rule 2 (waits on Q8 (c)): 5; rule 4: 4; rule 7: 2; rule 9: 2. *Writing a classed triple*, rule 11: 15; rule 15: 3; rule 16: 8; rule 18: 1; rule 19: 7; rule 21: 9; rule 24: 1.
  - The four judgment calls, decided as the review recommended (my decisions). A, the record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0": "from the entire comet encounter" is set off by commas right after "the EDR (raw) sensor data", so the phase is theirs: "RDR data" (Dataset) HAS_MISSION_PHASE "encounter" (MissionPhase) is now "EDR (raw) sensor data" (Dataset) HAS_MISSION_PHASE "encounter" (MissionPhase), source text "the EDR (raw) sensor data, from the entire comet encounter". B, the record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0": the title's "67P/CAL" is a "/" list whose item "67P" the notes name as "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko" (rule 22 of *Writing a classed triple*, *Lists joined by "/" or "+"*), so the record's dataset HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko" (CelestialBody), with the title's "67P" as source text (rule 17, *A title's facts*), as the title's "9P/TEMPEL 1" gives in the STARDUST record; the phase keeps its own target. C, the record "VIIRS/NPP BRDF/Albedo Parameter 3 Band M4 Daily L3 Global 30 ArcSec CMG V002": "the nine VIIRS moderate resolution bands … included in the VNP43MA1 product" are that product's bands, so no VIIRS HAS_NUMBER_OF "nine VIIRS moderate resolution bands" (left out, rule 21 of *Writing a classed triple*; considered and not changed). D, the record "BARREL 4G Rate Counter (RCNT) NaI Scintillator Diagnostics, Level 2, 4 s Data": "the website maintained by A.J. Halford" is a site the record names in words, a Document (rule 20, *A page named, or given as an address*), like its two siblings in the same list ("Millan et al. - **The second opinion's 50 new facts to review (from 2026-11-02; after the TM).** On 2026-11-02 the second opinion drafted all 20 finished records again, because the drafting prompt's ground truth schema had gained two patterns, and `py helpers/second_opinion.py --apply true` then added 50 draft-only facts to `annotations/second_opinion_candidates.csv` with an empty `decision` (200 rows; the 150 decided on 2026-11-01 are unchanged). They are undecided. I review them after the TM, one by one against the record and the rules, as the first 150. Any fact added changes the ground truth, so a free re-score of the six runs follows (`py 070_evaluate/run.py --suggest false` after each `py 060_extract/run.py --repeat N`). The TM's limitations name them.
- **A blind [second judge](terminology.md#4-ground-truth-and-samples) of my decisions (2026-11-02; built, to run on the NASA laptop).** My decisions on the second opinion's 150 facts and my verdicts on the 62 partial pairs of the baseline's repeats are one rater's, and they judge what two models wrote; one rater may lean the way of the output judged, and a model asked to judge favors output like its own (self-preference). Now `py helpers/second_judge.py` asks a model of a third maker (`gpt-6-astra`, OpenAI's; fallback `gpt-6.1-sol`) to decide each row again, blind to my decision (a test builds each prompt under every label and finds one), with everything the decision was made from: for the facts, the record's text, the candidate and its source text, `annotations/guidelines.md` and the ground truth schema, and the record's ground truth triples as they were before my decisions changed them (`--ground_truth_at 13a71b1`); for the partial pairs, the record's text, the two triples and the definitions of `070_evaluate/metrics/pairs.md`. Only rows with my label are judged, so the judge's label can't shape mine. Its labels go to `annotations/second_judge.csv`; the report gives per task the raw agreement, the agreement per label, Cohen's kappa κ = (*p*o − *p*e) ÷ (1 − *p*e) with raw agreement beside it for the prevalence effect, and every disagreement (to read with me). All 62 partial pair verdicts of 2026-11-02 were `same fact` (κ = 0 by construction on them alone); the 13 of the final re-score added 7 `not the same fact`. The GPT-6 models' settings were added to `common/common_helpers/llm.py`, each from OpenAI's docs: no temperature, `reasoning_effort`, `max_completion_tokens`. Fixed on 2026-11-03: the partial pairs task read only `annotations/partial_pairs_to_review.csv`, so after `py helpers/partial_pairs.py --apply true` had moved every verdict into `annotations/partial_pair_reviews.csv` the first laptop run found 0 partial pairs to judge; it now judges each verdict in either file, once per pair, and stops on two different verdicts for one pair (`tests/test_second_judge.py`, the moved verdicts). The judge through Ask Sage is `gpt-5.6-sol-gov`. Partly run on 2026-11-03 through Ask Sage: 83 of the 150 facts were judged before the account reached its monthly token limit (findings.md), with raw agreement 51% and κ = 0.21 on those 83, which were not chosen at random (the run stopped where the limit fell); it would add 18 facts, which are under review; the other 67 facts and the partial pairs were not judged. Left out of the TM on 2026-11-05 (my decision) because it is incomplete; the TM keeps only a future-work line for an independent check of my decisions. To do: finish it (`docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *A second judge of my decisions*), then read the disagreements and record the numbers in `docs/findings.md`. Home: `helpers/second_judge.md`; tests: `tests/test_second_judge.py`. From: Artstein & Poesio, "Inter-Coder Agreement for Computational Linguistics", Computational Linguistics 34(4), 2008 ([paper](https://aclanthology.org/J08-4004/)).

Left as they were: the describes triples (code writes them, and step 070 compares a record's describes triple apart, without `drafted_by`; 6 keep an empty one) and records with only an id (there are none). The finished records' classed triples by `drafted_by`, describes triples left out, before → after: `claude-opus-5.5` 126 → 263, `gemini-2.5-pro` 149 → 149, `gemini-4-argon` 4 → 4, empty 137 → 0 (416 in all). So step 070's *By who drafted the ground truth* has no empty group for these files (should a row a person adds leave `drafted_by` empty, its label is "no drafting model recorded"), and step 060's warning when it extracts with `claude-opus-5-5` (the same model, spelled as the service also accepts it) now covers every record that has such rows: 18 of the 20 finished records, 13 before.
