- **Gemini 2.5 Pro and error 500:** in a test with an answer limit of 20 tokens it answered error 500 ("no choices"), probably its thinking using up the limit. Watch for it after the next 050 runs: an HTTP 500 "no choices" now lands in the failed-call record with its HTTP status and, when the service reports them, its thinking tokens; the thinking cap (default `low`) should prevent it if thinking was the cause. Checked on 2026-10-31 (research F-1): Google's docs say the output limit includes the thinking (`max_output_tokens` "sets the maximum number of tokens a response can generate, including thought tokens", [Gemini thinking](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/thinking)), and Gemini 2.5 Pro's thinking can't be turned off ([LiteLLM's Gemini page](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/providers/gemini)), so a 20-token limit leaves no room for an answer. The mechanism is documented; that it caused this 500 is still not confirmed from a log. To confirm it, ask again with a limit above Gemini 2.5 Pro's smallest thinking budget, 128 tokens (strong secondary: the Vertex AI thinking docs, through a search extract). From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-12.
- **A drafted record cut off at the answer length limit, what remains:** draft record d5477563-0415-4e65-ab7c-d4bc9ed809e5 (pool position 7) again with 050's default `--thinking medium` (since 2026-10-30, with the answer limit raised to 32,000 tokens: decided on 2026-10-30, from the review of the prompts; Google's Gemini docs recommend more thinking for complex tasks, and through LiteLLM `medium` is a thinking budget of 2,048 tokens for Gemini 2.5 Pro, `low` 1,024), to check it now fits (the cause and the guards added: [`findings.md`](findings.md#2026-10-21--step-050--why-a-drafted-answer-was-cut-off), 2026-10-21; [`running_on_nasa_laptop.md`](running_on_nasa_laptop.md), section 5). Then the thinking cap's trade-off: less thinking may make the answers worse, and I haven't measured it. To test it: run the same records with `--thinking low`, `medium`, and `high` (or `model_default` with a higher `MAX_ANSWER_TOKENS`) and compare 070's scores; each report records the setting used. From: 2026-10-21; 2026-10-22.
- **Mission or spacecraft, when they share a name (open; to settle with more records):** NASA often gives a mission and its spacecraft the same name (Dawn, Rosetta, Aqua), and a record may name it without saying which. In record #5 (pool position 5), the title "DAWN GRAND MAP VESTA FAST NEUTRON RESIDUAL V1.0" names Dawn only as the first word. Planetary (PDS) titles follow NASA's [PDS3 Standards Reference, Chapter 6](https://pds.nasa.gov/datastandards/pds3/standards/sr/Chapter06.pdf), which builds a dataset's name from up to seven components, starting with the instrument host: the platform carrying the instrument, usually a spacecraft. So the first word is a good clue for the Spacecraft entity class, as with record #1's "ROSETTA-ORBITER". Its limits: the instrument host can also be Earth-based (a ground observatory) or a rover, or, in PDS4, a lander (corrected on 2026-10-31: the PDS3 data dictionary's standard values of INSTRUMENT_HOST_TYPE are DATA BASE, EARTH BASED, N/A, ROVER, SPACECRAFT and UNK, with no campaign, [PSDD rev. F](https://pds.nasa.gov/data/pds4/documents/document_pds3_standards/psdd_rev_F_20080611.pdf); until then this said "a ground observatory or an observing campaign"); the order of the other components isn't reliable (record #5's title puts the instrument before the target); and Earth-science titles follow other conventions. So it is a clue for the annotator's choice of entity class, not a rule. The graph side (keep same-named Mission and Spacecraft nodes apart) is in step 080's item on joining instances. From: 2026-10-22.
- **SpatialRepresentation: a closed list, or the record's own words? (open, waiting on experts: [`annotations/questions_for_experts.md`, Q1](../annotations/questions_for_experts.md#q1-should-spatialrepresentation-be-a-closed-list))** The tuning records' instances are "swath" (#2), "gridded" (#3; "Polar-Gridded" until 2026-10-31), "spatially gridded" (#4), "map" (#5) and "gridded" (#6). If the experts confirm a closed list, I add a rule (a new exception to rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple*, like its rules 19, 22, 23) making the list's value the instance, change #4 to "gridded" and #5 too (checked on 2026-10-31: #5's map is a table of fixed latitude-longitude cells, see Q1's My notes), and update the model's copy of the rules (060's next run re-asks the model). Until then #4 stays "spatially gridded": rules 11 and 19 of *Writing a classed triple* ("the record's own words, as written"; "A qualifier stays in the instance, as written, if it is any other kind"); it already forms a partial pair with "gridded". From: 2026-10-24.
- **Rule 19 can lose which fact a qualifier went with (decided on 2026-10-30: option 2).** Rule 19 of *Writing a classed triple* (annotations/guidelines.md): "A qualifier that is a temporal resolution, an area the data cover, a processing level, a spatial resolution, a country, a time of day, or a target body is a ground truth triple of its own, and is taken out of the instance." Setup: a ground truth triple S P O, where the object instance O contains a qualifier q ("Earth"). Rule 19 takes q out of O and writes a new ground truth triple S Q q (e.g. X HAS_TARGET "Earth"). S, O and O′ are subject instances or object instances; q becomes the object instance of the new ground truth triple; P, P′ and Q are predicates of the ground truth schema. The problem: S Q q says q is about S, but in the record q was only about O. That goes wrong when S has another ground truth triple S P′ O′ whose object instance O′ did not contain q. Two forms, depending on O′:

  | Form | O′ contains | Result | What's lost |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | A | another qualifier q′ of the same kind ("Mars") | S Q q and S Q q′ | which of q and q′ goes with which object instance |
  | B | no qualifier of that kind ("solar wind") | S Q q only | that q does not go with O′ |

  No problem when every other object instance of S that a qualifier of this kind could describe also contained q (Aqua: "study the global dynamics of the Earth atmosphere, land and oceans": "Earth" goes with all three). *Suppose:* a record says X was launched "to study the Earth atmosphere and the Mars surface". *Then:* after rule 19, X HAS_TARGET "Earth" and X HAS_TARGET "Mars", and nothing says which goes with which purpose (form A). *Suppose:* a record says X was launched "to study the Earth atmosphere and the solar wind". *Then:* X HAS_TARGET "Earth" reads as covering the solar wind too (form B). Measured on 2026-10-27 over the 13 annotated records: rule 19 took 19 qualifiers out of an instance (20 more ground truth triples of a qualifier's kind are stated directly about the record's dataset). 14 lose nothing; 1 is form A (record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0": "Level 2/3 (EDR/RDR) data" gave the record's dataset HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 2 (EDR)" and "Level 3 (RDR)", while "EDR (raw) sensor data" and "RDR data" are PART_OF the record's dataset, and nothing pairs them); 2 are possibly form B (record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006": "daytime" and "nighttime" came from the surface temperature bands but read as covering the emissivities too; the Stardust record's HAS_TARGET "9P/Tempel 1" came from the encounter phase but reads as covering the cruise phase too, though the title names the comet for the whole dataset). Measured again on 2026-10-30, after audit finding R2 (rule 19 now says whose fact a qualifier becomes: the instance it was in, when that instance is the subject of a ground truth triple of its own; otherwise the subject of the ground truth triple whose object the instance was): rule 19 takes 20 qualifiers out of an instance (the 19 above, and "Tempel 1" in "The Tempel 1 encounter data", whose ground truth triple was missing); 0 are form A (the Stardust levels now have the subject instances "EDR (raw) sensor data" and "RDR data", so the ground truth says which data are at which level); 2 are possibly form B, the same two. The annotation tool's several_qualifiers flag now marks 2 subject instances (the land surface temperature record's "daytime" and "nighttime", and "skin SST"'s "day" and "night", which lose nothing), not 3. Form A can be found by code (two values of one kind on one subject instance), though two values are not always a loss (a dataset observed at daytime and at nighttime); form B needs a person reading the text. Options: (1) keep the qualifier in the instance when moving it would lose something (rejected: whether a qualifier stays would depend on the record's other facts, so the same qualifier is in some instances and out of others); (2) rule 19 as it is, always, accepting the measured loss, with checks flagging form A for a person; (3) a node of its own for each such fact (the W3C's pattern for a fact with more than two parts, in "Defining N-ary Relations on the Semantic Web" ([paper](https://www.w3.org/TR/swbp-n-aryRelations/)), Noy & Rector, 2006): nothing lost, but new predicates, more ground truth triples, and nodes with no words from the record, which step 070 cannot pair by their words; (4) the qualifier stays attached to the ground truth triple it came from, as an extra part of that triple, not a ground truth triple of its own (as Wikidata's statement qualifiers ([Help:Qualifiers](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Help:Qualifiers)) and a graph database's properties on a relationship do): nothing lost, every instance still in the record's words, and step 070 still pairs ground truth triples as now, with a second score for the qualifiers (as in HyperRED, Chia et al., 2022, "A Dataset for Hyper-Relational Extraction and a Cube-Filling Approach" ([paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.10018)), where a fact counts only if its triple and its qualifier both match); it changes the ground truth files, the annotation tool, the rules, extraction's output and 070. Option 2 was recommended on 2026-10-27, because my NASA model access ends on 2026-11-20, but not decided then: that day I rejected splitting Aqua's purpose into three (it made the facts depend on each other), and called rule 22's test, whether "each item alone is a complete object", a judgment call. Decided on 2026-10-30: option 2, rule 19 as written, always; rule 22 splits every list joined by "and", after rule 19, with the "complete object" condition dropped (the dependence I saw was on "Earth", which rule 19 takes out of every purpose anyway). Form A is flagged for a person by the annotation tool's check `several_qualifiers` (050_annotate/050_annotate.md, *Checks on each row*); form B needs a person reading the record. Option 4 is future work; the loss it would avoid is a limitation I document. The measurements and options above stay as the record of why. Done on 2026-10-30: annotations/guidelines.md (rule 22 and its Aqua example), the model's copy of the rules (common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt), and the finished records (pool position 4: Aqua HAS_TARGET "Earth", and three HAS_PURPOSE in place of one). Three lists joined by "and" in finished records were left whole, because splitting each may not give true facts. Decided on 2026-10-30: the record "NASA Standards and and Technical Assistance Resource Tool"'s HAS_PURPOSE "make it simpler for technical people to access NASA technical information quickly and conveniently" (pool position 0) is split into "… quickly" and "… conveniently" (each is true alone). Decided on 2026-10-30: the record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0"'s "modified nonlinear SST algorithm" HAS_PURPOSE "provide continuity of SST derived from heritage and current NASA sensors" (continuity needs both; pool position 4) stays whole, by a collective-"and" exception added to rule 22: a list is kept whole when it is what one of five nouns is about, "continuity", "ratio", "difference", "comparison" or "correlation" (the list follows the noun, as in "continuity of … A and B" or "difference between A and B"), not when the noun is part of a name; the list of nouns was closed, and a new noun was added only by a decision recorded here (replaced later on 2026-10-30 by the definition of a collective "and", with the five nouns as examples: see *Audit of the rules and the ground truth*). A first test, approved earlier the same day, kept a list whole whenever one of seven words ("between", "continuity", "combined", "merged", "ratio", "difference", "compared") was in the phrase; it was never written, because it would also keep whole three lists that are split correctly in finished records ("Normalized Difference Snow Index (NDSI) and a series of screens", where "Difference" is part of a name; "developed collaboratively between … (NASA) and … (CONAE)", where each agency developed it; "Combined Terra and Aqua", where the data come from each spacecraft). Checked on 2026-10-30 over the 13 finished records: the five nouns occur three times ("continuity of SST derived from heritage and current NASA sensors"; "Normalized Difference Snow Index", a name; "anti-correlation of the variability with X-ray luminosity", with no list), so the exception keeps whole only the continuity case. Decided on 2026-10-30: rule 22 covers a list in the subject too ("Then put one item alone in the list's place (the subject or the object),"), so that record's subject instance "Peter Minnett and his team" (Team), in four ground truth triples, is split, and the item "his team" is dropped by What counts as a fact, rule 9 ("A ground truth triple needs a subject and an object the record names."): the four ground truth triples have the subject instance "Peter Minnett" (Person). The price: the team's share of the affiliation and the responsibilities is lost (kept in annotations/left_out.md). No other finished record has a list in a subject instance. Then the ground truth schema's entity class Team ("a group of people without a name of its own, e.g. Peter Minnett and his team") had no instance left, and was removed on 2026-10-30: a named group is an Organization, and an unnamed group names no specific thing, so it gives no fact (What counts as a fact, rule 9), so Team could have no instance. Task, Role, SPONSORED_BY_PARTY, DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY and LAUNCHED_BY_PARTY now name only a Person or Organization; the translation table's row for the current schema's Team translates to (none), unchecked, and its rows for Task, CREATED_BY and CREATED are to be checked again, since the definitions they translate to changed. From: 2026-10-27; 2026-10-30.
- **Held-out records may coin entries, marked held-out only (decided on 2026-11-01; done).** Until then the ground truth schema was frozen for held-out records, and a fact no entry fitted was left out of their ground truth: a miss of the pipeline there would have been hidden (rule 2 of *What counts as a fact*: "nothing else catches the omission"). Now a held-out record is annotated as a tuning one, every fact, and what it coins is a [held-out-only entry](terminology.md#3-schemas): in the file, never shown to a model, never a target of the translation table. Unmarking (making it a normal entry) is allowed only when a tuning record needs the entry, and the entry's source then names that tuning record (my decision of 2026-11-01: the definition's wording is the only thing that reaches the models, and it is read again then; the held-out records keep using the entry). To do: re-check the held-out records annotated from 2026-10-26 to 2026-11-01 for facts left out only because no entry fitted (`annotations/left_out.md`), and coin held-out-only entries for them. The earlier item, kept: **Re-check the records finished before the ground truth schema is frozen against it.** Since 2026-10-26 the ground truth schema is frozen for held-out records (annotations/guidelines.md, *Before you start*; the annotation tool refuses changes to it from them, and 070 notes an entry added from one). It still changes while I annotate tuning records (I coin entity classes and predicates, and redefine some), so a record finished early may use a component class whose definition changed later, or lack one coined later. The held-out records are, from pool position 6 on, every third record (8, 11, 14, …), between the tuning records. Each part is a fair sample on its own, so I can finish the tuning records first, then annotate the held-out records, and re-check every record finished before then against the ground truth schema as it then is. From: the literature check of 2026-10-26: Noy & McGuinness (2001), "Ontology Development 101: A Guide to Creating Your First Ontology" [a schema], Stanford Knowledge Systems Laboratory, KSL-01-05, for whom building an ontology [a schema] is a process of repeated passes; Pustejovsky & Stubbs (2012), *Natural Language Annotation for Machine Learning* (O'Reilly), whose MATTER cycle (model, annotate, train, test, evaluate, revise) revises the model [the schema] and the guidelines [annotation guidelines] in rounds of annotation.
- **More reviewers.** Suggested at my demo on 2026-10-27: (a) librarians of the NASA STI Program, who keep the NASA Thesaurus (see *Linking subject instances and object instances to reference lists*, under 080), for the classification side: the ground truth schema's entity classes and the lists of values ([`annotations/questions_for_experts.md`, Q1](../annotations/questions_for_experts.md#q1-should-spatialrepresentation-be-a-closed-list) and [Q21](../annotations/questions_for_experts.md#q21-should-instruments-spacecraft-missions-and-physical-quantities-follow-a-reference-list-and-what-should-the-knowledge-graph-answer)); (b) the people who maintain the data, for finer distinctions in the ground truth schema and for questions about one record. What the catalog gives for (b), measured on 2026-10-27 over the 36,406 harvested records (`outputs/intermediate_results/010_harvest`): `maintainer_email` is filled on every record (433 distinct addresses); `author_email` is empty on every record. About 65% of the records (23,589) give a shared help desk address, e.g. earthdata-support@nasa.gov on 10,651 records and pds-operator@jpl.nasa.gov on 6,214; the other 12,817 give a personal address, but one of those addresses covers 9,595 records, so a `maintainer_email` address is a contact for the catalog entry, not necessarily the person who made the dataset. 671 records' notes contain an email address. From: my demo, 2026-10-27.
- **Missing twin predicates in the ground truth schema.** Two predicates of the ground truth schema have no twin: HAS_SKY_AREA ("covers this much of the sky, each part counted once": the size of everything covered) has no ground twin, a predicate for the size of the ground area covered; and HAS_SPATIAL_RESOLUTION ("each of its values covers this much ground": the size of one value) has no sky twin, a predicate for the angle one value covers (e.g. "1.5 arcseconds"). No finished record needs either yet. When one does, decide whether to coin the twin or to broaden the existing definition to cover both ground and sky. Two more, found by the second audit (finding S9, 2026-10-31): ENDED_ON ("came to an end on") has no start twin (a predicate for when something began, other than LAUNCHED_ON); and PROVIDES_ACCESS_TO_DOCUMENT ("makes this Document available") covers Documents only, so a service a party provides had no predicate until PROVIDED_BY_PARTY was coined (below), which reads the other way (the thing provided, then the party). A third, found on 2026-10-31: HAS_GRANULE_DURATION ("each of its files covers this much time") has no ground twin, a predicate for the ground each file covers; the record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006" says its data are "in a 1,200 by 1,200 km grid", which NASA LAADS shows is one tile, the ground each file covers, but the record doesn't say so, so it stays left out (`annotations/left_out.md`). From: 2026-10-30; 2026-10-31.
- **HAS_SKY_AREA counts each part once (decided on 2026-10-30; done).** The definition of HAS_SKY_AREA changed from "covers this much of the sky" to "covers this much of the sky, each part counted once", so that it is plainly different from HAS_TOTAL_FIELD_AREA ("the areas of its parts add up to this, counting overlaps each time"; until 2026-10-30, "of its fields", a word from astronomy, replaced by the general "parts" after the generality check); the record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3" gives both ("~ 504 deg^2" and "~ 814 deg^2"). HAS_TOTAL_FIELD_AREA was renamed HAS_SUMMED_SKY_AREA on 2026-10-31, with a parallel definition (below). From: 2026-10-30.

- **Audit of the rules and the ground truth (2026-10-30; its findings are decided one at a time).** A read-only audit of `annotations/guidelines.md`, the ground truth schema's definitions, the model's copy of the rules (`common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`) and the 13 finished records found 16 findings on the rules and 11 on the ground truth. Decided so far:
  - R1 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 22 of *Writing a classed triple* split every list joined by "and", so a range such as "between 2014-11-19 and 2015-03-10" would have become two false facts, while the ground truth schema and the ground truth keep a range as one value. Rule 22 now says "A range is one item, not a list", and the collective "and" exception points to it; the model's copy of the rules says the same (its fingerprint changes). No ground truth triple changes: every range in the 13 records is already whole.
  - R2 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 19 of *Writing a classed triple* did not say whose fact a qualifier taken out of an instance becomes, and its own examples answered two ways (NASA BASED_IN "U.S."; the record's dataset HAS_TARGET "Vesta"). Rule 19 now says: "The new ground truth triple's subject is the instance the qualifier was in, when that instance is the subject of a ground truth triple of its own (NASA BASED_IN "U.S."; "pixels" HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "L2"). Otherwise it is the subject of the ground truth triple whose object the instance was (the dataset HAS_TARGET "Vesta")." The model's copy of the rules follows (its fingerprint changes). Ground truth changes, all in record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0" except the last: HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 2 (EDR)" has the subject instance "EDR (raw) sensor data" and "Level 3 (RDR)" has "RDR data", instead of the record's dataset (this removes the one form A loss of rule 19); "encounter data" HAS_TARGET "9P/Tempel 1" is added (finding G2: "The Tempel 1 encounter data" had its target taken out with no ground truth triple; "9P/Tempel 1" by rule 23); in record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0", HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko" has the subject instance "comet escort 1" instead of the record's dataset, since it came from "the 67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko comet escort 1 mission phase" and "comet escort 1" has a ground truth triple of its own (HAS_TIME_SPAN); the record's dataset no longer has a target in that record.
  - R3 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 12 of *Writing a classed triple* writes the record's dataset as its title, while rule 23 could pick another wording for it. Rule 23 now says "The record's dataset is the exception: it is always written as its title (rule 12)."; the model's copy follows. Checked: in all 13 records the describes triple's object is the title as written, and no other instance is a variant of the title.
  - R4 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple* listed three exceptions to "as written", but rules 5 and 10 of *What counts as a fact* also remove words. It now says "Exceptions: *What counts as a fact*, rules 5 and 10 (opinion words, and words true only as of writing, are left out); and rules 19, 22 and 23 below."; the model's copy follows.
  - R5 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 23 said nothing about wordings that differ only in spaces, hyphens or punctuation. It now says "A wording that differs only in capitals, spaces, hyphens or punctuation is the same wording, not a second one. Write it as it appears where the record isn't typed all in capitals (usually the notes); if it appears more than once there, the form used most often (on a tie, the first)." A first version ("Write the first one the record's text uses") was withdrawn: it contradicted the capitals rule ("Vesta", not the all-capitals title's "VESTA"). The model's copy now has these sentences too (they were in the guidelines only). Differences between records ("ROSETTA-ORBITER" in two Rosetta records, "Rosetta Orbiter" in the ALICE record) are not covered: joining them is the job of the 080 item on joining instances that name the same thing. Checked over the 13 records: no instance breaks the new wording, except four left as they are pending a decision, because the rule as written picks a title's Title Case form on a tie: "swath" (title "Swath"), "weekly" (title "Weekly") and "daily" (two titles "Daily").
  - R6 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 22's "in the singular" was tailored to its example. It now says "Each item is now one thing, so shared words that agree with the number of items are put in the singular to agree with one item ("channels" → "channel"; "are" → "is"); each item's own words stay as written ("oceans")."; the model's copy follows. Checked: every split list in the 13 records already follows it.
  - R8 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 17's source text, "the shortest passage that states the fact", now reads "the shortest passage that contains words for the subject, the predicate and the object; when the subject is named only in an earlier sentence, from where it is named to the end of the fact." The model's copy, terminology.md's *source text* and the annotation tool's subject_not_in_source and object_not_in_source messages follow. Ground truth change: record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3", 2XMMi-DR3 HAS_SKY_AREA "~ 504 deg^2": its source text now starts at "The total area of the catalog fields", the sentence's words for 2XMMi-DR3 (it was "the net sky area covered independently is ~ 504 deg^2", with no words for the subject).
  - R9 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 21's "include the fact of how the two datasets are connected" asked for a connection even when the record states none. It now says "include the connection the record states (rule 1 of *What counts as a fact*: only what the record states)"; the model's copy and step 060's two-pass prompt follow, and the quotes in left_out.md and questions_for_experts.md are updated. "Version 6.1" SUPERSEDES "Version 6" is not changed here (G4, to decide).
  - R11 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 1 of *What counts as a fact* now adds "This includes its entity class: an unexplained code gets no ground truth triple whose meaning depends on what it stands for."; the model's copy follows. Not changed, to decide: instances in finished records whose entity class depends on a code's meaning the record doesn't give, all from titles: "RSI" (Instrument; "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 …"), "DAWN" (Spacecraft), "STARDUST" (Spacecraft; the notes name the Stardust-NExT mission only), "L3" (ProcessingLevel; "MODIS/Terra … Daily L3 Global …"), and the title versions "V1.0", "V005", "V2019.0", whose entity class Version depends on reading "V" as version. Known in the unfinished record "ROSETTA-ORBITER PRELANDING OSINAC 2 EDR MTP003 V2.0": "MTP003" as a MissionPhase.
  - R12, R13, R14, R15 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 23's type-word sentence points to rule 4 of *What counts as a fact* instead of restating it; the first worked example now says "The two purposes ("… quickly", "… conveniently", rule 22) are written without "great"."; rule 1 of *What counts as a fact* now has the model's rule "The record may be one part of a longer record ("part 1 of 2"); state only what this part states."; rule 22 now says an item that names no specific thing gives no ground truth triple, pointing to "Not a fact without a specific thing".
  - R16 (decided on 2026-10-30): lists joined by "/" or "+" were split with no rule asking for it. Rule 22 now says "Split such a list only when the record's text also names its items separately somewhere else; otherwise keep it whole, as written."; the model's copy follows. The annotation tool flags an instance holding one item of such a list whose items the text never names separately (slash_list_split, 050's guide), with a test. Checked over the 13 records: no flag; "Level 2/3 (EDR/RDR)" splits (the record names the RDR and EDR data separately), "RSI 1/2/3", "PSA/PDS", "MODIS/VIIRS" and "Aquarius/Satélite de Aplicaciones Científicas (SAC-D)" stay whole.
  - G6 (decided on 2026-10-30): the two MODIS sinusoidal-grid records left out the title's "Global" for different reasons in left_out.md. Both cited rule 19's "A qualifier stays in the instance, as written, if it is any other kind", reading "Global" as part of the grid's name. Revised on 2026-10-30 after research (NASA LAADS DAAC on the MODIS tiles: "Each tile has fixed Earth-locations covering an area of approximately 1200 x 1200 km (10 x 10 degrees at the equator)"; MODLAND calls the grid the "Sinusoidal Tile Grid" (https://modis-land.gsfc.nasa.gov/MODLAND_grid.html), and NASA CMR names its tiling system "MODIS Tile SIN"; CMR gives these products global spatial coverage; 8 of the catalog's 384 "SIN Grid" titles have no "Global"): no source calls "Global 1km SIN Grid" the grid's name, so by rule 19 "Global" is an area the data cover. Both records now have the ground truth triple HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Global" (source text "Global 1km SIN Grid"), the aerosol record next to its HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Land" (the several_qualifiers flag marks the two, as expected), and both left-out items are gone.
  - R10 (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 19 keeps its two tests that need judgment, the ground-or-water test ("the [rest] of the [word]") and "if taking it out would give a triple that isn't true". No test on the words alone can replace them: the same wording gets opposite answers ("Ocean Cloud Cover": "Ocean" is taken out; "Ocean Surface Temperature" stays whole). The NASA report (section 8.3) states it as a limitation: the annotator judges whether a statement is true, and a second annotator's agreement on these tests is not measured yet. A third test that needs judgment was added on 2026-10-30: rule 22's collective "and" ("what the record says holds only for the items together"), see below.
  - G3 (decided on 2026-10-30, option (b)): rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple* now has the exception "A period of time or a range of values whose start and end the record states with other words in between is written as one instance, the start and the end joined by the record's own linking words, the words in between left out."; the model's copy follows, and the annotation tool's object_not_in_text message names it as a possible reason. Ground truth change: record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0", HAS_TIME_SPAN "January, 2007 up to March, 2011" is now "from January, 2007 up to March, 2011", keeping the record's "from".
  - Inference from the record's own statements (decided on 2026-10-30): rule 1 of *What counts as a fact* now says "A fact the record does not state in one passage may be written when a predicate's definition gives a test that the record's own statements meet (e.g. SUPERSEDES …); never infer from outside knowledge.", with one test for every comparison (G5): "when the record says one thing is lower, higher, better (or any other comparison) without saying than what, and it names exactly two things of that kind together, the other one is what it is compared with; with three or more, no fact." The source text of such a fact is the passages that meet the test, joined by " … " (`common/common_helpers/validate.py`, `PASSAGES_JOIN`; a source text so joined is in the record's text when each passage is, with a test). The model's copy states both tests in the rules, since the model reads the current schema's definitions, not the ground truth schema's. Precedent: DocRED (Yao et al., "DocRED: A Large-Scale Document-Level Relation Extraction Dataset", ACL 2019) counts facts that need reasoning across a document's sentences: "at least 40.7% of the relational facts in DocRED can only be extracted from multiple sentences", with the reasoning types pattern recognition (38.9%), logical (26.6%), coreference (17.6%) and common-sense (16.6%). This rule is stricter: inference only through a test written in a predicate's definition or in the rules, from the record's own statements, never from outside knowledge.
  - G4 (decided on 2026-10-30): "Version 6.1" SUPERSEDES "Version 6" stays. SUPERSEDES's definition (ground truth schema) now ends "A record states this when it says the older one is discontinued or decommissioned and directs users to the newer one"; the translation table's row SUPERSEDES → SUPERSEDES is therefore stale and waits for a person's check.
  - G5 (decided on 2026-10-30): "SST4" HAS_LOWER_UNCERTAINTY_THAN "skin SST" stays, by the comparison test above; its source text is now "Two SST products can be present in these files. The first is a skin SST … identified as SST4. The SST4 product has lower uncertainty".
  - G7 (decided on 2026-10-30, option (a)): in record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0", "skin SST (NSST)" is now "skin SST" in its seven ground truth triples: "(NSST)" follows "night", not "skin SST", so it is not a name with its abbreviation in parentheses. "NSST" is added to annotations/left_out.md; the expert questions name "skin SST".
  - G8 (decided on 2026-10-30, no change): "Aquarius L-band radiometer" stays: by rule 23, the words left once the type word is removed, "Aquarius L-band", are not a name, so the type word is kept. Follow-up (2026-10-30): rule 23 now says "the words left once the type word is removed" instead of "the words without it", so it can't be read as any shorter part of the name.
  - G9 (decided on 2026-10-30): "encounter data" HAS_DURATION "~40 minute period" is now "~40 minute" (rule 18: the number and its unit). Checked over the 13 records for other quantities carrying words besides the number and its unit; not changed, to decide: "36 spectral bands" (HAS_SPECTRAL_BANDS, where the predicate already says what is counted), "353,191 detections (above the processing likelihood threshold of 6)" and "from 2 to 25 epochs each" (HAS_NUMBER_OF, where the counted noun stays, but the condition and "each" may not), and "in the 0.5 - 4.5 keV band, between ~ 10^43 erg/s and ~ 10^46 erg/s" (HAS_LUMINOSITY_RANGE, with its band).
  - G10 (decided on 2026-10-30): the record's dataset HAS_VARIABLE "SST4" keeps its source text's two passages, joined by " … ": the title's "SST MID-IR" and the notes' "At night, a second SST product is generated using the mid-infrared 3.95 and 4.05 micron wavelength channels which are unique to MODIS; the SST derived from these measurements is identified as SST4".
  - ALICE record (confirmed on 2026-10-30, no change): in "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0", the target stays on the mission phase, "comet escort 1" HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko", as rule 19's subject sentence (R2) gives it; the record's dataset gets no HAS_TARGET of its own. Revised on 2026-11-01 (the second opinion's results, judgment call B): the target stays on the phase, and the title's "67P" now gives the record's dataset HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko" too, a fact of the title this note did not consider.
  - G11 (to do when the record is corrected): the unfinished record "ROSETTA-ORBITER PRELANDING OSINAC 2 EDR MTP003 V2.0" (pool position 8), drafted by gemini-2.5-pro, needs three corrections under the rules: HAS_TARGET "comet 67P" becomes "67P" (rule 23, a type word that only describes the thing); "V2.0" SUPERSEDES "previous deliveries of the same dataset" is left out, since it names no specific version (rule 9 of *What counts as a fact*); and "MTP003" as a MissionPhase is left out, since the record does not say what the code stands for (rule 1 of *What counts as a fact*, R11; as the OSINAC 4 record's "MTP020"). A fourth, found by the third audit (finding G-13, 2026-10-31): HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "images" (PhysicalQuantity): images are data, not a property that can be measured.
  - One rule for periods and ranges (decided on 2026-10-30, from the generality check): rule 22's range sub-item ("A range is one item, not a list") and rule 11's period exception (G3) are merged into one sub-item of rule 22: "A period of time or a range of values is one instance, however it is worded" ("between A and B", "from A to B", "A to B", "A–B", "A through B", "A until B"), with the start and end joined by the record's own linking words when other words come between them. Rule 11 now points to it, and so does the collective-"and" exception. No ground truth triple changes.
  - Collective "and" as a definition, not a closed list (decided on 2026-10-30, from the generality check): rule 22's exception keeps a list whole when "and" means "A and B together" (what the record says holds only for the items together); the five nouns ("continuity", "ratio", "difference", "comparison", "correlation") are now examples of its signs, with "average of …" and "merger of …", not a closed list. Why: the five came from one record ("continuity of SST derived from heritage and current NASA sensors"); finding the rest in the catalog would need a search that leaves out the ground truth candidates pool (see *Considered and not changed*); the definition covers every case. It needs judgment, like rule 19's two tests (R10), and the NASA report says so. Checked on the 13 records: the continuity list stays whole, and "Normalized Difference Snow Index (NDSI) and a series of screens", "between … NASA and … CONAE" and "Combined Terra and Aqua" stay split. The model's copy of the rules says the same, with invented examples; its fingerprint changes.
  - Title-case ties in rule 23: considered and not changed on 2026-10-30; see *Considered and not changed*, below.
  - Title codes in `annotations/left_out.md` (corrected on 2026-10-30, from research on the Rosetta archive): the notes on "1/2/3" (CODMAC processing levels 1, 2 and 3), "1149" (a running measurement number), "CAL" (the PDS3 target "CALIBRATION", not "calibrated": the ALICE data are uncalibrated, CODMAC level 2) and "MTP020" (planning period no. 20 in the phase COMET ESCORT 3) now say what the sources say, with links. The items stay left out: the records' texts do not explain the codes (rule 1 of *What counts as a fact*).
  - Second sweep (2026-10-30, read-only, after the first audit's decisions). Wording and consistency fixes (no rule's, definition's or triple's meaning changed): examples that broke their own rules were fixed (rule 2's "MODIS/Aqua" title example now says the notes name MODIS and Aqua separately, as rule 22's "/" sub-item needs; rule 15's "collected by MODIS aboard Terra" now gives "MODIS" ABOARD "Terra", not the record's dataset FROM_SPACECRAFT, as the ground truth does for that sentence shape; rule 16's "Polar-Gridded" example, which rule 19 would change, is replaced by "Normalized Difference Snow Index (NDSI)"; the model's copy follows with invented examples, and its collective example "mean of the morning and evening passes", whose times of day rule 19 takes out first, is now "ratio of the red and near-infrared reflectances"); the ground truth: record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0"'s HAS_TIME_SPAN keeps the record's "from" (rule 22's period rule), and record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3"'s HAS_REDSHIFT_RANGE and HAS_LUMINOSITY_RANGE have source texts with words for the subject and predicate (rule 17); the rules' shape: rule 19's three parts are bullets with bold leads ("Whose fact it is", "Ground and water words", "What stays in the instance"), as in rules 21 and 22, and rules 3, 5 and 17 have a *Suppose*/*Then* example. Decided on 2026-10-30: a name stays whole (finding R-A1): rule 11 now says "A name stays whole: rules 19 and 22 never split or shorten a name". Decided on 2026-10-30: rule 21 includes facts about another dataset when the record states a chain of "part of" or "made from" links that connects it to the record's dataset (its parts, what it is part of, what it is made from, and so on), and leaves out facts about other datasets, such as a variant or a product made from it (finding R-A2; before, it left out facts about the record's dataset's own parts, which the ground truth keeps). Research of 2026-10-30 on the experts' questions (sources from NASA's data centers, the Planetary Data System and ESA) is noted where it confirms a reading: in annotations/left_out.md (NSST, the MODIS tile, RDR, lien) and in the notes of Q1, Q2, Q3, Q6, Q10, Q18, Q19 and Q28; Q20's quote of NASA's LANCE page is dated, since the page's wording changed. To do after the rerun: the NASA report's Table 2 has no row for HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE, which now has 6 ground truth triples; recount the table. The sweep's other findings are to decide, one at a time.
  - Rule 19's whose-fact test by the entity class (decided on 2026-10-31, second audit's finding R-B1): rule 19 of *Writing a classed triple* gave the new ground truth triple to the instance the qualifier was in "when that instance is the subject of a ground truth triple of its own", which depends on the record's other facts (a sentence elsewhere giving the instance a fact of its own moved the qualifier): the very reason option 1 of *Rule 19 can lose which fact a qualifier went with* was rejected. It is now decided by the instance's entity class: a standalone entity class (`rule 19: standalone`: Dataset, Mission, MissionPhase, Spacecraft, Instrument, Organization, Person, WebTool, Document, SatelliteFormation, Variable, Algorithm, Channel, Grid, Phenomenon, CelestialBody, Country) keeps the qualifier's fact; for a describing entity class (`rule 19: describing`: Purpose, Task, Role, PhysicalQuantity, Issue, Discipline, Time, TimeOfDay, Value, Version, ProcessingLevel, SpatialExtent, SpatialResolution, TemporalResolution, Identifier, WebPage, MapProjection, SpatialRepresentation, Orbit) the fact goes to the subject of the triple whose object the instance was. The field's practice: ontologies [schemas] of observations treat a variable as a thing of its own [a thing entity class] (RDA I-ADOPT, `Variable`, "a description of something observed or derived", [I-ADOPT ontology](https://i-adopt.github.io/ontology/); until the third audit's finding S-9 of 2026-10-31 this also cited W3C SOSA/SSN's `sosa:ObservableProperty`, which SOSA uses for bare properties such as temperature too, so it supports no line between Variable and PhysicalQuantity), a method as a thing of its own (W3C [PROV-O](https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-o/), `prov:Plan`, PROV-O; IAO's "algorithm", a plan specification, [IAO_0000064](http://purl.obolibrary.org/obo/IAO_0000064)), an instrument's parts as things of their own (`ssn:hasSubSystem`, which links a system to its component parts, in the same SSN ontology), a named grid as an identified thing (OGC's Two Dimensional Tile Matrix Set standard and its register of tile matrix sets, [OGC TMS](https://www.ogc.org/standards/tms/)), phenomena as things of their own (the [SWEET](https://github.com/ESIPFed/sweet) ontology's `phen` modules, SWEET), and an orbit as a property of a collection [a dataset] (NASA CMR's UMM-C puts `OrbitParameters` in a collection's spatial extent, [UMM-C schema](https://cdn.earthdata.nasa.gov/umm/collection/v1.18.1/umm-c-json-schema.json)). One home: the marks are lines `rule 19: thing` / `rule 19: property` under each entity class of `annotations/ground_truth_schema.txt`, and rule 19's qualifier predicates are marked there too (`rule 19: qualifier, a country`, and so on); the annotation tool's `several_qualifiers` check reads them from the file (`schema_io.qualifier_predicates`; `validate.py` no longer keeps its own list), and the tests check that every entity class has exactly one mark, that every qualifier predicate exists, that their kinds are exactly rule 19's list, and that no code keeps a list of its own. The model's copy of the rules says the same in words (it isn't shown the ground truth schema; its fingerprint changes), and its swaths example no longer rests on a triple rule 24 may not give (finding R-A7): "the Level-1B swaths are made from the raw counts". New terms: *standalone entity class*, *describing entity class*, *qualifier predicate* (docs/terminology.md); the first two were named *thing entity class* and *property entity class* until 2026-10-31, renamed then because "thing" and "property" are words the definitions themselves use in other senses (a PhysicalQuantity is "a bare property"), and the marks with them (`rule 19: standalone`, `rule 19: describing`). Not BFO's independent and dependent continuants: our sort differs from BFO's (Basic Formal Ontology, ISO/IEC 21838-2): there an algorithm is a plan specification (IAO_0000064, above), a piece of information, which BFO counts as a generically dependent continuant [a describing entity class: it exists only as carried by something else], while our Algorithm is standalone. Ground truth re-derived for every triple rule 19 gave: one change, in record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0": "encounter with comet 9P/Tempel 1" now gives "encounter" (MissionPhase, a thing) HAS_TARGET "9P/Tempel 1", and, by rule 22's shared words, "approach" HAS_TARGET "9P/Tempel 1" ("approach to and encounter with comet 9P/Tempel 1"); the record's dataset keeps HAS_TARGET "9P/Tempel 1" as a fact of its title (source text "9P/TEMPEL 1"). This also removes the possible form B loss recorded for that record (the target read as covering the cruise phase too). Every other case gives the same subject as before (NASA and CONAE, Organizations; "pixels", "EDR (raw) sensor data", "RDR data", "encounter data", Datasets; "comet escort 1", a MissionPhase; "skin SST", a Variable; Aqua's purposes, the measured quantities and the Rosetta gravity measurement, properties). Not re-derived: the held-out, unfinished record "ROSETTA-ORBITER PRELANDING OSINAC 2 EDR MTP003 V2.0" (G11, to correct when it is annotated).
  - A country in rule 19 (decided on 2026-10-31, finding R-B7): a country is BASED_IN when it qualifies a Person or an Organization ("U.S. NASA"), and an area the data cover (HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE) when it qualifies what is measured ("U.S. precipitation"). Why: BASED_IN is wrong for a dataset. The field's practice: W3C DCAT gives a dataset's area as its spatial coverage, `dct:spatial` ([DCAT 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/)). Both copies of the rules; no ground truth triple changes (no finished record has a country qualifying what is measured).
  - Inference tests in rule 1 (decided on 2026-10-31, finding R-B2): the guidelines' rule 1 now states both tests itself, replacement (SUPERSEDES) and comparison, and says the list of tests is closed, as the model's copy does; before, the guidelines let any predicate's definition give a test, which the model is never shown. SUPERSEDES's definition keeps its sentence. The allowed difference for rule 1 (tests/allowed_differences.txt) is gone.
  - Source texts of title facts and of words in different places (decided on 2026-10-31, finding R-B3): rule 17 (both copies) now says that for a fact the title states by its words alone, the source text is the title's words for the object, and that when the words for the subject, predicate and object are in different places, the source text is those passages, each copied exactly, joined by " … ". Ground truth made consistent (source texts only): record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0" HAS_VERSION "V1.0": "COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0" → "V1.0"; record "MODIS/Aqua Snow Cover 5-Min L2 Swath 500m V061" ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT: "MODIS/Aqua" → "MODIS", FROM_SPACECRAFT "Aqua": "MODIS/Aqua" → "Aqua"; record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005" HAS_VERSION "V005": "Salinity V005" → "V005"; record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0" FROM_SPACECRAFT "Aqua": "MODIS Aqua" → "Aqua"; records "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid" and "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006" HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Global": "Global 1km SIN Grid" → "Global"; the second's FROM_SPACECRAFT "Terra": "MODIS/Terra" → "Terra". Left as they are: the title facts of record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3", whose title has words for its predicates ("of", "in"), so rule 17's shortest passage applies (applied on 2026-10-31, below).
  - Rule 2's list of the rules that leave a fact out (decided on 2026-10-31, findings R-B4, P-2): now "rule 1 above and rules 3 to 10 below, and rules 16, 20, 21 and 24 of *Writing a classed triple*", checked against the rules annotations/left_out.md cites (1, 3–7, 9, 10, 16, 21, 24, and rule 2's expert's answer; rules 22 and 23 leave out words, not facts, and rule 22's item that names no specific thing is rule 9).
  - Rule 24's words that name no method (decided on 2026-10-31, findings R-B6, P-2): they give no triple and stay in the instance, as written (rule 19: "any other kind"); and the words for a processing step's input and method are not part of another instance, so rule 11's exceptions now include rule 24 (both copies). Not changed yet: record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0"'s "image data in W/m^2/sr/nm", which by this rule would keep its words ("solar stray-light corrected, in-field stray-light corrected, radiometric calibrated and geometric distortion corrected (resampled) image data in W/m^2/sr/nm"), waits on the pending decision on rule 22's lists of words that describe one thing (finding R-B5). Done on 2026-10-31, below (*Describing words joined by "and" or a comma*).
  - Parentheses in rule 20 (decided on 2026-10-31, findings R-B8, B13): "A name with its own abbreviation, another name or code of its own, or a short note in parentheses is one instance, as written." Parentheses that hold the code of something else are not part of the instance. Ground truth: record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0" "EDR (raw) sensor data" HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 2 (EDR)" → "Level 2", and "RDR data" HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "Level 3 (RDR)" → "Level 3": EDR and RDR are PDS3 data set types (Experiment Data Record, Reduced Data Record), not abbreviations of the levels (PDS3 Standards Reference, chapter 6, [Chapter06.pdf](https://pds.nasa.gov/datastandards/pds3/standards/sr/Chapter06.pdf)), and the subjects already carry them. The quotes in annotations/questions_for_experts.md and annotations/left_out.md follow.
  - The rules' shape (done on 2026-10-31, finding P-1, the rest): rule 23's parts are bullets with bold leads (*The record's dataset*, *Type words*, *Capitals and punctuation*, whose note that it is only in the guidelines stays), and so are rule 24's (*One step after another*, *Words that name no method*), rule 1's two tests and rule 17's two new parts; rule 19 gains *A country*; rule 12 has the RSI example, moved from *Examples*. The model's copy labels the same parts the same way. Rule 22's sub-items are unchanged (a decision on them is pending).
  - Describing words joined by "and" or a comma (decided on 2026-10-31, finding R-B5; it reverses the 2026-10-30 split of record "NASA Standards and and Technical Assistance Resource Tool"'s purpose, recorded above under *Rule 19 can lose which fact a qualifier went with*): rule 22 (both copies) has a new sub-item: "Keep them whole when the word they describe is singular; split them when that word is plural" ("a sun synchronous, polar orbit" is one orbit; "access … quickly and conveniently" is one action; "the 11 and 12 micron channels" is two channels). Why: the test "true of each item alone" split words that describe one thing as if they were a list of things, and the ground truth treated the same situation two ways (START's purpose split, Aqua's orbit whole). Ground truth: START's two HAS_PURPOSE ("… quickly", "… conveniently") are again one, "make it simpler for technical people to access NASA technical information quickly and conveniently"; Aqua's orbit stays whole; record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0"'s HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "image data in W/m^2/sr/nm" is now "solar stray-light corrected, in-field stray-light corrected, radiometric calibrated and geometric distortion corrected (resampled) image data in W/m^2/sr/nm" (findings R-B6, G-7: rule 24's words that name no method stay in the instance). The worked example of *Writing a classed triple* and rule 17's example follow; the model's copy has invented examples (its fingerprint changes).
  - CONAE's role (decided on 2026-10-31, finding R-B9; it reverses the 2026-10-25 decision recorded in annotations/left_out.md): record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005" now has "Comisión Nacional de Actividades Espaciales (CONAE)" SERVES_AS "Argentina's space agency" (Role), and no longer BASED_IN "Argentina". Why: rule 4's exception ("it names a role only JPL holds, not a type": "Argentina's space agency" names a role only CONAE holds), and rule 19's "if taking it out would give a triple that isn't true" ("space agency" alone is a type, so "Argentina's" stays in the instance). Role's definition now says so ("a position only one Person or Organization holds (rule 4), e.g. the official archive for SST; Argentina's space agency", finding S3).
  - PhysicalQuantity and Variable (decided on 2026-10-31, findings S4, G-8): PhysicalQuantity is "a bare property that can be measured, whatever method measures it, e.g. temperature; gravity; sea surface temperature; snow cover"; Variable is "a property narrowed by a method, a band, a threshold, another object or another constraint, e.g. SST4; NDSI; band 31 emissivity; dust fluences at each of twelve mass thresholds" ("by one particular Algorithm" is gone: it needed outside knowledge, since no record names the NDSI's algorithm). The field's practice: RDA I-ADOPT, whose Variable is "A description of something observed or derived, minimally consisting of an ObjectOfInterest and its Property", narrowed by a Constraint, which "limits the scope of the observation and confines the context to a particular state" ([I-ADOPT ontology](https://i-adopt.github.io/ontology/), checked 2026-10-31). A difference from I-ADOPT, kept on purpose: what a property is of, as the record names it with the property ("sea surface temperature", "Land Surface Temperature", "Aerosol Optical Depth"), stays part of a bare PhysicalQuantity here, while I-ADOPT would call it a Variable; "another object" means a thing other than what the property is of, such as a band (a Channel) or an instrument. Reading every PhysicalQuantity strictly by I-ADOPT would move almost all of them to Variable, which Q3 asks the experts. Re-checked every PhysicalQuantity and Variable of the 13 finished records; changed (HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF → HAS_VARIABLE, since HAS_VARIABLE is pinned to Variable): record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006"'s "band 31 emissivity" and "band 32 emissivity" (a band; their COMPUTED_FROM "land cover types" keep it, with the subject class Variable); record "STARDUST 9P/TEMPEL 1 DFMI 2/3 NEXT V1.0"'s "dust fluences at each of twelve mass thresholds" (a threshold); record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3"'s "Ensemble X-Ray Variability" (a band, X-ray). Unchanged: every other PhysicalQuantity (bare, or named with what it is of), and every Variable (SST4, skin SST, NDSI, the two fast neutron count rates: each narrowed by a method or an energy band).
  - Ground truth schema, the rest of the second audit (decided on 2026-10-31): HAS_SPECTRAL_BANDS is merged into HAS_NUMBER_OF (S1: "36 spectral bands" and "412 quasars" are the same kind of fact; "MODIS" HAS_NUMBER_OF "36 spectral bands"; no translation row named it); HAS_IDENTIFIER is "has this Identifier, other than a DOI (HAS_DOI)" (S2), and Identifier "a code that names one thing, or one product line, permanently, e.g. a DOI; MOD11A1" (B6: in NASA CMR a short name names a product line across its versions, and the DOI names one version; "MOD11A1" and "MCD19A2N" stay); HAS_TOTAL_FIELD_AREA is renamed HAS_SUMMED_SKY_AREA, "covers this much of the sky, each part of the sky counted as many times as it is covered", next to HAS_SKY_AREA, now "covers this much of the sky, each part of the sky counted once" (S5: the name still said "field"; no translation row named it); the PREDICATES section says "A predicate whose definition names its object's entity class is pinned to it", with the pins it was missing (S6: HAS_TARGET → CelestialBody, BASED_IN → Country, BINNED_INTO → Grid, MAPPED_INTO → MapProjection, AFFILIATED_WITH → Organization, RESPONSIBLE_FOR → Task, SERVES_AS → Role, HAS_IDENTIFIER → Identifier, AVAILABLE_AT → WebPage; the annotation tool's checks flag nothing new in the finished records); HAS_TARGET is "is about, or studies, this CelestialBody" (S7: it reads for Aqua and for a MissionPhase too), and CelestialBody "a natural object in space, or a kind of such objects, e.g. Vesta; active galactic nuclei" (G-10: HAS_TARGET "active galactic nuclei (AGN)" stays); SpatialExtent ends "… the south pole of Mars; a country, when the data cover it)" and Country is "a nation, as where a Person or Organization is based, e.g. U.S.; Argentina" (S8, as rule 19's *A country*); a comment under ENTITY CLASSES says CatalogEntry, the describes triple's subject class, is written by code and not listed; the sibling rewordings, with no change of meaning (P-3): FROM_MISSION "was collected during this Mission" (as ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT, FROM_SPACECRAFT, HAS_MISSION_PHASE), CREATED_ON "was made on" (as the other dates), HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION "its values are this far apart in time, e.g. daily" (as HAS_SPATIAL_RESOLUTION and HAS_GRANULE_DURATION), every entity class definition that began "the …" or "how …" now begins "a/an …", and twins sit next to each other (FROM_MISSION and FROM_SPACECRAFT, HAS_TIME_SPAN and HAS_DURATION, the two resolutions, the two sky areas). Translation table: the rows whose ground truth schema definition changed only in wording (P-3: SpatialResolution, ProcessingLevel, Frequency → TemporalResolution, HAS_TIME_RESOLUTION → HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION) keep their check, with the new wording stored; the rows whose definition changed in meaning are stale, for me to check again (PhysicalQuantity, HAS_TARGET, AstronomicalObject and Comet → CelestialBody, SpatialCoverage → SpatialExtent, DOI → Identifier).
  - A service and its provider (decided on 2026-10-31, finding G-9 (a)): PROVIDED_BY_PARTY, "is provided to users, as a service or as data, by this Person or Organization", in the _BY_PARTY family (pinned to Person or Organization); record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3" has the record's dataset PROVIDED_BY_PARTY "NASA HEASARC" ("This is a service provided by NASA HEASARC"), and its two left-out items are gone. Why: providing a service is not creating it, and rule 2 says leaving out a fact is the worst mistake. The field's practice: schema.org's `provider` ("The service provider, service operator, or service performer; the goods producer", [schema.org/provider](https://schema.org/provider)) is a property apart from `creator`; W3C DCAT has `dcat:DataService` and `dct:publisher` ("The entity [the Person or Organization] responsible for making the resource available"), apart from `dct:creator` ([DCAT 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/), checked 2026-10-31). The decision gave the definition as "provides this service or data to users"; it is written subject to object (rule 13), as "is sponsored by this Person or Organization" is. The translation table's row PROVIDED_BY → (none) waits for my check again. The left-out item on "the authors" as the dataset's developer now cites rule 9 ("the authors" names no specific people), not Q26 (G-9 (b)).
  - Radio science is a technique (decided on 2026-10-31, B4): a new entity class Technique ("a way of making measurements, e.g. radio science"; `rule 19: describing`) and a new predicate USES_TECHNIQUE ("was collected using this Technique", pinned to it); record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0"'s HAS_DISCIPLINE "Radio Science" is now USES_TECHNIQUE "Radio Science" (Technique). Why: the Planetary Data System has no radio science node among its six discipline nodes and files "RADIO SCIENCE" as an INSTRUMENT_TYPE ([PDS, About](https://pds.nasa.gov/home/about/); the data set's catalog files, [RSI CATALOG](https://archives.esac.esa.int/psa/ftp/INTERNATIONAL-ROSETTA-MISSION/RSI/RO-C-RSI-1-2-3-ESC4-1149-V1.0/CATALOG/)), and NASA's Science Mission Directorate's five divisions don't include it. A source on the other side, found later on 2026-10-31 (research of NASA's reference lists, Q10): PDS4's own search facet `discipline_name` allows "Radio Science", beside "Imaging" and "Spectroscopy" ("The attribute pds:discipline_name must be equal to one of the following values 'Atmospheres', 'Fields', 'Flux Measurements', 'Geosciences', 'Imaging', 'Particles', 'Radio Science', 'Ring-Moon Systems', 'Small Bodies', 'Spectroscopy'", the PDS4 schematron, IM 1.23.0.0, [PDS4_PDS_1N00.sch](https://pds.nasa.gov/datastandards/schema/released/pds/v1/PDS4_PDS_1N00.sch)). That facet mixes fields with techniques, so it is no list of fields of science, and radio science still fits Technique by our definitions; B4 stands. Discipline and HAS_DISCIPLINE stay defined (no ground truth triple uses them now; see *Considered and not changed*). Rule 12's example in the guidelines follows; Q10's sub-questions on RSI and on radio science are settled in its notes, and it asks only which list of disciplines to follow. The translation table's row Method → Algorithm waits for my check again, since a Method may now be a Technique.
  - "RSI" left out (decided on 2026-10-31, findings G-2, B10): record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0"'s ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT "RSI" (Instrument) is deleted: "RSI" is an unexplained code, and its entity class depends on what it stands for (rule 1: "This includes its entity class"). annotations/left_out.md has the item, with what the Planetary Data System files it as. (Reversed later on 2026-10-31 by *Names on an official NASA list*, in *The rule gaps found by the reviews of batches 4 and 5, closed*, below: the triple is written again, from the PDS4 context product; its meaning stays out.) No PDS3 title convention (the order of a data set name's parts) is added to rule 1's closed list: see *Considered and not changed*.
  - Latency (decided on 2026-10-31, B11): a new entity class Latency ("a measure of how soon data are released after observation, e.g. Near Real Time (NRT)"; `rule 19: describing`; the decision's wording "how soon …" begins "a measure of", as P-3 asks of entity class definitions) and a new predicate HAS_LATENCY ("is released this soon after observation"); record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid" has HAS_LATENCY "Near Real Time (NRT)", and its left-out item is gone. The field's practice: NASA CMR's UMM-C `CollectionDataType`, a closed list (NEAR_REAL_TIME "ready for end user consumption 1 to 3 hours after data acquisition", LOW_LATENCY, EXPEDITED, SCIENCE_QUALITY, OTHER; [UMM-C schema](https://cdn.earthdata.nasa.gov/umm/collection/v1.18.1/umm-c-json-schema.json), checked 2026-10-31); the instance keeps the record's words (rule 11). The translation table's row HAS_LATENCY → (none) is now marked *now exists*, for my check.
  - Ground truth, the rest of the second audit (decided on 2026-10-31): record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006"'s issue "significant loss of data retrieval following the Constellation Exit Maneuvers" is now "loss of data retrieval following the Constellation Exit Maneuvers" in its two ground truth triples (G-4, rule 5: "significant" is a judgement, as the AGN record's left-out item already said), with a left-out item; record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005"'s HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION "Polar-Gridded" is now HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Polar" and HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION "gridded" (G-5, B8: by rule 19, as "Global" in G6, and rule 23's capitals: the notes' "gridded"; checked on 2026-10-30: the product covers only latitudes poleward of 50° in each hemisphere, "SSS retrievals at latitudes greater than 50 degrees in both hemispheres are averaged and gridded into a weekly product", on EASE-Grid 2.0 North and South, [NSIDC user guide](https://nsidc.org/sites/default/files/aq3_sss-v005-userguide_0.pdf)), and its left-out item is gone (rule 16's example in the guidelines no longer uses this case since the second sweep); record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid"'s COMPUTED_FROM has the subject instance "MAIAC Land Surface Bidirectional Reflectance Factor (BRF)", not "MCD19A1N" (G-11: the record names that product three ways, each once, so rule 23's tie gives the first); record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3"'s three title facts take rule 17's shortest passage as source text: HAS_VARIABLE "Ensemble X-Ray Variability": "Ensemble X-Ray Variability" (no words for the predicate, so the title's words for the object); HAS_TARGET "active galactic nuclei (AGN)": "Variability of AGN"; COMPUTED_FROM "2XMMi-DR3": "AGN in 2XMMi-DR3" (each the predicate's word, "of" or "in", with the words it joins). The held-out, unfinished record "ROSETTA-ORBITER PRELANDING OSINAC 2 EDR MTP003 V2.0" (corrections only, no rule shaped from it; G11 above, findings G-12, B12): its HAS_TIME_SPAN keeps the record's "from"; "PRELANDING" HAS_TIME_SPAN is deleted (in the text, "covering" goes with the data set, and the period is the data set's); HAS_PROCESSING_LEVEL "EDR" is deleted (an unexplained code, rule 1, as the OSINAC 4 record's "RDR"; checked on 2026-10-30: EDR is a PDS3 data set type, not a level). annotations/left_out.md: the two items marked "Rule: none to quote" now cite rule 16 (the purpose "used to calculate", the fact COMPUTED_FROM already states) and rule 9 ("XMM-Newton sample", whose sources have no instance of their own).
  - Renamed terms (decided on 2026-10-31): *thing entity class* and *property entity class* are now *standalone entity class* and *describing entity class*, and the schema's marks `rule 19: standalone` and `rule 19: describing` (see *Rule 19's whose-fact test by the entity class*, above, for why not BFO's).
- **Third audit of the rules, the ground truth schema and the ground truth (2026-10-31; read-only, 51 findings; decided the same day).** Decided and done:
  - Names in rule 11 and rule 24 (findings R-1, R-2, R-3): rule 11's exceptions now include rule 20 ("parentheses that hold the code of something else are not part of the instance"), which removes words too ("Level 2/3 (EDR/RDR)" gives "Level 2"); "rules 19, 22 and 24 never split or shorten a name"; and one exception the ground truth already followed in three records: a name made of a product's code and its version gives its parts as facts of their own (HAS_IDENTIFIER, HAS_VERSION), and a version so named is written as the version alone ("MOD11A1 Version 6.1" gives "Version 6.1"). Rule 24 now says the words for an input and a method are taken out only of the words for what a step made, not of a name that contains them: in record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid", "MAIAC" is taken out of the notes' words for the record's dataset (whose name is its title) and stays in "MAIAC Land Surface Bidirectional Reflectance Factor (BRF)", the name of another product. Why: rule 11 said a name stays whole, while the ground truth shortened names in ways no rule stated. No ground truth triple changes.
  - Source texts in rule 17 (R-5, R-6, R-7): two new parts. *A name's facts*: for a fact a name in the notes states by its words alone, the source text is the name as written. *The thing the title names*: for a fact about it, its words may be left out of the source text, as the annotation tool already allowed and about 35 ground truth triples do; I did not extend those source texts, since step 070 doesn't read source texts when pairing, so it would change no score. *Words in different places* now applies in its two cases only (the title and the notes, or the passages that meet a test of rule 1). Ground truth: three source texts are now the name as written ("Tempel 1 encounter data", "GRaND plus Z boron loaded sensor", "GRaND epithermal neutron map").
  - Wordings in rule 23 (R-8, R-9, G-7): "it contains the other's words, each as a whole word" (so "Nighttime" in a title and "night" in the notes are two equal wordings, and "night", the one the record uses most often, stays); the exception is the thing the title names (the describes triple's object instance), which is not always a Dataset (rule 12 already said so); *Capitals and punctuation* now says a leading "a", "an" or "the" is left out, in the guidelines only like the rest of it, since evened-out matching ignores it (`common/common_helpers/text_match.py`, `LEADING_WORDS`). Ground truth: "a series of screens" is "series of screens" in its three ground truth triples, and the ground truth schema's examples follow (Algorithm, Time, SpatialExtent, SatelliteFormation, Channel, Role).
  - Rules 19, 21 and 22 (R-10, R-12, R-13, R-11): a qualifier taken out of a describing instance goes to the subject of the triple whose object the instance was in the qualifier's sentence; the chain of "part of" or "made from" links runs through further datasets or variables (the NDSI, a Variable, in record "MODIS/Aqua Snow Cover 5-Min L2 Swath 500m V061"); Aqua's example says why "global" stays in its purposes; "data" is plural only when the record treats it so, with a plural verb or determiner ("these data are"), otherwise singular, so record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0"'s image data keep their describing words whole.
  - Rules 1, 4 and 16, and the model's examples (R-15, R-16, R-17, R-18, R-20): rule 4 says "one specific role" in both copies ("or thing" dropped: every sentence names some thing); the model's rule 1 has the guidelines' sentence on using what the words mean (rule 23); its replacement example uses versions ("Version 3 was decommissioned …") instead of codes no convention explains; its rule 24 example's method is a threshold test, not a cloud mask (a mask is data, and Q7, input or method, is open); rule 16's examples (both copies) show one fact stated twice, not a choice of wording. The model's copy's fingerprint changes, so 050's and 060's next runs ask again.
  - Ground truth schema (S-1 to S-7): pins added to HAS_RELEASE (Dataset), HAS_DOI (Identifier; now "has this Identifier, a Digital Object Identifier"), HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE (now "covers this SpatialExtent"), HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF (now "holds measurements of this PhysicalQuantity", so it no longer overlaps HAS_VARIABLE), and LAUNCHED_ON, DECOMMISSIONED_ON, PRODUCTION_DISCONTINUED_ON, ENDED_ON and CREATED_ON (each now "… on this date (a Time)"; "launched on" could be read as a launch vehicle); BASED_IN is "is based in this Country" ("belongs to" is AFFILIATED_WITH's word); Value is "an amount, or a range of amounts, …" (three ground truth triples give it a range); WebPage excludes "a DOI written as an address (an Identifier)". Every ground truth triple already fits the pins. `tests/test_schema_io.py` now checks the schema's own rule that a predicate whose definition names its object's entity class is pinned to it. Translation table: rows changed only in wording keep their check (Algorithm and Method → Algorithm, HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE and HAS_PROPERTY → HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE, HAS_DOI, TimePeriod → Time, Wavelength → Channel); rows whose target's meaning changed are stale and wait for a check: Quantity, Number, Altitude and Mass → Value; URL → WebPage; MEASURES, PROVIDES and MEASURED_BY → HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF; LOCATED_IN → BASED_IN.
  - Ground truth (G-1, G-3, G-4, G-8, G-9; through the annotation tool's save_batch; finished records 224 → 222 ground truth triples): record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006", the page's ALSO_KNOWN_AS "MODIS/VIIRS Land Quality Assessment" is deleted (it is a link's text, not words of the record's own, and a WebPage is an address), and left_out.md has it; record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0", the source text of "skin SST" ESTIMATES "skin sea surface temperature (SST)" now reaches the object's words, and Q18 (e) asks whether "The first is a skin SST" states a fact; record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0", "ALICE UV Spectrometer" is "Rosetta Orbiter ALICE UV Spectrometer" in its two ground truth triples (rule 19 takes no spacecraft out of a name; its ABOARD "Rosetta Orbiter" stays, by *A name's facts*); record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3", HAS_NUMBER_OF "from 2 to 25 epochs each" is deleted (it is each quasar's count, and the quasars have no instance: rule 9) and is in left_out.md, and the source text of 2XMMi-DR3 FROM_SPACECRAFT "XMM-Newton" runs from "the catalogs of XMM-Newton" to "namely 2XMMi-DR3", one passage, since two passages of the notes are not one of rule 17's two cases for joining.

Quotes in annotations/questions_for_experts.md follow.

G-13 joins G11's corrections, above. Decided later on 2026-10-31, all as the audit recommended: see the next entry, *Facts that waited on an expert, settled, and the third audit's unsure findings*, and under 070, *Extracted triples matching facts that wait on an expert are left out of precision*. From: the third audit, 2026-10-31.
- **Facts that waited on an expert, settled, and the third audit's unsure findings (decided on 2026-10-31).** Every item of `annotations/left_out.md` that cited rule 2's "or it waits on an expert's answer" was decided, from the research of 2026-10-30 (NASA LAADS, OBPG, PDS and ESA sources), from field practice, or by me; two still wait (below). Ground truth (through the annotation tool's save_batch; finished records 222 → 229 ground truth triples; new rows have `drafted_by` "claude-opus-5.5", as the rows added in the earlier reviews):
  - "SIN Grid" (records "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid" and "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006"): the record's dataset BINNED_INTO "SIN Grid" (Grid), source text "SIN Grid" (rule 17, *A title's facts*). Why: the grid is a fact of its own, as "pixels" BINNED_INTO "integerized sinusoidal area grid (ISEAG)" already was; "SIN Grid" is the grid's name (research G3: MODLAND's "Sinusoidal Tile Grid", CMR's "MODIS Tile SIN"; no source calls "Global 1km SIN Grid" the grid's name), and rule 19 already takes "Global" and "1km" out. "in a 1,200 by 1,200 km grid" stays left out, now finally, by rule 1 (Only what the record states): that it is one tile, the ground each file covers, comes from NASA LAADS, not from the record, and no predicate holds the ground each file covers (*Missing twin predicates*, above). Q1 (c) is settled.
  - "Average" (record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0"): the record's dataset PRODUCED_USING_ALGORITHM "Average" (Algorithm), source text "Average daily, weekly (8 day)", beside its HAS_TEMPORAL_RESOLUTION "weekly (8 day)". Why: averaging names a method (rule 24), as the land surface temperature record's "the average of all qualifying observations" would if it weren't hedged (rule 7); research M12 (the OBPG: an "8 Day" value is the mean of all Level 2 data in a fixed, contiguous 8-day period) shows the 8 days are both the averaging period and the time between values, so the two triples agree and no new predicate is needed (Q5 option 1; Q5's 8-day part is settled).
  - "retrievals" (record "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005"): HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "Sea Surface Salinity (SSS)" → "Sea Surface Salinity (SSS) retrievals", by rule 24's *Words that name no method* ("It gives no ground truth triple, and it stays in the instance, as written"). Whether a retrieval is a Variable stays Q3.
  - "Earth Observation Satellites (EOS)" (record "MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0"): "Aqua" FLIES_IN "Earth Observation Satellites (EOS)" (SatelliteFormation), the same kind of triple as "A-train", with the same source text. Why: rule 11 calls it a name ("keeps "Earth""), of the group Aqua flies "formation flying … with". Q23 (spacecraft or mission) now covers both.
  - Q26, settled by me ("Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3"): the record's dataset HAS_PURPOSE "determine the ensemble variability properties of two serendipitously selected samples" (the record's words; rule 19 takes "AGN" out, a target body, already HAS_TARGET "active galactic nuclei (AGN)"; "serendipitously selected" names no method, so it stays, rule 24) and PRODUCED_USING_ALGORITHM "structure function (SF)" (rule 24). The findings are left out, by a new part of rule 8 of *What counts as a fact*, *A study a record tells* (both copies of the rules; the model's copy's fingerprint changes): "The rest of the study is not: its background, its findings, and what it says about methods it did not use." Why: the knowledge graph is for finding the catalog's datasets; schema.org's `Dataset` has `measurementTechnique` but no findings ([schema.org Dataset](https://schema.org/Dataset)), W3C DCAT has neither ([DCAT 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/)), and research knowledge graphs such as the Open Research Knowledge Graph store findings for another purpose, comparing papers (Jaradeh et al., "Open Research Knowledge Graph: Next Generation Infrastructure for Semantic Scholarly Knowledge", K-CAP 2019). The method's details: the 1.5 arcsecond matching distance and its reason ("corresponding to the uncertainty in the X-ray positions") are a setting of the matching step, left out by rule 24 (a step gives up to two ground truth triples); "cross-correlated" names no method (rule 24), as before; "taking account of the substantial overlaps" is what HAS_SKY_AREA's definition already says (rule 16).
  - G-12: "SDSS Quasar Catalog" HAS_RELEASE "DR7 edition of the SDSS Quasar Catalog" (Dataset → Dataset, as HAS_RELEASE's pin says), source text "DR7 edition of the SDSS Quasar Catalog", as "namely 2XMMi-DR3" gives its catalog's releases in the same record.
