Rule 11's exceptions now name rule 9 too. Why: saying how a fact holds needs a fact about a fact, a qualifier kept with its triple, which is future work (*Rule 19 can lose which fact a qualifier went with*, above, option 4: recommended on 2026-10-27, decided on 2026-10-30).
  - Q28 (a) to (c), settled by me: "NASA" in "global NASA skin SST products" is left out by rule 16 (by rule 19's *Whose fact it is* it qualifies the record's dataset, so its fact is who made it, which DEVELOPED_BY_PARTY "NASA Ocean Biology Processing Group (OBPG)" states more precisely; rule 21 doesn't leave it out, since the record's dataset is one of those products); "known issues" is left out by rule 9's new sentence (it says what the page's details are about), and the field's practice says no more than DOCUMENTED_AT does: W3C DCAT's `dcat:landingPage` and FOAF's `foaf:page`, "A page or document about this thing" ([FOAF](http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/)); "Lien" is left out by rule 16 (REVISED_AFTER already says the dataset was corrected after the review, and a lien is an issue the review raised, PDS's definition).

Asked for: pinned to WebPage, after `dcat:landingPage`; not done as asked, since two ground truth triples have a Document object ("LAWRENCEETAL2013", "Schneider et al. 2010, AJ, 139, 2360") and FOAF's `foaf:page` has the range `foaf:Document`, which covers both. DOCUMENTED_AT already existed (coined for tuning record #4), so nothing was coined. Translation table: DESCRIBED_IN → DOCUMENTED_AT waits for a check again (its target is narrower now).

Both settled on 2026-10-31: see *The research of 2026-10-31 on the expert questions, applied*, below.

- **The research of 2026-10-31 on the expert questions, applied (decided on 2026-10-31).** An inventory of every uncertainty the project marks (the expert questions, the left-out items, my notes) and research on it, from primary sources where they could be read; each answer was applied only as a way to read the record's words, a choice of entity class or predicate, a dated note with its source, or an item for *Knowledge enrichment from the model, verified and labelled* (080): never as a fact the record doesn't state (rule 1 of *What counts as a fact*). Ground truth (through the annotation tool's save_batch; finished records 229 → 228 ground truth triples):
  - "Global Gravity" kept whole (record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0"): the record's dataset HAS_VARIABLE "Global Gravity" (Variable), in place of HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "Gravity" (PhysicalQuantity), and HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Global" (SpatialExtent) is deleted. It reverses the decision of the same day to keep the split (research B3, *Considered and not changed*), on new evidence: the radio science team's naming document, shared by Mars Express, Venus Express and Rosetta, defines the label keyword: "Observation_Type | Mode of the executed measurement. Possible values are: Commissioning, Occultation, Target Gravity, Global Gravity, Solar Conjunction, Bistatic Radar, Phobos Gravity" (ROS-RSI-IGM-IS-3087, in the data set's [DOCUMENT/RSI_DOC](https://archives.esac.esa.int/psa/ftp/INTERNATIONAL-ROSETTA-MISSION/RSI/RO-C-RSI-1-2-3-ESC4-1149-V1.0/DOCUMENT/RSI_DOC/) folder), so "Global Gravity" is one name, and a name stays whole (rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple*); "Phobos Gravity" names the target, Phobos ([MEXMRS_2346 inst.cat](https://pds-geosciences.wustl.edu/mex/mex-m-mrs-1_2_3-ext2-v1/mexmrs_2346/catalog/inst.cat)), so a split by position would read "Phobos" as an area. The entity class is Variable, not PhysicalQuantity: by the definitions, it is gravity narrowed by a constraint, its scope (RDA's I-ADOPT: a Constraint "limits the scope of the observation and confines the context to a particular state", [ontology](https://w3id.org/iadopt/ont)), not "a bare property"; so its predicate is HAS_VARIABLE, since HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF is pinned to PhysicalQuantity. No entity class for a kind of measurement is coined (Q3's old option 5). Q3 (c) and Q11's "Global" at a comet no longer ask it. The partial-pair verdict in `annotations/partial_pair_reviews.csv` on the old "Gravity" no longer applies, so step 070 will say so (it is kept, as the verdict I gave).
  - Supporting layers (Q3 (b)): a new predicate, HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA, "holds this supporting information about its values, such as quality flags or viewing geometry", next to HAS_VARIABLE in the ground truth schema, pinned to Variable, the entity class HAS_VARIABLE takes (each layer is a property of another object, the values or the view: a Variable's "narrowed by … another object"; the CF conventions call such layers ancillary variables). Record "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006": "quality control assessments", "observation times", "view zenith angles" and "clear-sky coverages" are HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA (Variable), each HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF a PhysicalQuantity before; record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid": "view geometry" the same (its "atmospheric properties" stay HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF). Why: the field keeps them apart from what is measured: the CF conventions, section 3.4, *Ancillary Data*: "When one data variable provides metadata about the individual values of another data variable", e.g. "quality flags indicating the results of a quality control test" ([CF 1.11](https://cfconventions.org/Data/cf-conventions/cf-conventions-1.11/cf-conventions.html)); a view angle is a quantity of the viewing (CF's sensor_zenith_angle); PDS4 keeps "data that are supplementary to observational data" in a Product_Ancillary ([PDS4 data dictionary](https://pds.nasa.gov/datastandards/documents/dd/v1/PDS4_PDS_DD_1P00.html)). No row of the translation table translates to the new predicate, and HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF's meaning is unchanged; to do: check whether the current schema's PROVIDES ("supplies or produces the object, a data product, measurement, variable or information layer"), which translates to HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF, needs a conditional row for supporting layers (the annotation tool's Translation table view).
  - What the structure function "allows for" (Q9 (b), PT-1 of the inventory) and "which operates in the time domain" (Q28 (d)), record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3": final left-outs, by rule 8 of *What counts as a fact*, *A study a record tells* ("The rest of the study is not"): the method is a ground truth triple, and what it allows for and in which domain it works are details the study gives of it. Why for the first: a use is not a purpose. Wikidata keeps "has goal" (P3712, "desired result or outcome") apart from "has use" (P366, "main use of the subject"; another name of it is "can be used for"), and the OBO ontologies' objective specification is "an intended process endpoint" (IAO_0000005); HAS_PURPOSE ("is meant to achieve") is the first, and coining a predicate for a use for one record would tailor the schema to it. For the second: the field's home for it is a subject list, the Unified Astronomy Thesaurus's "Time domain astronomy", [uat/2109](http://astrothesaurus.org/uat/2109), linked in 080 (*Knowledge enrichment from the model, verified and labelled*). Q9 and Q28 are withdrawn; `annotations/pending_triples.csv` keeps its header only, and step 070's handling of facts that wait on an expert stays for the next one (its tests cover a file with its header only).
  - Organizations (Q8 (a), (b)): JPL keeps the Role "official Physical Oceanography Data Archive (PO.DAAC) for SST" (the record's words make it something JPL is; W3C DCAT's dcat:Role, "the function of a resource or agent with respect to another resource", [DCAT 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/)); that GCMD lists PO.DAAC as a provider of its own under JPL ("NASA/JPL/PODAAC") goes to 080's enrichment. The NASA Technical Standards Program stays an Organization (option 1), and the field's split is noted in Q8: schema.org's Project and W3C ORG's OrganizationalCollaboration are kinds of Organization, while the TAC KBP slot guidelines say "Programs or projects should not be considered organizations" ([TAC KBP 2014 Slot Descriptions](https://tac.nist.gov/2014/KBP/ColdStart/guidelines/TAC_KBP_2014_Slot_Descriptions_V1.4.pdf)). Q8 asks only (c).
  - An input or a method (Q7 (b)): rule 24 of *Writing a classed triple* has a new part, *Input or method* (both copies; the model's with an invented example, so its fingerprint changes): "A method is what could be applied again to other data (an algorithm, a procedure, and its settings, such as coefficients tuned per month); an input is the data the step was applied to, which its output is made from." It is the test of W3C SOSA/SSN, a procedure "is re-usable, and might be involved in many Observations", an input "Any information that is provided to a Procedure for its use" ([SSN](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-ssn/)), and of PROV-O's plan apart from what an activity used. No ground truth triple changes. Q7 (c), the corrected or calibrated words of record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0": no change; each names a documented step of the OSIRIS calibration pipeline ("Correction of solar stray light", "Correction of in-field stray light", "Radiometric calibration", "Correction of geometric distortion": *OSIRIS calibration pipeline OsiCalliope*, RO-RIS-MPAE-MA-007, in the data set's [DOCUMENT/CALIB](https://archives.esac.esa.int/psa/ftp/INTERNATIONAL-ROSETTA-MISSION/OSINAC/RO-C-OSINAC-4-ESC3-67P-M20-INFLDSTR-V1.0/DOCUMENT/CALIB/) folder), and those names are for 080's enrichment, since they are not the record's words (rule 11). Q7 asks only (a).
  - Cochran's chapter 5 (finding I-7 of the inventory): checked in the book, section 5.3, p. 91: stratification with proportional allocation "gives a self-weighting sample" (Cochran, *Sampling Techniques*, 3rd ed., 1977); `070_evaluate/metrics/approximately.md` and docs/WIP_NTRS_Iordanescu.md now cite the section, the page and the term. Its chapters 2 (the 5% rule) and 6 (the ratio bias) stay cited, marked as not verified in the book (below, under 070).
  - No rule for the whole catalog (Q2 (b)), decided as research B10 was for Planetary Data System titles: in the MODIS products' own metadata (NASA CMR, UMM-C `Platforms`) the word after "MODIS/" is the platform (MYD10_L2: Aqua; MOD11A1: Terra; MCD19A2: both), but other titles of the same products drop the slash ("MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST …") and "Aquarius L3 …" starts with the instrument, platform "SAC-D"; so it is a clue per family of titles, no new convention in rule 1. The same CMR records list "Terra" and "Aqua" as projects too: noted in Q2's context, for its (a).
  - Settled from sources, so taken out of the expert's view (B14's pattern; each question's notes keep the words and the sources): Q1 (b) (the GRaND map is a table of fixed cells), Q3 (a)'s computed quantities (CF, I-ADOPT: a method makes no new quantity; Q3 now asks only whether an index is a kind of thing of its own), Q4 (b) (the field's platform), Q5 (c) (the RDR data come only from the encounter; "entire" fits the raw data), Q6 (a) (L2G is both Level 2 on a grid and a level of its own), Q18 (a) (SST4 is a skin SST, but the record says "SST": research B9), Q25 (a) (PROV-O's agent, SOSA's platform), and all of Q9 (a: goals are stated on the spacecraft's item), Q19 (a Collection is both a version and a reprocessing), Q20 (short names name a product line; the BRF is computed from the values the record's dataset holds) and Q23 (Wikidata files the A-Train on the spacecraft). Withdrawn now: Q9, Q19, Q20, Q23 and Q28. Q18 (b) stays with the experts: one research note proposed closing it from a strong secondary source, another kept it as a reading of the record's sentence, and I kept the question.

Rule 8 of *What counts as a fact*, *A study a record tells* (both copies; the model's with an invented example, so its fingerprint changes): the details of a study's method beyond what rule 24 writes (how it was applied, its parameters, what it allows) are not facts either, as the two left-out items of record "Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3" that cite it already read it.
- **The rule gaps found by the reviews of batches 4 and 5, closed (decided on 2026-10-31).** Both copies of the rules changed (`annotations/guidelines.md`, and the model's copy `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt` with invented examples), so the prompt fingerprint changed once, before the baseline repeats. The research for each gap quotes its sources; the ones that carry a decision are below.
  - Names on an official NASA list (my decision; rule 1 of *What counts as a fact*): a name the record doesn't explain gets its entity class, and nothing else, when an official NASA list names it as that kind of thing for the record's own mission or platform (the GCMD Instruments or Platforms keywords, or the PDS4 context products); it is never expanded, and the list goes in a note of the record's section in annotations/left_out.md. The model's copy is *Names you recognize*. Why: the entity class is what a search by instrument needs, and the lists are NASA's own, so no two annotators differ; the meaning of the name stays out (rule 1). Checked on 2026-10-31: PDS4's context product urn:esa:psa:context:instrument:ro.rsi is "The Radio Science Investigations (RSI) for the Rosetta Orbiter", with an instrument_to_instrument_host reference to the Rosetta Orbiter ([the context products](https://pds.nasa.gov/data/pds4/context-pds4/instrument/)); GCMD's Instruments keywords (version 25.1) list HIRDLS, "High-Resolution Dynamics Limb Sounder", and MODIS, "Moderate-Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer", and the CMR records of H3ZFCGPH version 007 and MOD11A1 version 006 put them under the platforms Aura and Terra ([GCMD keywords](https://gcmd.earthdata.nasa.gov/kms/concepts/concept_scheme/instruments?format=csv)). It reverses the deletion of ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT "RSI" decided earlier on 2026-10-31 ("RSI" left out, above), and settles R11's "RSI" and the batch 5 review's "HIRDLS". A caution kept from *Linking subject instances and object instances to reference lists* (080): GCMD's own "RSI" is another instrument, so the list must be the one of the record's own mission or platform.
  - Membership by name (my decision; a third test in rule 1's closed list, gap 17): a statement about a group whose name the record's dataset's title or identifier contains is written for the record's dataset only when it holds for each member alone ("all", "each", or a property every member has). Why: it is the narrow test pool position 7 already used ("V6 Terra MODIS LST&E data products"); the field gives no downward rule (W3C DCAT 3 defines inheritance only upward, "property values of child datasets are inherited by their parent", [DCAT 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/), section 12.2), so the test is ours, and as narrow as rule 22's distributive "and".
  - Rule 23 as a numbered fallback order (my decision, gap 3): 1. the wording that says more; 2. otherwise the wording from a sentence that states a fact about the thing with words for its predicate; 3. otherwise the one used most often; 4. otherwise the first. Why: the second step matches the finished precedent "SST4" (the notes' words, not the title's "SST MID-IR"); Stanford CoreNLP's fixed order for choosing a representative wording would pick the title (checked in its source code on 2026-10-31), so the field has no standard for this choice.
  - Rule 22's describing words (my decision, gap 5): ask whether one of the things described can have all the describing words at once; yes keeps them whole, no splits them. It replaces the singular-or-plural test of R-B5 (above), which split "over 50 small, approximately 20 kg, stratospheric balloons" into three counts of the same balloons. Why: a plural has both readings, each item having all the words or the items sharing them out (Paillé, "Non-Boolean conjunction with atomic and plural subjects": "Predicate conjunctions are amenable to two construals"), and the question tells them apart. Every finished split and every finished whole one keeps its result; the sentence on when "data" is plural is gone, since the test no longer asks.
  - CLEANED_OF (my decision, gap 20): what a step removed from the data, or corrected in them, is written with a new predicate, and the record's words also stay in the instance (rule 24, *Words that name no method*). Why: rule 24 dropped the only words that say what was removed ("noise lines … have been edited from the data set"); ISO 19115 keeps a process step as free text and PROV-O models a removal as an invalidation, so neither gives a typed slot (research of 2026-10-31). Applied to every record that names one, finished ones included (the ground truth entry below).
  - The rest, from the field (gaps 1, 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 10 to 16, 18, 19, 21 and 23): an abbreviation set off by commas is one in parentheses, by Schwartz and Hearst's letter test (rule 20; OntoNotes' appositive "may be separated only by a comma, colon, dash, or parenthesis"); one lowercase use makes a word a type word everywhere in the record (rule 23); "approach" and "algorithm" are a method's type words (rule 23; Wikidata's labels "Kalman filter" and "Dijkstra's algorithm"); the parts of one method are a collective "and", and "both" is no sign (rule 22; Paillé: marked conjunctions "single out" the distributive reading); "raw" and "calibrated" are not processing levels (rule 19, *A processing level*; the bare word means CODMAC level 1 or 2, or NASA's Level 0, by scheme); a country is BASED_IN only of the named Person or Organization it qualifies (rule 19, *A country*; ACE ties the country to the thing it modifies); a band named inside a Variable's words gives FOR_CHANNEL (rule 17; CF's sensor_band_identifier keeps the band apart from the quantity); what a record's dataset relates to is what is connected to its id, and an explanation of a concept gives no triple (rule 21; DocRED and TACRED set the scope by a closed list of predicates, which we don't have, so the scope is the graph's purpose, rule 8's reason); a use is a fact of the work that used the data (rule 9; PROV-O records a use on the activity that used the data, prov:used); a record that contradicts itself is written as it states and the conflict quoted in left_out.md (*Finishing a record*; NASA's ARC Priority Matrix sends incorrect information back to the provider); a phrase that is half "is a" gives the fact it states besides the kind (rule 4; Wikidata keeps P31 and P361 apart); rule 7 covers what the record requires or requests (ACE's OTHER modality includes "Commanded and Requested Events"); a fact per member passes to the group only when it stays true of it (rule 9; DCAT's upward union); a word whose removal would state more is kept (rule 9; CF's valid_range is not actual_range); a fact in parentheses with a predicate is taken out, a gloss stays (rule 20; ACE tags facts stated inside parentheses), and rule 11's code-and-version exception covers only the record's dataset's own name; an amount of data is not an input (rule 24; CF's cell bounds against DCAT's temporalResolution, "the minimum spacing of items"); Grid and its predicate cover data given on a grid, renamed ON_GRID (CF keeps the cell method apart from the grid). Each changes no finished triple except FOR_CHANNEL's two (below). From: the reviews of batches 4 and 5 (2026-10-31), their rule issues; the research of rule gaps A and B (2026-10-31).
  - Gap 22 (option A, my decision): PhysicalQuantity's definition now leaves out the forms data take (images, image cubes, spectra, maps), and SpatialRepresentation covers them. Why: I-ADOPT's variable "describes WHAT has been observed", and PDS4's data dictionary files images and spectral cubes as array structures (Array_2D_Image, Array_3D_Spectrum), not as what is measured. It changes one finished triple (below).
- **Platform in place of Spacecraft, and ON_GRID in place of BINNED_INTO (decided on 2026-10-31; done with the annotation tool's rename, which changed every ground truth file, the ground truth schema and the translation table).** Platform is "a craft or station that carries Instruments, in space, in the air, on water or on the ground: a spacecraft, an aircraft, a balloon, a ship or a ground station", and FROM_SPACECRAFT is FROM_PLATFORM, "was collected from this Platform" (my decision, Q4's option 3). Why: batches 4 and 5 hold a balloon (BARREL) and an aircraft ("Tail 655"), and the field has one kind of thing for anything that carries instruments: W3C SOSA's Platform, GCMD's platform categories (Space-based, Air-based, Water-based, In Situ Land-based), and PDS4's Instrument_Host types, which list "Aircraft", "Balloon" and "Spacecraft" side by side (Q4's notes, checked on 2026-10-31). Coining Balloon and Aircraft as narrow twins, as the reviews proposed, would have needed a hierarchy later; one entity class needs none. Grid is now "a fixed set of cells or points that data values are given on", and BINNED_INTO is ON_GRID, "its values are given on the cells or points of this Grid" (rule gap 23): the finished records used it for data that are only given on a grid (the decision above on "SIN Grid"), and CF keeps how values are computed in a cell (its cell_methods) apart from the grid itself. No ground truth triple's content changed. The translation table's rows SpaceStation and Spacecraft (entity class), SOURCED_FROM (its two conditional rows) and OBSERVED_BY now translate to Platform or FROM_PLATFORM and are marked checked = no, for me to check again (*Translation table*, `docs/upkeep.md`).
- **Batches 4 and 5 finished, with the ground truth schema entries they need (decided on 2026-10-31).** The eight tuning records at pool positions 15, 16, 18, 19, 21, 22, 24 and 25 are saved through the annotation tool's save_batch and ticked finished, with no row error (step 070's evaluability gate finds none): 169 ground truth triples (10, 13, 19, 51, 8, 8, 34 and 26). The rows are the reviews' final rows, changed by every decision of the day; `drafted_by` is kept on rows that come from a draft and is "claude-opus-5.5" on rows I added. The finished records now number 20, with 404 ground truth triples.
  - The reviews' calls, as they recommended (my decisions): BARREL is a Mission, whose definition is now "a named effort to collect data, from space, from the air, on water or on the ground" (it was "a named spaceflight effort"; NASA calls BARREL a "Mission of Opportunity", and PDS4 and UMM-C keep a mission apart from a field campaign); its four campaigns are a new entity class, Campaign, each PART_OF BARREL (UMM-C's Project "also includes the Campaign sub-element", Q4's notes); the launch stations wait on Q8 (c), with no row in annotations/pending_triples.csv, since whether a station is an Organization or a place decides its entity class and predicates, and a pending row must use the ground truth schema's (the 070 gate); data caveats are HAS_ISSUE; a web page named only in words is a Document; "NASA/NOAA" stays inside the platform's instance; the Equinox Epoch's background is left out (rule 21) and "Mean of Date" is the method; HIRDLS is an Instrument (rule 1, *Names on an official NASA list*); "Tail 655" and the BARREL balloons are Platforms; the Kalman filter's two passes stay one method; a period or a duration in parentheses is taken out (rule 20); "useful" goes into HAS_VERTICAL_RANGE (rule 9).
  - New ground truth schema entries, each checked against the existing ones and against each other first: entity classes Campaign, Software, FileFormat, Unit, CoordinateSystem, EmailAddress, AccessRights; predicates MEASURES, INFORMED_BY, HAS_VERTICAL_RESOLUTION, HAS_VERTICAL_RANGE, HAS_COMPOSITING_PERIOD, OBSERVED_AT_ALTITUDE, FOR_CHANNEL, HAS_ACCESS_RIGHTS, HAS_COORDINATE_SYSTEM, HAS_CONTACT_POINT, EXTENDS, COORDINATED_WITH, HAS_FILE_FORMAT, HAS_SOFTWARE, HAS_UNIT, PRODUCTION_STARTED_ON, HAS_ISSUE, CLEANED_OF, HAS_FREQUENCY_RANGE, HAS_MASS. Each sits next to its twin, with its definition, its pin when the definition names an entity class, its rule 19 mark, and a source line naming the finished tuning records that use it. Merged before coining: Balloon and Aircraft into Platform (above); the reviews' CONTACTED_AT is HAS_CONTACT_POINT, and the names of the format, access and contact entries follow W3C DCAT (dct:format, dct:accessRights, dcat:contactPoint, [DCAT 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/)). Not coined: a use predicate (rule 9 now leaves uses out) and a minimum-mapping-unit predicate (HAS_VARIABLE fits, the batch 5 review's C6).
  - Definitions changed: PhysicalQuantity now ends "not a form the data take, such as images, image cubes, spectra or maps (a SpatialRepresentation)", and SpatialRepresentation is "a form data are laid out in", over the ground or as images, image cubes, spectra or maps (rule gap 22, option A); Orbit and SatelliteFormation say "spacecraft" in lowercase, since Spacecraft is no longer an entity class.
  - Finished records changed (each through save_batch, still finished): "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P RSI 1/2/3 COMET ESCORT 4 1149 V1.0" gets ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT "RSI" (Instrument) back (rule 1); "MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006" gets "band 31 emissivity" FOR_CHANNEL "band 31" and "band 32 emissivity" FOR_CHANNEL "band 32" (rule 17); "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0"'s HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "… image data in W/m^2/sr/nm" (PhysicalQuantity) is HAS_SPATIAL_REPRESENTATION "… image data" (SpatialRepresentation) and HAS_UNIT "W/m^2/sr/nm", and the record's dataset is CLEANED_OF "solar stray-light", "in-field stray-light" and "geometric distortion" (rule 24). annotations/left_out.md has the eight new sections, the lists behind RSI, MODIS and HIRDLS, and notes on the OSIRIS items that CLEANED_OF now writes.
  - For me to check in the translation table (marked checked = no, since their target's definition changed): entity classes Mission → Mission and PhysicalQuantity → PhysicalQuantity. Rows a new entry may now fit better, to look at (their translations are unchanged): entity classes Campaign, DataFormat (FileFormat), Aircraft (Platform) and EmailAddress, now (none), and CoordinateSystem, now MapProjection; predicates HAS_FORMAT (HAS_FILE_FORMAT), COORDINATED_WITH, HAS_CONTACT (HAS_CONTACT_POINT) and EXTENDS, now (none), HAS_COORDINATE_SYSTEM, now MAPPED_INTO, and MEASURES, now HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF (an Instrument's MEASURES has its own entry).
  - The unfinished held-out record "ROSETTA-ORBITER PRELANDING OSINAC 2 EDR MTP003 V2.0" (pool position 8) is not changed; when it is corrected, its corrections can be written with CLEANED_OF too (G11, above).
- **Wording audit of both copies of the rules (decided on 2026-10-31, my decision; wording only, no rule decides otherwise).** Every rule of `annotations/guidelines.md` and of the model's copy, `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`, reread word by word: each word means one thing (docs/terminology.md's terms); no "it", "this" or "thing" where the rule should name what it means (rule 22 no longer says "Each item is now one thing": each ground truth triple names one item); every sequence of choices is a numbered order (rule 19's *Whose fact it is* and *A country*, rule 23's *Type words* and *Capitals and punctuation*, after rules 22 and 23 of the day before); each example says its whole setup (*Suppose:* … *Then:*); and the two copies say the same thing (the model's copy named the same NASA lists in rule 1 for one day, until the entry of 2026-11-01 below; rule 7 lists "are asked to" in both copies; "are encouraged to" stays in the guidelines only, since a ground truth record says it and no prompt may quote one). The quotes of the rules in the expert questions, in `annotations/left_out.md` and in the docs follow the new wording; the entries above quote each rule as it read on the day it was decided. The model's copy changed, so the prompt fingerprint changed once more before the baseline repeats.
- **"notes", never "description" (decided on 2026-10-31, my decision).** The free text of a record's `notes` field is called the notes everywhere (docs/terminology.md, *text field*), so that one word names the field the pipeline reads; the three places that called it the description (README.md, docs/WIP_NTRS_Iordanescu.md, the review page) now say notes. Quotes kept word for word (the expert questions' old options) are not changed.
- **The double check of batches 4 and 5, applied, and one handling of each situation across the 20 finished records (decided on 2026-11-01).** A second reading of the eight records of batches 4 and 5 against the rules as written, made without the first reviews in view (findings F15.1 to F25.4; certain or probable where a rule's words decide, unsure where a judgement does), applied where the rules decide, every record through the annotation tool's save_batch and still finished, with no row error: 20 finished records, 410 ground truth triples (404 before). `drafted_by` is kept on changed rows and is "claude-opus-5.5" on added rows.
  - Rows added: "MODIS" (Instrument) ABOARD "Terra" (Platform), source text "Terra MODIS" ("MODIS/Terra Land Surface Temperature/Emissivity Daily L3 Global 1km SIN Grid V006"); the record's dataset ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT "Plasma Wave Spectrometer (PWS)", source text the title's "PWS" ("VG1 J/S/SS PWS EDITED SPECTRUM ANALYZER FULL RES V1.0", F16.1); "Visible Infrared Imaging Radiometer Suite (VIIRS)" ABOARD "NASA/NOAA Suomi National Polar-orbiting Partnership (Suomi NPP)", source text the name as written ("VIIRS/NPP BRDF/Albedo Parameter 3 Band M4 Daily L3 Global 30 ArcSec CMG V002", F18.1); for "BARREL 4G Rate Counter (RCNT) NaI Scintillator Diagnostics, Level 2, 4 s Data": HAS_COMPOSITING_PERIOD "4 s" (F19.5), "BARREL balloon" (Platform), "BARREL team" (Organization) and "BARREL instrumentation" (Instrument) each PART_OF "BARREL" with the name as source text (F19.3), and "2016 campaign" HAS_NUMBER_OF "eight flights" (F19.4); the record's dataset HAS_GRANULE_DURATION "~3 years", source text "Each file contains a zonal object with data for the entire mission" ("HIRDLS/Aura Level 3 Geopotential Height 1deg Lat Zonal Fourier Coefficients V007 (H3ZFCGPH)", F24.1).
  - Rows deleted: "South African National Antarctic Expedition" BASED_IN "South African" (F19.1; rule 11 keeps a name whole, and Country is "a nation"); "Flight Data For Tail 655" FROM_PLATFORM "Tail 655" (F22.1; rule 1); "OPERA Dynamic Surface Water Extent from Sentinel-1 (Version 1)" HAS_IDENTIFIER "DSWx-S1" (F25.1; rule 20's abbreviation test). Each has an item in `annotations/left_out.md`.
  - Source texts changed (F19.2): the three PART_OF "BARREL" rows of the 2013, 2014 and 2016 campaigns had source texts with no words for BARREL; each now runs from where the campaign is named to "Summing over the four BARREL campaigns" (rule 17: "when the subject is named only in an earlier sentence, from where it is named to the end of the fact").
  - The same situation handled the same way in all 20 finished records (my decisions, each from the rules as written; the earlier records changed too): 1. An instrument's platform stated only by a name that puts the platform's name right before the instrument's ("Rosetta Orbiter ALICE UV Spectrometer", "… (Suomi NPP) Visible Infrared Imaging Radiometer Suite (VIIRS)", "Terra MODIS") is ABOARD, with the name as source text (rule 17, *A name's facts*; rule 21); a title's "MODIS/Terra" is a "/" list of two facts of the dataset and states no ABOARD, so "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid" ("Combined Terra and Aqua" in its notes) has none. 2. A title's instrument beside the notes' component: both are written, with PART_OF between them, as "DAWN GRAND MAP VESTA FAST NEUTRON RESIDUAL V1.0" already did. 3. A country word inside a name stays in the name (rule 11); BASED_IN needs the country outside the name, as in rule 19's "the U.S. National Aeronautics and Space Administration (NASA)" ("U.S. Government" also stays whole). 4. A compositing period is written beside the temporal resolution whenever the record states both, even with the same value: two predicates, two facts (rule 16 joins only statements of one fact). 5. A parenthesised short name after the dataset's name is an Identifier only when it fails rule 20's abbreviation test ("VNP43D12", "H3ZFCGPH"); one that passes ("DSWx-S1") is the name's abbreviation and gives no ground truth triple. 6. An unexplained title name gets an entity class only from the record's words or an official NASA list (rule 1): "Tail 655" is on neither (GCMD Platforms, version 25.1, checked), so, like "HelioWeb" and "CH1-ORB", it gets none, and its record names no platform; "VG1" keeps "Voyager 1", which the notes name (rule 23). 7. A thing named "BARREL <noun>" is PART_OF BARREL by its name (rule 17, *A name's facts*, as "GRaND plus Z boron loaded sensor" PART_OF "GRaND"), which connects the balloon, the team and the instrumentation to the record's id; the policy ("BARREL Data Usage Policy") and SANAE stay unconnected: the record states no connection for the first (rule 7 leaves out "all users are expected to read and follow"), and the second waits on Q8 (c).
  - The model's rule 1 is *Names you recognize* again (my decision; `tests/allowed_differences.txt`, family rules): the model can't consult a list while it answers, so its copy says to give a name the entity class the model knows it has, write out nothing the name stands for, add no other fact from its own knowledge, and leave the name out when it doesn't know; the guidelines keep *Names on an official NASA list* for the annotator, who checks the model's entity class against the list. The wording audit of 2026-10-31 (above) had made the two copies the same for this sub-part; that change is undone.
  - Rule gaps left open that day (each needed a decision; (a) to (f) were decided later on 2026-11-01, next entry): (a) rule 17's two join cases don't cover a fact whose subject and object words are in two notes passages far apart (the campaigns' PART_OF; "(~3 years)" and "Each file contains … the entire mission"): the earlier-sentence rule gives long source texts, and a third join case would shorten them. (b) Rule 20 could keep an abbreviation the record then uses alone as the product's name ("DSWx-S1") as an Identifier, for search. (c) Rule 24 doesn't say that a noun naming the statistic computed over a period ("Average", "accumulations") names a method while a participle ("detrended", "binned") doesn't, though every finished record decides so (decided on 2026-10-31 in the review of batch 4). (d) The ground truth schema has no home for a page or site named without its address ("VNP43MA1 product page", "BARREL at Dartmouth", Documents for now). (e) Rule 17's *A name's facts* could say that a name of the form "<platform> <instrument>" states ABOARD (decision 1), so that no one infers it from the precedents. (f) A band named inside a Variable ("VIIRS band M4 (0.555 μm)") names its instrument, and the ground truth schema has no predicate from a Channel to an Instrument (the batch 4 review's I-12).
  - Considered and not changed (the double check's unsure findings, my reading of the rules): the 2016 campaign's HAS_TIME_SPAN stays "2016" ("eight flights occurred during August 2016" dates the flights, not the campaign); "BARREL instrumentation" stays an Instrument (the record names it so, and MEASURES fits); the two instruments' "to measure …" stay HAS_PURPOSE ("to measure" states the intent); the two DOCUMENTED_AT rows keep the record's dataset as subject (the sources were used for "this resource description", which describes the dataset); the coefficients' list keeps PART_OF for the mean and HAS_NUMBER_OF for the two counts ("up to 7" is a count, "the mean" is not); "Sentinel-1 radar observations" and "Copernicus Sentinel data" stay two inputs (the record's words differ, and rule 20's example writes the second); no triple says whose ephemeris the Solar Orbiter record holds (no predicate, and the title conflicts with the notes: the conflict item covers it); the grid keeps HAS_PURPOSE "use in climate simulation models" (rule 12: the relative clause is about the grid); "BARREL PI" stays the Role's wording (rule 23's test 1 ties, and it says more); the "Mean of Date" item's rule citation stays rule 4 (the table row says what the method is).
- **Five of the six rule gaps of 2026-11-01 closed (decided on 2026-11-01).** Each gap was checked against what the field does before a wording was chosen; the sources are quoted here. Both copies of the rules changed (`annotations/guidelines.md`, `common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`), so the prompt fingerprint of steps 040, 050 and 060 changes once more before the baseline repeats. Step 070 pairs triples by subject, predicate and object, not by source text (`070_evaluate/070_evaluate_helpers/pairing.py`), so (a) changes no score. Ground truth: 21 records (20 finished), 421 ground truth triples (420 before), every change through the annotation tool's save_batch, every record still finished with no row error; `drafted_by` kept on changed rows, "claude-opus-5.5" on the added row.
  - (a) Rule 17 is three numbered cases: 1. the shortest passage; 2. one passage from the earlier of two adjacent sentences to the end of the later; 3. otherwise the passages joined by " … " (*Words in different places* now lists the three join cases: the title and the notes, rule 1's tests, two notes passages with a whole sentence between them). The field's provenance for one fact is a set of short passages, never one long one: TAC KBP 2017 Cold Start allows "up to 3 docid:startoffset-endoffset triples" of "at most 200 UTF-8 characters" each ([task description](https://tac.nist.gov/2017/KBP/ColdStart/guidelines/TAC_KBP_2017_ColdStartTaskDescription_1.0.pdf)); DocRED marks "supporting evidence" as sentence numbers, and "at least 40.7% relational facts can only be extracted from multiple sentences" (Yao et al., "DocRED: A Large-Scale Document-Level Relation Extraction Dataset", ACL 2019, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/P19-1074.pdf)); ACE 2005 writes no fact across sentences at all ("We will limit Relations [relationships] to only those that are expressed within a single sentence", "ACE (Automatic Content Extraction) English Annotation Guidelines for Relations", [paper](https://www.ldc.upenn.edu/sites/www.ldc.upenn.edu/files/english-relations-guidelines-v6.2.pdf), version 6.2, §2.1), which rule 2 rules out for us. Seven source texts changed, no classed triple: the 2013, 2014 and 2016 campaigns' PART_OF "BARREL" and the 2016 campaign's HAS_NUMBER_OF "eight flights" ("BARREL 4G Rate Counter (RCNT) NaI Scintillator Diagnostics, Level 2, 4 s Data"), "skin SST" ESTIMATES "skin sea surface temperature (SST)" ("MODIS Aqua Level 3 SST MID-IR 8 Day 4km Nighttime V2019.0") and "2XMMi-DR3" FROM_PLATFORM "XMM-Newton" ("Ensemble X-Ray Variability of AGN in 2XMMi-DR3"), each now two passages joined by " … " (909, 909, 403, 362, 685 and 1,293 characters before; 80, 80, 68, 82, 60 and 135 after); and HAS_GRANULE_DURATION "~3 years" ("HIRDLS/Aura Level 3 Geopotential Height 1deg Lat Zonal Fourier Coefficients V007 (H3ZFCGPH)"), whose source text had no words for its object and now begins "the entire mission (~3 years) … " (66 characters before, 98 after). The annotation tool's message for a subject missing from the source text says the new rule (`helpers/annotator/server.py`); `docs/terminology.md`'s *source text* follows.
  - (b) Rule 20 has one exception to "Write ALSO_KNOWN_AS only when the record gives the other name in words of its own": a parenthesised short name of the thing the title names that passes the abbreviation test is written as that thing's ALSO_KNOWN_AS, since rule 23 writes the thing as its title and the abbreviation would otherwise be lost; one that fails the test is an Identifier (HAS_IDENTIFIER), as before. The field files such a short name as an alias, not an identifier: schema.org's `alternateName` is "An alias for the item" and its `identifier` "any kind of identifier for any kind of Thing, such as ISBNs, GTIN codes, UUIDs" ([alternateName](https://schema.org/alternateName), [identifier](https://schema.org/identifier)); W3C DCAT 3's `dcterms:identifier` is "A unique identifier of the resource being described or cataloged" ([DCAT 3, §6.4.11](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/#Property:resource_identifier)); ACE 2005 keeps an acronym inside the name ("Any ORG's with a corresponding acronym are labeled NAM", "ACE (Automatic Content Extraction) English Annotation Guidelines for Entities", [paper](https://www.ldc.upenn.edu/sites/www.ldc.upenn.edu/files/english-entities-guidelines-v6.6.pdf), version 6.6, §5.1.3); NASA [CMR](https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/search/collections.umm_json?keyword=OPERA%20Dynamic%20Surface%20Water%20Extent%20Sentinel-1)'s identifier of this product is another string, ShortName "OPERA_L3_DSWX-S1_V1" and DOI 10.5067/OPDSWS1-L3V1 (CMR, checked on 2026-11-01). The test stays Schwartz and Hearst's, whose algorithm skips non-alphanumeric characters, so "DSWx-S1" passes it ([PSB 2003](https://psb.stanford.edu/psb-online/proceedings/psb03/schwartz.pdf)). One row added: "OPERA Dynamic Surface Water Extent from Sentinel-1 (Version 1)" (Dataset) ALSO_KNOWN_AS "DSWx-S1" (Dataset), source text "Dynamic OPERA Surface Water Extent from Sentinel-1 (DSWx-S1)" (the pattern Dataset -> Dataset is new to ALSO_KNOWN_AS, which is not pinned); its `annotations/left_out.md` item is gone, and Q20's notes and Affects name it. Two things differ from the gap as recorded: the condition "the record then uses alone" is dropped (the loss happens whether or not the record reuses the abbreviation, and a condition on later use is one more thing to check for no gain), and the home is ALSO_KNOWN_AS, not Identifier (HAS_IDENTIFIER "DSWx-S1", deleted on 2026-11-01, stays deleted: Identifier is "a code", and an abbreviation of the name is not one). No other finished record gives the thing its title names a parenthesised abbreviation that passes the test ("(VNP43D12)", "(MCD19A2N)" and "(H3ZFCGPH)" fail it; the other parentheses abbreviate a quantity, a target, a variable, an instrument or a list).
  - (c) Rule 24's *Words that name no method* writes down the line every finished record already follows (decided on 2026-10-31 in the review of batch 4), in three numbered cases: a noun or a name in the method's place of output = method(input) names a method ("accumulations", "Average", "root-mean square"); a verb or participle that says only that something was done to the data ("corrected", "calibrated", "detrended", "binned") names none; a noun for a statistic that names a stored field ("the mean" among the coefficients) is a Variable. The field names the statistic computed over a cell as a method, by a closed list of nouns, the CF Conventions' `cell_methods` (sum, "a sum or accumulation over the cell"; mean; maximum; minimum; median; mode; range; standard_deviation; variance; root_mean_square: CF 1.11, [§7.3](https://cfconventions.org/Data/cf-conventions/cf-conventions-1.11/cf-conventions.html#cell-methods) and Appendix E), and splits what was done to the data (W3C PROV-O's `prov:Activity`, [PROV-O](https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-o/)) from the re-usable way of doing it (`prov:Plan`; W3C SOSA's `sosa:Procedure`, "A workflow, protocol, plan, algorithm, or computational method", [SSN](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-ssn/#SOSAProcedure)); it has no grammatical test. *Input or method* now names the CF list as the field's test. No triple changes; the ten `annotations/left_out.md` items that quoted the old sentence quote the new one. Open, for me to decide when a record has one (not decided either way, so that the rule is not fixed by accident): whether a participle that names a CF statistic ("values are averaged over 8 days", "summed") counts as a method, as CF would have it, or stays out, as the grammar line says. The 20 finished records have none: "smoothed or averaged out" in the HelioWeb record is a general explanation, left out by rule 21, and "temporally weighted to the ninth day" is a setting of the compositing (Q7).
  - (d) Document is "a written publication, or a web page or site, or a set of them, named in words or by a reference key (a web address written as itself is a WebPage)", and rule 20's *A page named, or given as an address* gives the three cases in order: an address alone is a WebPage; a name without an address is a Document; a link with both gives the address when it is a web address and the name when the address is a DOI. The field agrees that a page is a document and may be named without its address: DCAT 3's `dcat:landingPage` has the range `foaf:Document` ([DCAT 3, §6.4.17](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/#Property:resource_landing_page)); FOAF's `foaf:page` is "A page or document about this thing" and its Document entity class does "not (currently) distinguish precisely between physical and electronic documents" ([FOAF](http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/)); schema.org's WebPage is a CreativeWork whose `url` is optional ([WebPage](https://schema.org/WebPage)); PDS4's `External_Reference` requires `reference_text` and has no `url` ([PDS4 IM](https://pds.nasa.gov/datastandards/documents/dd/current/PDS4_PDS_DD_1N00/webhelp/all/ch03s42.html)). NASA UMM-C differs: its `RelatedUrlType` requires the URL, so a page named without one has no home there (umm-cmn JSON schema v1.18.7). No triple changes: the two rows, DOCUMENTED_AT "VNP43MA1 product page" and DOCUMENTED_AT "BARREL at Dartmouth", are Documents already. For me to check in the translation table (marked checked = no, since their target's definition changed): entity classes Document → Document and Paper → Document.
  - (e) Rule 17's *A platform in an instrument's name* writes decision 1 of the double check into the rule: in the notes, a noun phrase that names an Instrument and begins with a Platform's name states ABOARD, with the phrase as source text; a title's words, a "/" or "+" list, and the instrument's name before the platform's state none. ACE 2005 annotates a relationship stated only by a proper noun that modifies another ("PreMod": "those Relations [relationships] that are established by the construction in which a proper adjective or proper noun modifies a taggable entity [thing]", "ACE (Automatic Content Extraction) English Annotation Guidelines for Relations", [paper](https://www.ldc.upenn.edu/sites/www.ldc.upenn.edu/files/english-relations-guidelines-v6.2.pdf), version 6.2, §2.3.3); the relationship between a platform and the instrument it carries is the hosting one: W3C SOSA's `sosa:hosts`, "Relation [relationship] between a Platform and a Sensor, Actuator, Sampler, or Platform, hosted or mounted on it" ([SSN](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-ssn/#SOSAhosts)); UMM-C nests each `Instruments` list inside its `Platform`; PDS4 links them by `instrument_to_instrument_host`. No triple changes: the three rows exist ("Terra MODIS", "Rosetta Orbiter ALICE UV Spectrometer", "… (Suomi NPP) Visible Infrared Imaging Radiometer Suite (VIIRS)").
  - (f), a predicate from a Channel to its Instrument (decided on 2026-11-01; done): CHANNEL_OF, "is one of the Channels this Instrument measures in", in `annotations/ground_truth_schema.txt` beside its twin UNIQUE_TO_INSTRUMENT, with the source `ground truth tuning #18`. It coins a predicate, and the field has no standard for one (CF calls a band "a sensor's band" and records it as a coordinate of the data; UMM-C keeps an instrument's bands as a `Characteristics` entry and its parts under `ComposedOf`; W3C SSN's `ssn:hasSubSystem` is for parts), so it is not PART_OF, since the field keeps parts and bands apart. It is pinned `only: Channel -> Instrument`, while UNIQUE_TO_INSTRUMENT is pinned `any -> Instrument`: the difference is on purpose, since CHANNEL_OF's definition says "Channels". It has no `rule 19:` line: rule 19's list of qualifiers has no instrument, so it isn't a qualifier predicate. One ground truth triple, in the record at pool position 18: "VIIRS band M4 (0.555 μm)" (Channel) CHANNEL_OF "Visible Infrared Imaging Radiometer Suite (VIIRS)" (Instrument), with the source text "VIIRS band M4 (0.555 μm)" (the record's other wording of the channel, "VIIRS band M4" inside "BRDF/Albedo values for VIIRS band M4", is the same fact: rule 16). Rule 17 has a new case, *An instrument in a channel's name*, in both copies of the rules; written now (decided on 2026-11-01) because the baseline runs haven't happened yet, so the prompt fingerprint's change loses no paid run. To do: (1) run `py helpers/second_opinion.py --apply true` again, so that the new ground truth triple is checked (until then 070 counts it as not checked); (2) UNIQUE_TO_INSTRUMENT implies CHANNEL_OF (a channel found only on an Instrument is one of its channels), so a graph question "the channels of MODIS" needs both predicates: decide whether its definition should say so (no row of the translation table translates to it today, so a changed definition makes no row stale); (3) the same record's "BRDF/Albedo values for VIIRS band M4" (Variable) has no FOR_CHANNEL "VIIRS band M4 (0.555 μm)" in the ground truth, and `annotations/left_out.md` doesn't list it: probably rightly left out (rule 21: a fact of a product made from the record's dataset), to confirm and write in `left_out.md`.
- **Review unexplained codes for new catalog conventions** (from 2026-10-30). Rule 1 of *What counts as a fact* counts a code as explained only by the record's own text or by a closed list of catalog conventions ("V"/"Version" + a number: a version; "L"/"Level" + a number: a processing level). Every code left out as unexplained is in `annotations/left_out.md` with the rule quote "never write down what an unexplained code or abbreviation stands for", so searching for that quote lists them all. Review the list from time to time: a code that turns out to follow a catalog-wide convention is added to the list by a decision recorded here, and then applies to every record. Their meanings can also be looked up, with sources, by *Knowledge enrichment from the model, verified and labelled* (080).
- **Two pool records are no longer public** (from the research of 2026-10-31, F-11): the records at pool positions 163 and 754, not annotated, now answer 403 on data.nasa.gov (still in its database, not public). The project works from the harvest of 2026-10-01, so nothing changes now. If 050 ever drafts one, it should say before paying that the record's page is no longer public, so the annotator can't check it there. From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-31, *data.nasa.gov · Records that vanish, and the count today*.

## 060_extract

- **Patterns added to the prompt** (until 2026-11-15 *Patterns in or out of the prompt*; no prompt shows them since then: *No patterns and no pins in any prompt*, above): patterns were 58% of the schema text (the cached, identical part of each request was 42,450 tokens, schema, rules and examples together). Compare runs with and without them. The patterns are used nowhere else: the prompt requires only entity classes and predicates, 060 only flags an unlisted pattern (`pattern_not_in_schema`, which nothing reads), and 070 ignores them. If adding them raises partial F1 (rule of the TM, section 6.5), add them back to the prompt. If they help, replace the raw list (1,233 patterns at `min_support` 1, still growing) with a domain and a range per predicate: the subject and object entity classes seen in at least a minimum proportion of the texts, short enough to list and safe enough to enforce, as the ground truth schema's pinned predicates are. 040 may still count patterns as a diagnostic (the saturation curve). From: 060's guide, *How it works*, *The schema in the prompt* (the baseline schema of 2026-10-12); the TM review, 2026-11-04.
- **Patterns are not enforced:** 060 keeps a classed triple that joins two known entity classes with a known predicate even if the schema has no such pattern. Read 070's precision per predicate to see whether that lets in wrong facts. From: 060's guide, *Known limits*.
- **Prompt caching with Gemini is unclear:** in a test through LiteLLM, Gemini 2.5 Pro's first request reported its whole prompt as read from a cache though it had not been sent before, and the next three failed with HTTP 500. Test it again before 060 runs with Gemini (060 marks the schema for caching). Explained on 2026-10-31 (research F-1): LiteLLM turns a `cache_control` mark into Google's explicit context cache, which it creates first and then reads, so the first request reads a cache made a moment before; an explicit cache is billed for storage (about an hour by default), while Gemini 2.5's implicit caching is automatic and free for an identical start of 2,048 tokens or more ([LiteLLM, prompt caching](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/prompt_caching); [Google, context caching](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/caching)). Decided on 2026-10-31: Gemini gets no cache mark. Checked in the code that day: it gets none already, since `_messages` (`common/common_helpers/llm_litellm.py`) marks the cached start only for Anthropic's models, so 060 with Gemini sends no mark (the parenthesis above was wrong for Gemini), and 050 passes no `cache_until` at all. Nothing to change; keep it so. From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-12, *Caching inside the user message, and Gemini*.
- **Raise With Mentors: loosening 060's source text check.** Read the classed triples 060 removed with the reason `source_text` (070's report now lists what 060's checks removed, per reason); if many state true facts, loosen the matching in `common/common_helpers/text_match.py`. From: [`metrics/recall.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/recall.md), row 2.
- **Experiment: the source text first in the model's answer.** The reply asks for subject, predicate, object, and their entity classes, then the source text last, so the model states a fact and finds its passage afterwards. Asking for the passage first, then the fact, may give facts that keep closer to the text. A model that thinks before answering (claude-opus-5.5 does) may gain less. From: Negro, Futia, Kůs, and Montagna, *Knowledge Graphs and LLMs in Action* (Manning, 2026), section 14.7.1.
- **An entity class or predicate that does badly:** try renaming it first (a generic entity class or predicate gave unstable results there; a more specific one fixed it). From: Negro, Futia, Kůs, and Montagna, *Knowledge Graphs and LLMs in Action* (Manning, 2026), section 5.3.3.
- **One LiteLLM session per 060 run (done on 2026-10-31, research F-2).** LiteLLM's default routing picks a deployment at random among those that share a model name, and its docs advise session affinity "when the deployments behind a model group do not share state, for example provider-side prompt caching"; "The proxy reads it from the `x-litellm-session-id` header" ([LiteLLM, routing](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/routing), read 2026-10-31). That fits the writes and reads of 2026-10-12 ([`findings.md`](findings.md)). 060 now sends that header with every request of a run, its value the run's id, recorded in the run's record (`session_id`, in `made`) and in the report's *Model calls*; the words the model gets, the settings, the cache key and every answer's fingerprint are the same (`tests/test_model_calls.py`). No other step sends it: 060 is the only step whose requests share a cached start. Whether it takes effect depends on the proxy having session affinity on (question 4 for the administrators, above). To do: compare the cache reads in the next 060 report's *Model calls* with earlier runs'.
- **Prompt size and cost:** the 060 run that 070 run 2026-10-22_1615 evaluated has its tokens in its report (section *Model calls*), on the NASA laptop. Record them in [`findings.md`](findings.md), with the schema's share of each prompt. From: 2026-10-12.
- **The content filter:** in step 040 the service refused phrases from GeneLab (space biology) records. Watch whether 060's failed records cluster there. Checked on 2026-10-31 (research F-4): Claude Opus 5.5 runs Anthropic's biology safety classifier, whose refusal is an HTTP 200 reply with `stop_reason` "refusal", which LiteLLM reports as `content_filter`; a refusal of the category "bio" is billed even before any output ([Refusals and fallback](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/refusals-and-fallback)), and asking the same request again "usually results in another refusal". So a refused record costs a billed call. To do: 060 already records the service's own stop reason where LiteLLM passes it on (`native_finish_reason` in the failed-call record); whether LiteLLM passes Anthropic's `stop_details` category on is question 5 for the administrators (above). From: [`findings.md`](findings.md), 2026-10-12.
- **A count without what is counted (from 2026-11-02; done on 2026-11-13 as a rule, rule 18: "A count with the noun for what is counted is such an amount", so its effect alone is not measured):** in the final re-score of the six runs, extraction wrote HAS_NUMBER_OF with the number alone in 7 of the 13 new partial pairs, e.g. "Flight Data For Tail 655" HAS_NUMBER_OF "186" where the ground truth triple says "186 parameters". I decided each "not the same fact" ("has this many" needs what is counted), so these facts count as missed. Add to the extraction rules that a count keeps the noun it counts, then compare runs. From: `annotations/partial_pair_reviews.csv`, 2026-11-02.
- **A fixed schema for the full run,** chosen and kept in Git, so that the full run doesn't depend on whatever `outputs/` holds. From: 2026-10-12.
- **Extraction stops short on long records:** in the first evaluation (070 run 2026-10-22_1615, 6 tuning records), 33 of the 73 missed ground truth triples were within reach of the current schema. Counted by subject instance: 20 of them have a subject instance other than the record's title, i.e. facts about things other than the dataset (an algorithm's purpose, the channels a variable is computed from, an input's DOIs, an instrument's parts), and 13 have the record's title as their subject instance, i.e. dataset facts simply skipped. Record 03a09af3 (pool position 4, MODIS Aqua SST) alone has 15: its model call finished normally (status extracted, no error, 1 text piece) with 11 extracted triples (10 besides its describes triple), against 48 ground truth triples in `annotations/ground_truth/batch_000.csv` (47 besides its describes triple), so it was not cut off. To try, one change at a time and measured against this baseline: a prompt instruction to state the facts about every thing the record names, not only the dataset; splitting long records into more text pieces (lower the setting `max_chars`, default 8,000, below the record's text length). Two more, from TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3): (a) first list every thing the record names, then state the facts among them, as its extraction does (sections 4.2 and 4.4); the evidence is its design, not a measured comparison (its ablation, appendix C.4, doesn't test it). Keep rule 21 of *Writing a classed triple* ("General facts: include them, unless they're about another dataset that the record's dataset isn't made from or part of"). Test it first with an edit to 060's own prompt only, since the reply format, `common/common_prompts/extraction_reply.txt`, is shared with 040 and 050. Done on 2026-10-30 for the prompt instruction above and (a), together: 060's prompt (`060_extract/060_extract_prompts/extract.txt`) now always asks for two passes, first every thing the record names, then the facts about each, TRACE-KG's order of work, in one request, with the reply format unchanged (060's guide, *Listing the things first*). It replaced the setting `list_things_first` (2026-10-25), which made this optional, because a prompt is changed directly and not by an on/off setting; the decision of 2026-10-30 was to make every improvement first, then measure them together with repeat runs (*Repeat runs, to measure run-to-run noise*, above). Not yet tried with the real model. Its effect shows mainly in recall within reach, compared with 070 run 2026-10-22_1615 (`py helpers/compare_runs.py`), though the rules changed on 2026-10-30 too, so not all of a difference can be put down to it. Since 2026-10-30, 050's drafting prompt asks for the same two passes and the same *completeness first* instruction as 060's, from one shared file, `common/common_prompts/extraction_passes.txt` (decided on 2026-10-30, from the review of the prompts: the ground truth is drafted the way 060 extracts; 060's prompt gained the *completeness first* sentence that 040's and 050's had). (b) Smaller text pieces, each sent with the pieces before it as context only: it cuts texts into pieces of at most 350 tokens (`max_tokens_per_chunk`, `TKG_Main.py`, line 374) and shows the model the pieces before each one (sections 4.1 and 4.2; 1 by default, line 809; 5 in its run, line 11967). Without that context, smaller pieces break rule 23 ("One thing, one wording, within a record": a thing named in full in one piece and shortened in the next gets two wordings) and rule 16 of *Writing a classed triple* ("Each fact once": a fact stated in two pieces in different words is extracted twice). From: 2026-10-22; TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3), for (a) and (b).
- **Duplicates and quantities (done on 2026-10-31, edge-case audit, groups G2 and G3: `triple_key` now uses `instance_key`, so 070 stops on such a pair in the ground truth until one is deleted; the item as it was:):** 060's `duplicate` check and the ground truth's [the same classed triple twice](terminology.md#4-ground-truth-and-samples) still compare subject instances and object instances as words (`common/common_helpers/triples_io.py`, `triple_key`), while 070 now compares quantities as quantities. So "1 km" and "1 kilometer (km)", with the same predicate and entity classes in one record, are two extracted triples for 060 but one element of 070's multiset, with multiplicity 2. Decide whether `triple_key` should use `instance_key` too; for the ground truth, that would make 070 stop on such a pair until one is deleted. From: 2026-10-24.
- **Run 060 on a public ground truth, to tell a weak extraction from hard records:** run 060 (the same model, prompts and extraction rules) on the texts of WebNLG [public short texts, each paired with triples written for it, whose predicates are the DBpedia ontology's] (Castro Ferreira et al., WebNLG+ 2020, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/2020.webnlg-1.7)), with the DBpedia ontology's predicates as the schema [the DBpedia ontology: a public schema of about 768 entity classes and about 3,000 predicates, [its page](https://www.dbpedia.org/resources/ontology/)]; score the result with WebNLG's own scoring program, and compare it with the scores published methods report on the same texts, e.g. EDC [a method that extracts triples, defines their predicates, then canonicalizes them into a schema] (Zhang & Soh, EMNLP 2024, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.548)). What it tells: whether low recall on the catalog's records comes from the extraction step or from the records being hard. Limits: the extraction rules are written for catalog records, so the comparison isn't perfectly fair; it needs some code to feed the texts to 060 and to convert the triples; and it makes paid calls for a few hundred texts. When: after the demo. From: discussion of 2026-10-26.
- **Baselines on the same records: the ground truth schema as 060's schema, and a fixed schema from GCMD (from 2026-11-01).** Why: every number so far compares the pipeline with itself. The papers' central claim, that the recall upper bound separates what the induced schema can't state from what extraction misses, is tested directly by extracting with the ground truth schema: the difference is the price of inducing the schema, measured. The extraction papers cited here all have a baseline: Text2KGBench reports two models on its task (Mihindukulasooriya et al., ISWC 2023, [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.02357)); EDC evaluates the triples it extracts with no schema before it canonicalizes them (Zhang & Soh, EMNLP 2024, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.548/), section 4.1.1). Smallest version: the ground truth schema run, whose code is done (*The comparison run translated by identity*, under 070; the commands: `running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *Comparing with the ground truth schema*), on the finished records, compared with the baseline by `--across_schemas true` (shown, not decided: the decision rule doesn't mix translations); then a fixed schema written from GCMD's keyword categories (Platforms, Instruments, Projects, Science Keywords, Locations, Data Centers), the controlled vocabulary of NASA's CMR metadata ([Keyword FAQ](https://wiki.earthdata.nasa.gov/spaces/CMR/pages/71139630/Keyword+FAQ)): 6 to 8 entity classes, 8 to 12 predicates, one 060 run, a translation table of about 20 rows; a run with no schema (the extraction rules only) if time allows. Effort: about 1 day for the ground truth schema run, 1 more for the GCMD schema. Model cost: 20 to 40 record extractions each. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: yes. From: the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap B.
- **Experiments that change one part, with a budget (from 2026-11-01).** Why: this list has the experiments (the two-pass instruction on or off, *Patterns in or out of the prompt*, *Quote first* and *A vote over the baseline's repeats* under *Future experiments on the prompts*, `min_support` under 040) and their order (the noise floor first), but no budget, so none may happen before 2026-11-20. Smallest version: with the baseline's repeats as the noise floor, exactly two, on the finished records, 3 repeats each, each decided by the decision rule (*The decision rule for "did a change help"*, under 070): (a) the two-pass instruction off; (b) the patterns out of the prompt (the one that changes the cost most). Effort: 1 day at the keyboard. Model cost: 6 × the finished records' extractions. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: yes. From: the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap I.
- **Extraction with a model of a second maker (OpenAI), 3 repeats, for the papers (from 2026-11-01).** Why: every extraction so far is by one model of one maker (Anthropic's `claude-opus-5-5`); a second maker shows the method isn't tied to one model, and the field compares several (Text2KGBench, above; EDC, with GPT-4, GPT-3.5-turbo and Mistral-7b). Not Google's `gemini-2.5-pro`: step 050 drafts with it, so extracting with it inflates recall (`helpers/compare_runs.md`, *Making two recipes to compare*); OpenAI is a maker none of the ground truth's drafts come from. Smallest version: find which OpenAI models NASA's LiteLLM lists (not known yet), then `py 060_extract/run.py --service litellm --model <that model> --repeat 1` (then 2, 3), each followed by `py 070_evaluate/run.py`, on the finished records, and compare with the baseline's repeats by the decision rule. Effort: half a day at the keyboard. Model cost: 3 × the finished records' extractions. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: yes. From: my request of 2026-11-01; the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap I, which proposed a second extraction model.

## 070_evaluate

- **Rows accepted by spelling alone: five whose match may not be `same` (found on 2026-11-09; for the TP, not changed yet).** Step 070 adds a row by itself when a component class of the current schema is spelled like one of the ground truth schema, with `checked` = `same component class` and `match` = `same`. 26 rows of `annotations/component_class_mapping.csv` were accepted this way; for 3 (PhysicalQuantity, Person, Document) the match was later changed, and for the other 23 the file does not record that anyone compared the two definitions. Claude Opus 5.5 compared them on 2026-11-09 (my reading of the definitions, not checked by a person): 18 agree, and 5 may not be `same`: Mission (narrower: the induced definition covers space and flight only, the ground truth's also water and ground), Task (broader: the induced definition also covers objectives, which the ground truth schema puts under Purpose), ProcessingLevel (broader: the induced definition also covers Near Real Time, which the ground truth schema puts under Latency), HAS_IDENTIFIER (broader: the induced definition also covers a dataset that refers to items by a key, such as a table's primary key), and HAS_LATENCY (broader: the induced definition also covers an update delay). A match changes no translation and no pair, so no reported number changes except the part of the recall upper bound through `same` or `narrower` rows (67.5% in the TM): the 4 HAS_IDENTIFIER and 1 HAS_LATENCY ground truth triples of the 20 finished records could move out of it, to about 66.3% at most. To do before the TP's runs: check the five rows in the annotation tool, and score the six runs again (no model call). A test that would make such judgments repeatable, matching by instances (the share of a component class's extracted instances that fit the target's definition, with a share fixed in advance for a main meaning), is a possible later improvement. From: the discussion of 2026-11-09.
- **The translation table as two functions, and per-entry coverage (2026-11-09; the doc done, the report to do).** [`070_evaluate/metrics/translation_table.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/translation_table.md) is now the one home of what the table does to an extracted triple: the functions T<sub>E</sub> and T<sub>P</sub>, the rule that picks a predicate's row, the swap, what `(none)` does, their properties, the four kinds of match stated with sets, and per-entry coverage; the terminology's *match* and *conditional row* point there. Per-entry coverage (the proportion of the ground truth schema's entries that some checked row reaches, adapted from TRACE-KG's schema coverage) was computed by a script from step 070's own translation, with no model call, on the reported runs: 43 of the 84 predicates that the ground truth triples of the 20 finished records use (51.2%), 39 through a same or narrower row (46.4%); 30 of the 44 entity classes (68.2%), 19 (43.2%). To do: print it in step 070's report beside the list *Ground truth schema's component classes nothing translates to*, at both choices of the entries (the whole ground truth schema, and the entries the evaluated ground truth triples use), so that it no longer needs a script. The Technical Publication's section 6.1 should state the translation in this page's wording. From: the decision of 2026-11-09.
- **Two (none) rows that were wrong: HAS_SOURCE_COUNT and HAS_LAUNCH_COUNT (found and changed on 2026-11-05, my decision).** A sorting of the 71 rows of `annotations/component_class_mapping.csv` that translate to `(none)`, by Claude Opus 5.5 and not checked by a person, finds that these two count predicates of the current schema fit HAS_NUMBER_OF of the ground truth schema, by the table's own rule (a broader target when no target has the same meaning). Extraction writes counts as HAS_SOURCE_COUNT, so the row may move ground truth triples into reach and change the results. Changed on 2026-11-05 to HAS_NUMBER_OF, match `broader`, as the table's rule requires; the six runs were scored again for free (`py helpers/reproduce_results.py`): partial F1 52.3% → 53.0%, schema fit 85.6% → 87.2%, the recall upper bound unchanged at 74.3%. The changed rows made six new partial pairs; five join a count to its number alone ("412 quasars" and "412"), judged not the same fact like the seven such pairs before (my rule for counts), and one ("~40 minute" and "~40 minute period") the same fact. Scored again with these verdicts: partial F1 51.9%, precision 58.8%, recall 46.4%, schema fit 87.2%. A row changed after tuning results are seen is disclosed in the TM's limits (section 8.4, *Translation table*), with the earlier conditional rows of ACQUIRED_BY and COVERS. The sorting: `docs/analysis/none_rows_sorting.csv` (categories: divided meaning 25 predicates and 5 entity classes, left out by a rule 15 and 2, no ground truth counterpart 14 and 5, no stable meaning 2 and 3). From: my review of 2026-11-05.

- **Scoring again with no model budget (done on 2026-11-02).** My LiteLLM budget was used up on 2026-11-02, and the six existing runs had to be scored again after the translation table and the partial-pair verdicts changed; but 070's suggestion call for `(none)`, broader and overlap rows had a new prompt since e32a282, so its cached answer no longer matched and any rerun would have called the model. Now `py 070_evaluate/run.py --suggest false` skips that call (the report and `metrics.json` say *not asked (--suggest false)*), makes no model call at all (a call would stop the run, `forbid_model_calls`), and stops before paying when a component class of the current schema has no row. Suggestions never change a row, so the metrics are the same either way; the tests check it (`tests/test_pipeline.py`, `tests/test_match_kinds.py`). Docs: `070_evaluate/070_evaluate.md` (*Settings*, `suggest`), `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md` (*Re-scoring without budget*).

- **The partial pairs of several runs, reviewed through Git (2026-11-01; done).** The annotation tool's *Partial pairs* view shows only the latest 070 run's pairs, while the baseline's three repeats (070 runs `070_evaluate_2026-11-01_1644`, `_1701`, `_1706`, on the NASA laptop) each left different ones unreviewed (22, 23, 14), and the outputs stay on that laptop. `py helpers/partial_pairs.py --runs <070 run ids>` collects every partial pair of their tuning records with no verdict, once per verdict key, into `annotations/partial_pairs_to_review.csv` (in Git), each run's `compared_triples.csv` found, live or under `previous_runs/`, by the sha256 its manifest records; `--apply true` moves the filled verdicts into `annotations/partial_pair_reviews.csv` (only the two values, the filed cells unchanged, no conflict, or nothing moves); `--delete_stale true --runs <070 run ids>` deletes the verdicts that match no partial pair of any run given, by 070's own test (`pairing.verdict_keys`). Docs: `helpers/partial_pairs.md`, `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md` (*Reviewing the partial pairs of several runs*), 070's guide and its two warnings, `annotations/README.md`; tests: `tests/test_partial_pairs.py`.
- **Shared-model bias in every number it can affect, by who drafted the ground truth and by the second opinion (decided on 2026-11-01; done).** Step 070 gave only recall, precision and F1 per group of the second opinion, with no margin of error. A model that drafted the ground truth and extracts too can share its blind spots (inflating recall), its mistakes (inflating precision) and its phrasing (inflating exact pairs and entity-class accuracy) with the extraction (Kim, Garg, Peng & Garg, "Correlated Errors in Large Language Models", ICML 2025; Fort & Sagot, "Influence of Pre-annotation on POS-tagged Corpus Development", LAW IV, 2010). Now each part's *Metrics* has *By the second opinion* (found, not found, not checked) and, next to it, *By who drafted the ground truth* (one group per model in `drafted_by`, every spelling of one name together, as `same_model_name` compares them, so `claude-opus-5.5` and `claude-opus-5-5` are one group and `claude-opus-5` another; one group for an empty `drafted_by`). Per group: ground truth triples, records, recall at both levels, the exact share (exact pairs ÷ pairs), entity-class accuracy, attributed precision (the group's pairs ÷ (counted occurrences − the pairs of the other groups): an attribution, not a true per-group precision, since an unpaired occurrence belongs to no group) and F1, each with its clustered Wilson interval over the records (the groups split the same records, so the ranges aren't independent). Both from one function (`stats.py`, `by_group`) over each record's group counts (`pairing.py`, `group_counts`), in `metrics.json` (`numbers.second_opinion`, `numbers.drafted_by`). The pairings of steps where shared-model bias can enter, what each can inflate, what guards it and what the reports measure have one home: `070_evaluate/070_evaluate.md`, *Shared-model bias*, linked from the guides of 040, 050 and 060, `helpers/second_opinion.md`, `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md` (*Choosing a model*), the metric docs and `docs/terminology.md` (*shared-model bias*). Not done: a table crossing the two groupings (the second opinion's own report counts found and not found by `drafted_by`). The empty `drafted_by` group, which held rows a model first proposed before `drafted_by` was recorded, is gone from the ground truth since 2026-11-01 (*Every ground truth row names its drafting model*, under 050_annotate). The evaluation version is unchanged: no pair and no overall number changes. From: the decision of 2026-11-01.
- **Shared-model bias in the NASA TM's method and its limitations (done on 2026-11-02: the TM's sections 4.1, 5.4 and 8.3 name each step's model, the pairs that share a model or a maker, and the numbers by who drafted the ground truth; the item as it was:) (from 2026-11-01).** The method should say which model each step uses and which pairs of steps share a model or a maker, and that step 070 reports every number by who drafted the ground truth and by the second opinion. The limitations should name all five channels of shared-model bias: (1) recall, from shared blind spots; (2) precision, from shared mistakes; (3) the exact share and entity-class accuracy, from shared phrasing; (4) the schema: the current schema induced by the extraction model's family (step 040), and the ground truth schema grown while I reviewed drafted triples, so it may carry the drafting models' component classes; (5) the translation table, whose rows step 070's model proposes and I check, which moves schema fit, the recall upper bound and recall within reach. For each, what guards it and what is measured (`070_evaluate/070_evaluate.md`, *Shared-model bias*), with the second opinion's weaker independence from the Gemini-drafted rows (same maker), and the TM's references [8] and [10] (Fort & Sagot 2010; Kim et al. 2025). From: the decision of 2026-11-01.
- **The decision rule for "did a change help" (decided on 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01; done).** One run of a change against one baseline run, or a number read against the width of the baseline's repeats, has no stated chance of a wrong call, and among the about 30 numbers of a comparison some differ by chance. Now `py helpers/compare_runs.py --baseline <run ids> --changed <run ids>` applies one rule fixed before the runs: partial F1 alone decides (the deciding metric: *Untranslatable extracted triples left out of precision*, below), the other numbers are shown, not tested. Each record's counts are averaged over each recipe's repeats, *D* is the difference of the two recipes' pooled partial F1, and an exact paired permutation (sign-flip) test over records gives a two-sided *p*: every one of the 2^*n* swaps of the two recipes' counts on some of the *n* records up to 20 records, 100,000 random swaps from a fixed seed above. The verdict is *helped* (*D* > 0 and *p* ≤ 0.05), *worse* (*D* < 0 and *p* ≤ 0.05) or *can't tell apart*. The report also gives the smallest *p* the test can give on its records, 1 ÷ 2^(*n* − 1), so below 6 records it says that no verdict but *can't tell apart* is possible. It stops on runs evaluated under different evaluation versions, translated in different ways, or not all on the same tuning records.
