Home: `helpers/compare_runs.md`, *Did a change help?*; tests: `tests/test_evaluate_pairing.py`, *DecisionRule*. Not built: the research's other parts (a run-noise comparison beside *D*, a "not worse" rule for a change kept for other reasons). From: the research of 2026-10-31 on repeated runs (its protocol and decision rule), after Dror, Baumer, Shlomov & Reichart, "The Hitchhiker's Guide to Testing Statistical Significance in Natural Language Processing", ACL 2018 ([paper](https://aclanthology.org/P18-1128/)), and Miller, "Adding Error Bars to Evals: A Statistical Approach to Language Model Evaluations", 2024 ([arXiv:2411.00640](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00640)).
- **The comparison run translated by identity (decided on 2026-11-01; done).** 060 with the ground truth schema as its schema could not use the shared translation table: its rows are keyed by name, 40 of them share a name with the ground truth schema, and 8 of those translate elsewhere (e.g. MEASURES → HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF), so 070 would have stopped on stale rows, and checking them again would have changed the rows the baseline's repeats use. Now 070 recognizes such a run (060's `schema_used.json`, `made.ground_truth_schema`, and the same entity classes and predicates with the same definitions as the ground truth schema now) and translates every component class to itself in memory, without reading the table; it stops if the ground truth schema changed after 060 ran. Its report, `metrics.json` and recipe say "translation: identity (060 used the ground truth schema)", and `py helpers/compare_runs.py` refuses to mix it with runs through the table unless `--across_schemas true`. Also: the ground truth schema counts as learned from every finished tuning record (it was "pool positions 0 to 5", out of date since the schema grew with every record), and a record is never its own twin (in the comparison run, 18 of the 20 evaluated records would have been twins of themselves). The commands: `running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *Comparing with the ground truth schema*. From: the pre-run review of 2026-11-01 (S2, S3).

- **A `(none)` row that now exists needs checking (decided on 2026-11-01; done).** Five rows translated to `(none)` though the ground truth schema had since coined an entry of the same name (Issue, HAS_IDENTIFIER, HAS_LATENCY, HAS_MASS, HAS_VERTICAL_RANGE; fixed in the table that day), because the row state *now exists* was only a warning in 070's report and the annotation tool didn't count it as to check. Now step 070 stops on it (naming the file, the line and the entry), the tool counts and filters it among the rows to check, and the status lists it. Keeping `(none)` on purpose (e.g. AVAILABLE_AT → `(none)` beside its conditional row for an object of entity class URL) is ticking *Checked* again, which stores that entry's definition in `definition_in_gtt`: the row is then settled, and stale if that entry is redefined or deleted (`terminology.md`, *row state*). From: the pre-run review of 2026-11-01 (S1).

- **Untranslatable extracted triples left out of precision, and schema fit as its own number (decided on 2026-11-01; done).** An occurrence whose predicate translates to `(none)` through the row that applies to it (an [untranslatable extracted triple](../070_evaluate/metrics/schema_fit.md)) can pair with no ground truth triple, right or wrong, so it counted against precision though the ground truth schema can't say it. Step 070 now leaves it out of precision's and F1's denominators (partial F1 too), and reports schema fit, (occurrences − untranslatable extracted triples) ÷ occurrences, with its margin of error, beside precision (`070_evaluate/metrics/schema_fit.md`). Only the predicate decides (my decision of 2026-11-01): an entity class whose row says `(none)` doesn't make an occurrence untranslatable, since the fact can still be stated and paired, and only strictness is lost; EDC leaves out the predicates with no counterpart in the target schema, and the entity classes only affect strictness here. The definition of the deciding metric (partial F1) changed before any look at the held-out part (`annotations/held_out_looks.csv` held only its header): partial F1 rises wherever there are untranslatable extracted triples, compared with evaluation version 2, by design. `pairing.EVALUATION_VERSION` is 3, and `py helpers/compare_runs.py` refuses to compare runs of different versions. From: Zhang & Soh, EDC (2024, [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03868)), and Mihindukulasooriya et al., Text2KGBench (ISWC 2023, [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.02357)), quoted in [`metrics/schema_fit.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/schema_fit.md#sources).

- **Extracted triples matching facts that wait on an expert are left out of precision (decided on 2026-10-31; done).** My guidelines leave a fact out of the ground truth while it waits on an expert's answer (rule 2 of *What counts as a fact*: "or it waits on an expert's answer"), but the model's copy of the rules asks for every fact the record states, so step 070 counted the model wrong where it followed its own rules (the third audit of the rules, 2026-10-31, finding R-14). Now each such fact with a clear would-be ground truth triple is a row of `annotations/pending_triples.csv`, checked against `annotations/left_out.md`. After pairing with the ground truth triples, 070 pairs the occurrences still without a partner with the record's own pending triples, by the same passes. Those it pairs are left out of precision's denominator (and F1's), at each pair level; recall is unchanged, since a pending fact is not a ground truth triple. The report lists them (*Extracted triples matching facts that wait on an expert*) and states the formula; the recipe records that the exclusion was applied, how many pending triples there were and their fingerprint, so `helpers/compare_runs.py` shows when runs differ in it. Precision now sits between its value with those occurrences counted wrong and with them counted right, until the experts answer. One gap: a partial pair with a pending triple is in 070's per-record page and `compared_triples.csv`, but the annotation tool's *Partial pairs* view doesn't show it yet. Since 2026-10-31 the file holds its header only (both facts that waited were settled: see *The research of 2026-10-31 on the expert questions, applied*, under 050); the handling stays, for the next fact that waits. From: the third audit, 2026-10-31, finding R-14.
- **Translation that depends on the entity classes (done on 2026-10-30, as conditional rows; its 14 proposed rows were checked on 2026-10-31):** a row of the translation table translated a predicate one way only, whatever the entity classes of its subject and object (070's guide, *Known limits*, *One translation per component class*). Let a row translate a predicate of the current schema according to its subject's entity class, its object's entity class, or both: rows for (subject's entity class, predicate), (predicate, object's entity class), and (subject's entity class, predicate, object's entity class), besides the predicate alone as now. The most specific row that matches wins: all three over either pair, either pair over the predicate alone. When a (subject's entity class, predicate) row and a (predicate, object's entity class) row both match and disagree, 070 stops and names both rows, so a person adds the row with all three. These keys have the shape of the current schema's patterns (e.g. [Dataset, SOURCED_FROM, Mission]), so the rows can be listed from the patterns. Example: SOURCED_FROM (current schema) translates to (none) today; with rows, SOURCED_FROM with a Mission object would translate to FROM_MISSION, with a Spacecraft object to FROM_PLATFORM (ground truth schema), and otherwise to (none). In record 2705e0d1 (pool position 3), the extracted triple "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005" (Dataset) SOURCED_FROM "Aquarius/SAC-D" (Mission) is a true fact, counted extracted only; with the (SOURCED_FROM, Mission) row it would translate to FROM_MISSION, the predicate of the ground truth triple "Aquarius L3 Weekly Polar-Gridded Sea Surface Salinity V005" (Dataset) FROM_MISSION "Aquarius/Satélite de Aplicaciones Científicas (SAC-D)" (Mission). Changes 070, the translation table's format, and the annotation tool's Translation table view. From: 2026-10-24. Decided on 2026-10-30 in place of a larger induction sample, on this evidence: 12 of the 13 records of the ground truth have maintainers among the 10 that step 040 learned from; 040's saturation curve gave 2.8 predicates for its last 25 texts; and 69 of the 104 predicates of the current schema translate to `(none)`, many because their meaning splits. Projection, not a result: with the 14 proposed rows checked as proposed, 7 more of the 106 ground truth triples of the 6 tuning records of run 2026-10-22_1615 would be within reach (recall upper bound 73 of 106, 0.69, instead of 66 of 106, 0.62), counted from that run's `per_record.md`; the ground truth has changed since. Credit: EDOAL, the Alignment API's "Expressive and Declarative Ontology Alignment Language" (Scharffe, Euzenat & Zimmermann; [language description](https://moex.gitlabpages.inria.fr/alignapi/edoal.html)), whose restrictions `RelationDomainRestriction` and `RelationCoDomainRestriction` limit a correspondence [a row] to one entity class of its subject or of its object; such correspondences are what complex ontology matching produces (Thiéblin, Haemmerlé, Hernandez & Trojahn, "Survey on complex ontology matching", *Semantic Web* 11(4), 2020, [paper](https://doi.org/10.3233/SW-190366)).
- **Raise With Mentors: checking a sample of the occurrences left extracted only,** to see how far precision and recall are understated ([`metrics/pairs.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/pairs.md), *The same fact in different words*). It's an involved process: such an occurrence can be the same fact in different words as a ground truth triple left ground truth only, a repeat of a fact already paired, a fact the ground truth lacks, the result of a wrong translation, or actually wrong, and each means something different. From: 070's guide, *To do*, until 2026-10-14; now [`metrics/precision.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/precision.md) and [`metrics/recall.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/recall.md), row 4 of each; the literature check of 2026-10-26, for why it matters: Tan et al. (2022), "Revisiting DocRED – Addressing the False Negative Problem in Relation Extraction" [a fact the ground truth lacks; relation extraction], EMNLP 2022 ([doi:10.18653/v1/2022.emnlp-main.580](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2022.emnlp-main.580)): after the facts missing from DocRED's ground truth were added, models' F1 rose by about 13 points. Extended on 2026-11-01: **every one counted, by kind.** Why: the kinds above are named but never counted; Text2KGBench sorts its mistakes into invented subject instances, predicates and object instances (Mihindukulasooriya et al. 2023, section 4), and the re-annotations of DocRED (Tan et al. 2022, above; Huang et al. 2022, under *About 10 records annotated from scratch*) show that facts the ground truth lacks can move F1 by about 13 points. Smallest version: on the baseline's 070 run of the finished records, every extracted-only occurrence and every ground truth-only triple sorted by me into a fixed list of at most 8 kinds (those above, and for a ground truth-only triple whether it was within reach), with 2 examples each; precision and recall computed again counting the same fact in other words as a pair, beside the numbers as they are; and recall split by who drafted each ground truth triple (`drafted_by`: the model named there, or empty), at no cost. Effort: 1 to 2 days, by hand. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: no (070's saved outputs are enough). From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap E.
- **About 10 records annotated from scratch,** with no drafted triples: measures how far drafted triples steer annotation (recall may be overstated by a model-drafted ground truth: [`metrics/recall.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/recall.md)). From: the literature review of 2026-10-07, after Fort & Sagot (2010), "Influence of Pre-annotation on POS-tagged Corpus Development", LAW IV ([paper](https://aclanthology.org/W10-1807/)), whose annotators tagged sentences both with and without pre-annotation [a model's draft], so the two could be compared. More evidence that a draft steers people, from the literature check of 2026-10-26: Schroeder, Roy & Kabbara (2025), "Just Put a Human in the Loop? Investigating LLM-Assisted Annotation for Subjective Tasks", Findings of ACL 2025 ([paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15821)): annotators strongly took the model's suggestions [drafted triples], and the scores of models measured on those labels [that ground truth] rose (their tasks were subjective; ours is not, so the size of the effect may differ); Huang et al. (2022), "Does Recommend-Revise Produce Reliable Annotations? An Analysis on Missing Instances in DocRED", ACL 2022 ([doi:10.18653/v1/2022.acl-long.432](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2022.acl-long.432)): an analysis of the facts missing from DocRED, whose people revised suggested triples [drafted triples] instead of writing them from the text. Step 050's drafting model (`DRAFTING_MODEL`, gemini-2.5-pro, made by Google) is from another maker than step 060's (`MODEL`, claude-opus-5.5, made by Anthropic; both in `common/common_helpers/llm.py`), which lessens this bias but doesn't remove it: models of different makers still tend to make the same mistakes ([`metrics/recall.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/recall.md#sources), *Sources*).
- **Listing likely pairs that the wording hides:** pairing compares words, not meaning, so the same fact in other words leaves an extracted only occurrence and a ground truth only triple ([`metrics/recall.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/recall.md#when-a-ground-truth-triple-counts-as-not-found), row 4). Step 070 could list, per record, each extracted only occurrence and ground truth only triple with the same predicate and the same subject instance or the same object instance (once evened out), e.g. "MODIS" ABOARD "Aqua" and "the imaging spectroradiometer" ABOARD "Aqua", for a person to review in the annotation tool as with partial pairs; a pair marked the same fact would then count. This parallels the component class mismatch check, which finds the same subject and object instances under different predicates. A person decides, as for partial pairs (every partial pair of the tuning part is already reviewed: partial pair review in the terminology). The closest standard is WebNLG 2020's Strict, Exact, Partial and Type matching (Castro Ferreira et al. 2020, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/2020.webnlg-1.7)), which 070's exact and partial pairs already follow ([`metrics/pairs.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/pairs.md#sources), *Sources*); a model judging whether two classed triples state the same fact has been tried (Sun et al. 2025, [arXiv:2509.25107](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25107), a preprint), but isn't a standard method. From: 2026-10-24; the literature check of 2026-10-26, for the sources.
- **A second annotator** for a sample of records, to measure agreement between people, as the average pairwise F1 (Hripcsak & Rothschild 2005, "Agreement, the F-Measure, and Reliability in Information Retrieval", *Journal of the American Medical Informatics Association* 12(3), [paper](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC1090460/)). If the second annotator also started from the drafted triples, both people could keep the same drafted mistakes, and agreement would look higher than it is; so the second annotator starts from scratch (the evidence: *About 10 records annotated from scratch*). From: the literature review of 2026-10-07; the literature check of 2026-10-26, for starting from scratch. Extended on 2026-11-01: **with the held-out number.** Why: one annotator with no agreement figure is a reviewer's first objection, and a number measured only on the records the rules were revised on estimates nothing beyond them (Artstein & Poesio, "Inter-Coder Agreement for Computational Linguistics", *Computational Linguistics* 34(4), 2008, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/J08-4004/)). Smallest version: a colleague or mentor annotates 5 held-out records from scratch with `annotations/guidelines.md` and the annotation tool, no draft shown (about an hour a record for a newcomer, by the review's estimate); I report the pairwise F1 of our two sets of triples and the disagreements by rule; and I finish 10 held-out records (drafted and extracted before 2026-11-20: *Extract and draft, before model access ends*, under *The whole pipeline*) and run 070 on them once, with the pipeline frozen. Held-out records already coin their own entries (*Held-out records may coin entries, marked held-out only*, under 050). Effort: 2 days of mine, 5 to 6 hours of the second person. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: only the drafts and extractions. From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap F.
- **A second person checking a sample of the translation table,** frozen before the held-out part is looked at. From: the literature review of 2026-10-07.
- **Kinds of translation, not only "means the same" (decided on 2026-11-01; done).** Done: the translation table has a required column `match`, how a row's component class relates to the one it translates to: `same`, `narrower`, `broader` or `overlap`, empty only on a `(none)` row, in plain words with their SKOS (W3C SKOS Reference, section 10: exactMatch, broadMatch, narrowMatch, relatedMatch) and Alignment API (=, <, >) equivalents in `docs/terminology.md`, *match*. Plain words because SKOS names the target's side, which reads backwards; four values, no exact/close split (a table of two schemas never chains matches) and no confidence (a person checks every row). The person sets it when checking (annotation tool, *Translation table*); step 070 sets `same` on a row it adds as `same component class`; a value outside the four, or one on a `(none)` row, stops step 070. Migration: the header gained the column the same day, every row's value empty; a checked row with no value is read and used, step 070 warns, listing it, and the annotation tool and the status count it as a row to check (row state *no match value*), so the baseline can be scored again before every row is set. Step 070 now also warns about a predicate's unconditional row marked `broader` or `overlap` with no conditional row under it (warn, not stop: for a meaning no entity class tells apart, nothing better exists), notes a swap on a symmetric predicate, asks the suggestion call about `broader` and `overlap` rows too (an entry the ground truth schema gained for the part of a row's meaning its target misses, e.g. HAS_VERTICAL_RESOLUTION beside HAS_RESOLUTION → HAS_SPATIAL_RESOLUTION, which nothing flagged before), and lists merged predicates in the readings, not only merged entity classes. Its report gains *Through which kind of row* per part (the occurrences by the match of their predicate's row, of those the ones left extracted only, the entity classes by their row's match, the recall upper bound split into through same or narrower rows, no match value yet, and only through broader or overlap rows, and the pin misfits: translated occurrences whose entity classes the ground truth schema's pins exclude, left in precision) and, under *Results*, *Translation disagreements* (the pin misfits per pair of rows, tuning part only). No credit for an inexact match in any metric, as in EDC and Text2KGBench: no formula changes, and no pair or overall number changes, so the evaluation version stays 3. Docs: `docs/terminology.md` (*match*, *translation table*, *row state*), `070_evaluate/070_evaluate.md` (stage 2, *Through which kind of row*, *Checks and warnings*, *Known limits*), the metric docs of the recall upper bound, schema fit and precision, `annotations/README.md`, `docs/upkeep.md`; tests: `tests/test_match_kinds.py`. From: the review of the translation table's inexact translations, 2026-11-01. Before: a row of the translation table said only which component class of the ground truth schema a component class of the current schema translates to (the same meaning, or else its main meaning, a broader one counting: the rule is under *translation table* in [`terminology.md`](terminology.md#7-evaluating-extraction-step-070)), or `(none)`; it doesn't say which of these it is. TRACE-KG (Abolhasani, Ba, He & Pan, "Traceable Context-Enriched Knowledge Graph Generation with Induced Schemas", arXiv:2604.03496v3, 18 Oct 2026, [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03496)) judges each pair of a ground truth schema component class and a candidate from the schema it learns: "Judgements are Equivalent, Narrower, Broader, or Unrelated", and "Equivalent counts as exact recovery and Narrower as compatible refinement" (section 5.2). A model proposes and verifies: it takes the closest candidates for each ground truth schema component class and checks each pair with "a structured LLM verifier, with targeted human auditing of low-confidence cases" (section 5.2). The confidence is the verifier's own: the model reports one with each judgement, and a judgement below 0.88 goes to a person (appendices C.2 and D). Their code has no 0.88 threshold: `TKG_Experiment_2.py` (commit ebe14b0), `write_audit_subset` (line 4653), writes, for each of the two splits it reports, up to 12 predicates and 10 entity classes of the ground truth schema for a person to check, the most used ones that found no counterpart first, chosen without the confidence (`audit_n = 12`, line 4170). Consider recording the kind in each row, e.g. narrower: suppose the current schema has MOUNTED_ON_SATELLITE and the ground truth schema has ABOARD; then MOUNTED_ON_SATELLITE is more specific than ABOARD, and its row would say narrower. Then decide which kinds count as within reach (narrower, probably; broader, not without more). Consider also whether a model's confidence could pick which rows a person must check, alongside *A second person checking a sample of the translation table*. A broader row is the case *Translation that depends on the entity classes* handles: SOURCED_FROM (current schema) is broader than FROM_MISSION (ground truth schema), and its object's entity class picks the narrower meaning. From: 2026-10-24.
- **A conditional row for supporting layers (for me to check; from 2026-10-31):** since HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA (ground truth schema) was coined on 2026-10-31, the current schema's PROVIDES ("supplies or produces the object, a data product, measurement, variable or information layer"), which translates to HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF, may need a conditional row translating it to HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA when its object is a supporting layer (quality flags, viewing geometry). Check it in the annotation tool's Translation table view; no row is added until I do. From: *The research of 2026-10-31 on the expert questions, applied*, above (Q3 (b)).
- **Component class mismatches seen again and again in the final re-score (from 2026-11-02; to decide after the TM).** The warnings of 070 runs 070_evaluate_2026-11-02_1355, 1356 and 1356-2 (the same extractions and translation as the final runs 1421, 1422 and 1422-2) list these, per repeat. For each, I decide whether a row of the translation table is wrong or extraction (or the ground truth) has a habit:
  - CONTAINS (current schema) → PART_OF (ground truth schema), subject and object swapped, where the ground truth triple says HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA (5 to 9 times), HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF (3) or HAS_VARIABLE (3) (ground truth schema);
  - PROVIDES (current schema) → HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF, where the ground truth triple says HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA (5): *A conditional row for supporting layers*, above;
  - HAS_COORDINATE_SYSTEM (current schema) → HAS_COORDINATE_SYSTEM, where the ground truth triple says ON_GRID (4 or 5);
  - COLLECTED_DURING (current schema) → HAS_MISSION_PHASE, where the ground truth triple says HAS_TIME_SPAN (3);
  - HAS_ABBREVIATION (current schema) → ALSO_KNOWN_AS, where the ground truth triple says HAS_IDENTIFIER (3 or 4);
  - entity classes: PhysicalQuantity (current schema) → PhysicalQuantity where the ground truth triple says Variable (5 to 8); TimePeriod → Time where it says MissionPhase (3 to 6) or TemporalResolution (3 or 4); Website → WebTool where it says Document (3); Instrument → Instrument where it says Channel (2); Topic → Discipline where it says Purpose (2).
  Not changed now: the TM's numbers are those of the final runs (submission on 2026-11-03), and a changed row needs a free re-score and a TM update. A conditional row can split CONTAINS only by an entity class: its occurrences with a PhysicalQuantity object could go to HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF, but HAS_VARIABLE ("holds this Variable in its files") and HAS_ANCILLARY_DATA ("holds this supporting information about its values, such as quality flags or viewing geometry") both take a Variable, so they differ by meaning and no entity class tells them apart. From: the final re-score, 2026-11-02.
- **Gaps in the translation table** (164 rows, commit f92cf0c), found when reviewing it: (1) the current schema (from step 040) has no predicate for FROM_MISSION or FROM_SPACECRAFT (ground truth schema), though they are common facts about a dataset, so those ground truth triples can never pair strictly and lower recall whatever extraction does. Why, from `py helpers/audit.py --induction` on the NASA laptop (040 run 040_induce_schema_2026-10-12_1714): the model did state "comes from" facts in 040, but they were filed under broad predicates: SOURCED_FROM (its definition: "originates from the object, a source instrument, platform, observation set, data portion or institution"; 75 matching triple instances; HAS_DATA_FROM was merged into it at stage 4), ACQUIRED_BY (an instrument or platform), and COLLECTED_DURING. So the current schema expresses a FROM_MISSION or FROM_SPACECRAFT fact only through SOURCED_FROM's object entity class (see *Translation that depends on the entity classes*). (2) HAS_OBJECTIVE and USED_TO_STUDY (current schema) both translate to HAS_PURPOSE (ground truth schema), pinned any -> Purpose, but nothing translates to Purpose; check it against step 040's patterns, which exist only in the run on the NASA laptop. From: 2026-10-22.
- **Other quality metrics,** e.g. coverage (see *Coverage of the ground truth schema, as a second number next to the recall upper bound*). 070 already reports F1, partial pairs, entity-class accuracy, the recall upper bound, recall within reach, describes accuracy, and precision and recall per predicate and per entity class. From: my old to-do list.
- **Decisions waiting for me: the translation table's match values and three rows to split (from the review of 2026-11-01).** No row's translation was changed and no conditional row added: these are mine to decide. In (2) to (4), each row's own component class and each condition's entity class are of the current schema, each target of the ground truth schema. (1) Set the `match` of every checked row that isn't `(none)` (rows checked before the column; the annotation tool lists them as *no match value*), comparing the two definitions; until then step 070 counts their occurrences under "no match value". (2) COVERS (current schema; its object "a time period, geographic area or measured variable") → HAS_TIME_SPAN: its other meanings are told apart by the object's entity class, so the review proposes `(none)` with conditional rows (object TimePeriod → HAS_TIME_SPAN; SpatialCoverage → HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE; PhysicalQuantity → HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF). (3) ACQUIRED_BY ("an instrument or platform") → ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT: proposed `(none)` with conditional rows (object Instrument → ACQUIRED_BY_INSTRUMENT; Spacecraft, SpaceStation or Aircraft → FROM_PLATFORM). (4) HAS_RESOLUTION ("spatial or vertical") → HAS_SPATIAL_RESOLUTION, though the ground truth schema has HAS_VERTICAL_RESOLUTION: `(none)` with a conditional row (object SpatialResolution → HAS_SPATIAL_RESOLUTION) once the current schema has an entity class for a vertical resolution; until then keep it, marked `broader`. TimePeriod → Time stays `broader` (an entity class's row takes no condition). Each split changes the numbers, so it is a recipe change: a new 070 run, compared with the baseline by `py helpers/compare_runs.py`, never mixed with it. From: the review of 2026-11-01, edge cases E5, E6, E9, E11 and E15.
- **Coverage of the ground truth schema, as a second number next to the recall upper bound:** TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3, as above) reports coverage: the proportion of the ground truth schema's component classes that the ground truth triples use ("ontology [ground truth schema] relations [predicates] appearing in the gold [ground truth] triples together with their domain and range concepts [subject classes and object classes]", section 5.2) that have an equivalent counterpart in the schema it learns ("Exact"), or an equivalent or narrower one ("Compat"; its table fits that reading, its text doesn't spell it out). It weighs them by use: "Anchors [these component classes] are frequency-weighted according to scope-specific gold [ground truth] triple counts" (appendix C.2; section 5.2 says "according to their occurrence in the corresponding split"). The [recall upper bound](../070_evaluate/metrics/recall_upper_bound.md) counts ground truth triples too, so a predicate many ground truth triples use weighs more. A count with each component class once, however often the ground truth triples use it, would show how many kinds of fact the current schema lacks. Example: suppose 200 ground truth triples use 20 predicates, 2 of them in 1 ground truth triple each, and no checked row translates to those 2. Then the recall upper bound is 198/200 = 99%, while 18 of the 20 predicates (90%) are within reach. Report the count per predicate and per entity class; if narrower rows count (see *Kinds of translation, not only "means the same"*), it counts them too. 070's report already lists, in its section *The translation table*, the current schema's component classes that translate to (none) and the ground truth schema's component classes nothing translates to, by name (the first 20), and its tables *Per predicate and per entity class* give, for each component class of the ground truth schema, its ground truth triples within strict reach; it gives no counts next to the recall upper bound. Report there, next to the recall upper bound: how many of the current schema's component classes translate to one of the ground truth schema and how many to (none), how many of the ground truth schema's component classes nothing translates to, and how many ground truth triples are out of reach. The NTRS draft ([`WIP_NTRS_Iordanescu.md`](WIP_NTRS_Iordanescu.md), section 6.2) should present the recall upper bound as our own measure: the literature check of 2026-10-26 found no published source for it (its reference [18] supports only the comparison with an early stage of a pipeline). Superseded by the literature search of 2026-11-06: the bound itself has sources in other fields (schema completeness; an earlier stage bounding recall), listed in [`recall_upper_bound.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/recall_upper_bound.md), *Sources*; what has no source is computing it per ground truth triple through a translation table for an induced schema and using it to attribute each miss. The NTRS draft should say this and credit them. From: 2026-10-24; the literature check of 2026-10-26, for the counts of the translation table and the NTRS draft.
- **The model's run-to-run variation** isn't among the reasons the numbers are approximate ([`metrics/approximately.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/approximately.md)), and margins of error leave it out. Add it once repeat runs measure it (see *The whole pipeline*). From: the methods review of 2026-10-11, item 6.
- **Comparing runs whose schemas differ:** several component classes of a schema may translate to one of the ground truth schema, so a finer schema is forgiven some wrong entity classes ([`metrics/pairs.md`](../070_evaluate/metrics/pairs.md), row 5 of *When a pair isn't the same fact*). `helpers/compare_runs.py` should say so when the runs' schemas differ. Partly done on 2026-11-01: a run by identity (060 with the ground truth schema) beside one through the translation table is refused unless `--across_schemas true`, which labels the report (`helpers/compare_runs.md`); two runs through the table with different induced schemas are still not flagged. On 2026-11-02 the decision rule takes `--across_schemas true` too, for two groups translated differently (each group's own runs one way), titled and labelled as a comparison across schemas: the induced schema's 3 repeats against the ground truth schema's 3 (`docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`, *Comparing with the ground truth schema*). From: the methods review of 2026-10-11, item 10.
- **The margin of error from few records (done on 2026-11-01).** Step 070's margin of error is now a clustered Wilson interval (`070_evaluate/metrics/approximately.md`, *Sampling error*), from 2 records on, in place of the percentile bootstrap with no margin below 20 records; `py helpers/compare_runs.py` keeps a paired bootstrap for differences, with Hesterberg's expanded percentile range and 10,000 redraws. Decided on 2026-11-01, each with its reason: below 10 records (`FEW_RECORDS`) each range carries a caveat that it may still be too narrow (our choice: the nearest published line is the few-clusters literature's "fewer than eleven clusters"); at 0% or 100% every record counts as one trial (our choice: the conservative side, since the design effect is undefined there); the method ships only because its coverage measured on synthetic records at 20 records is at least 90% (measured: 94.3% at 20, 95.3% at 10, 95.1% at 28; `tests/test_evaluate_intervals.py`); Brown, Cai & DasGupta (2001, [paper](https://doi.org/10.1214/ss/1009213286)) is cited only after checking its abstract. Still open from the item as it was: the 10-point threshold of 070's warning that a stratum's share of the evaluated records differs from its share of the pool has no source; the nearest convention is a standardized difference of 0.1, which "some researchers have proposed" as "meaningful imbalance" between two groups (Austin, "Balance diagnostics for comparing the distribution of baseline covariates between treatment groups in propensity-score matched samples", *Statistics in Medicine* 28, 2009, [paper](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3472075/)), while ours is in raw percentage points; to decide: a standardized difference in place of raw points. The paired comparison redraws whole records, not single triples, the usual way (Berg-Kirkpatrick, Burkett & Klein 2012, "An Empirical Investigation of Statistical Significance in NLP", EMNLP 2012, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/D12-1091/)). Small sets of records often can't tell two runs apart (Card et al. 2020, "With Little Power Comes Great Responsibility", EMNLP 2020, [paper](https://aclanthology.org/2020.emnlp-main.745)), so a difference between two runs on so few records may still be chance. From: the literature review of 2026-10-07; the literature check of 2026-10-26; the review of the intervals of 2026-11-01 (Bowyer, Aitchison & Ivanova, ICML 2025; Franco, Little, Louis & Slud 2019, [paper](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6690503/); Hesterberg 2015; Miller 2024: `metrics/approximately.md`, *Sources*).
- **Citations not yet checked:** Cochran (1977) for the 5% rule (chapter 2) and the ratio bias (chapter 6), cited from memory in the metric docs, and marked there as not verified in the book. Checked on 2026-10-31 through secondary sources only: a survey methods paper attributes the 5% rule to Cochran's p. 25 (Chromy, [AIR](https://new.air.org/sites/default/files/2021-06/Chromy_0.pdf)), and the ratio estimator's bias shrinks faster than its standard error (O(1/n) against O(n^-1/2)). Chapter 5 is checked in the book (section 5.3, p. 91: proportional allocation "gives a self-weighting sample"). From: the literature review of 2026-10-07.
- **The partial-pair verdicts in the recipe:** `helpers/compare_runs.py` compares two runs of 070 on records with the same ground truth, but a verdict changed between the runs (`annotations/partial_pair_reviews.csv`) also changes the numbers, and neither the recipe nor the ground truth's fingerprint holds the verdicts. Fingerprint each record's verdicts with its ground truth, so such a record is left out and named. From: the project sweep of 2026-10-14.
- **Twins and "evened out":** 070 finds twins by comparing `title` and `notes` fields with case, spacing (also between a number and its unit), quote marks, and dashes ignored (`common/common_helpers/ground_truth.py`), while the term *evened out* (`docs/terminology.md`) also ignores edge punctuation and a leading "a", "an", or "the". Decide which the twins should use, and make the guide and the code say the same. From: the project sweep of 2026-10-14.

## 080_build_graph

- **Before designing 080, read the rest of** Negro, Futia, Kůs, and Montagna, *Knowledge Graphs and LLMs in Action* (Manning, 2026): chapter 3 (RDF or a labeled property graph), chapter 4 (combining several sources: phase 1's graph and phase 2's triples), and chapters 13 and 14 (how people ask questions of a finished graph, for the competency questions). Chapters 5 to 8 and part of 14 were read on 2026-10-14 (the items here citing the book). From: 2026-10-14.
- **Three layers:** the records; each extracted triple as extracted (never joined with another), with its record and source text; and the knowledge graph built from those. The knowledge graph can then be rebuilt without asking a model again, and every edge can show the passage it came from. From: Negro, Futia, Kůs, and Montagna, *Knowledge Graphs and LLMs in Action* (Manning, 2026), section 6.1.
- **A layer of facts from the model's own knowledge:** joining names ("MODIS" = "Moderate-resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer (MODIS)"), reading codes ("MID-IR" in a title meaning SST4), and background facts, each marked as the model's, kept apart from the extracted triples (which stay what the record states), and checked by a person on a sample, with its own accuracy. From: 2026-10-19.
- **A record's node: `Dataset` or `CatalogEntry`?** Phase 1's graph makes each record a node labeled `Dataset`, while the describes triple gives the record the entity class `CatalogEntry` and gives `Dataset` (or `WebTool`, …) to what its title names. Decide which the graph uses before building it. From: the terminology sweep of 2026-10-15.
- **Linking subject instances and object instances to reference lists,** so that one thing named several ways ("Aqua", "EOS PM-1") becomes one node. First processing levels, a short fixed list (L0, L1A, L1B, L2, L3, L4: every spelling, such as "Level 3" or "L-3", mapped to one; decided on 2026-10-31 (research B1): any such list records each value's scheme (CODMAC, NASA/Earthdata, the PSA's, PDS4's), since the same words mean different things across them (a PDS "Level 2" is CODMAC 2, NASA's Level 0), and maps between schemes only through the crosswalk of the PDS3 Standards Reference, chapter 6 ([Chapter06.pdf](https://pds.nasa.gov/datastandards/pds3/standards/sr/Chapter06.pdf): CODMAC 2 = NASA Level 0, 3 = Level 1A, 4 = Level 1B, 5 = Levels 2 to 5); "Level 2 (EDR)" is never mapped to NASA's Level 2); then NASA's missions ([A to Z of NASA missions](https://www.nasa.gov/a-to-z-of-nasa-missions/)), where a subject or object instance with no match is marked for a person to check, not dropped, since the catalog also names missions of other agencies; GCMD keywords as another list; and the NASA Thesaurus, the NASA Scientific and Technical Information (STI) Program's controlled vocabulary of subject terms (about 18,400 terms, 4,300 definitions and 4,500 USE cross-references, across aerospace engineering, physics, astronomy, astrophysics, planetary science, Earth sciences and biological sciences), available as RDF/SKOS ([its page](https://sti.nasa.gov/nasa-thesaurus/), checked 2026-10-27). How: the subject or object instance keeps the record's words and gets an edge to its entry, saying how the match was made; candidates come from the list, never from a model (exact match after evening out, "Long Name (ACRONYM)" split into both forms, then near spellings as candidates only); a model chooses only among two or more candidates, and may answer "none of these"; a small hand-checked set of subject or object instance → entry (or none) measures it. Aliases ("EOS PM-1" for Aqua) must be written for our lists. From: my old to-do list; 2026-10-13; Negro, Futia, Kůs, and Montagna, *Knowledge Graphs and LLMs in Action* (Manning, 2026), chapters 7 and 8 (which choose without a "none" answer and don't measure linking); 2026-10-27, for the NASA Thesaurus. Found on 2026-10-31 (research of NASA's reference lists, Q4 (b), Q10, Q21; each source in its question's notes): no one list covers the catalog, so one list per domain, the A to Z List as a fallback for missions: for Earth science, GCMD's Projects and Platforms; for planetary records, the Planetary Data System's context products (investigations, instrument hosts, instruments and targets, each with the archive's other names for it); for heliophysics, SPASE's Observatory and Instrument resources; for disciplines, GCMD's Disciplines list (beyond Earth science, with "Agricultural Sciences" apart from the subject "Agriculture"); for what carries instruments, PDS4's Instrument_Host types and GCMD's platform categories, if a Platform entity class is coined. A caution: GCMD has instruments with the same short name as planetary ones but other meanings (its "RSI" is a "Remote Sensing Instrument", a camera; its "OSIRIS" the "Optical Spectrograph and Infrared Imaging System" of the Odin satellite), so a match must use the record's domain (its maintainer), never a name alone. Codes the ground truth leaves unexplained are the subject of *Knowledge enrichment from the model, verified and labelled*, below. Also a check in code (decided on 2026-10-31, with rule 1's *Names on an official NASA list*): every instance whose entity class rests on such a list is looked up in a downloaded copy of the list (GCMD's Instruments and Platforms keywords, PDS4's context products), under the record's own mission or platform, and a name missing from it is reported. Extended on 2026-11-01: **a measured link rate first.** Why: none of the above has been tried or measured, and a graph whose "Aqua" and "EOS PM-1" are two nodes can't answer the Aqua question (*Competency questions*, under *The whole pipeline*). Smallest version: every subject instance and object instance of the finished records' ground truth whose entity class is Instrument, Platform, Mission, Organization or Variable (a few hundred), matched evened out against GCMD's Platforms, Instruments, Projects and Science Keywords and the A to Z list; the share linked per entity class, each link checked by me, and the kinds of miss (an abbreviation, a qualifier, another agency's platform); then the same on the 1% run's triples (*Cost per run*). Effort: 1 to 2 days. Needs the models before 2026-11-20: no. From (extension): the review of 2026-11-01, *Beyond a pilot*, gap G.
- **Knowledge enrichment from the model, verified and labelled** (planned; decided on 2026-10-30). (1) A model proposes the meaning of each code or abbreviation the ground truth leaves out as unexplained (annotations/left_out.md is the list; e.g. "NSST"). (2) Each proposal is checked against an authoritative source (Earthdata, the data center's product pages, GCMD). (3) Accepted meanings enter the knowledge graph with their provenance (the proposing model and its version, the confirming source's URL, the date), and are never part of the ground truth or of extraction, so step 070's scores keep measuring the reading of the record. (4) Step 1 needs model access, so it must run before 2026-11-20 if it is done. Why step 2 is required, from 2026-10-30: asked about "NSST", gemini-2.5-pro expanded it as "Nighttime Sea Surface Temperature", but also said "Terra" (the record is about Aqua) and gave the sun-glint reason that the record gives for SST4: partly right, partly wrong. A fact to add this way, with its source (2026-10-31, research B2): MAIAC, the method of record "MODIS/Terra+Aqua Land Aerosol Optical Depth Daily L2G Global 1km SIN Grid", also retrieves aerosol optical depth over inland, coastal and open-ocean water (the MCD19A2 files have "Over water" layers; [MCD19 user guide](https://lpdaac.usgs.gov/documents/1500/MCD19_User_Guide_V61.pdf)), so the title's "Land" names the product suite, not all the area covered (my reading; checked on 2026-10-31: no source says so in words, and "Land" is the MODIS land discipline's naming, strong secondary: Q11's notes). Facts and links to add this way, found on 2026-10-31 (each source in its question's notes): PO.DAAC as a provider of its own under JPL (GCMD Providers, "NASA/JPL/PODAAC"; Q8); the OSIRIS calibration steps' names for the corrections of record "ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P OSINAC 4 ESC3-MTP020 RDR-INFLDSTR V1.0" (Q7); a link from "structure function (SF)" to the Unified Astronomy Thesaurus's "Time domain astronomy" (Q28); the GRaND map's grid of 114 fixed cells (Q1); that the Stardust RDR fluences stop at the anomaly, about 27 minutes into the encounter (Q5); the Aquarius data's latitudes (poleward of 50 degrees, EASE-Grid 2.0) and frequency (1.413 GHz), and SAC-D as the spacecraft (Q1, Q13, Q2); the MAIAC run that writes both the aerosol and the BRF files (Q20); MCDLC1KM, the land cover input of the LST emissivities (Q3); the MCD19A2 layer list (Q3); and links between the datasets of one MODIS Collection, a shared reprocessing (Q19). See also *Linking subject instances and object instances to reference lists*, above.
- **Which predicates give properties instead of edges,** by the rule of README, *Phase 1*: a processing level, for example, has few values, which people filter by but don't go from node to node through. From: my old to-do list; 2026-10-13.
- **Splitting a number with a unit** ("250 m") into a value and a unit, as graphs store quantities (Wikidata, QUDT, schema.org's QuantitativeValue). Reuse the parser 070 now pairs with (`common/common_helpers/text_match.py`, `quantity` and `QUANTITY_UNITS`: value, unit, and physical dimension, one table of units), so the graph and evaluation read a quantity the same way. From: 2026-10-11.
- **One written form for every value:** every exported value is written in one form, whatever spelling the record used: a number, a space, and the unit, as the SI brochure prescribes (e.g. 500 m, never 500m; 4.63 km; the only exceptions are °, ′ and ″ of a plane angle, written with no space). Matching (070, the annotation tool) already treats both spellings as the same, and, since 2026-10-24, every spelling of one quantity ("1 kilometer (km)", "1 km", "1000 m": `common/common_helpers/text_match.py`, `QUANTITY_UNITS`, the one table to write the form from); the graph must not keep both. From: 2026-10-20; BIPM, *The International System of Units (SI)*, 9th edition (2019), section 5.4.3.
- **A fact many records state:** one edge per extracted triple, or one edge listing the records it came from. Either way, keep which records each edge came from. From: 2026-10-06.
- **Joining subject instances and object instances that name the same thing into one node** beyond the lists: similar spelling plus shared neighbors (two spacecraft names spelled alike that carry the same instrument), grouped into connected components, the longest name kept; generic words ("Data", "Product", "Level") never count as similar. Which subject instances and object instances to join across records, and which to keep as one node per record: specific named things join (one MODIS node, one Aqua, one NASA), since they are important and specific. Generic descriptions don't: "L2 pixels" (record #4, pool position 4), "radiance data" and "a series of screens" (pool position 2) each mean that record's own data or method, so one shared "L2 pixels" node would wrongly connect unrelated datasets. Matching words alone can't tell the two kinds apart, so 080 needs a test for it: e.g. a proper name or an acronym against a common noun phrase, or a model's judgment checked on a sample. Organizations inside organizations: the ground truth keeps an organization's name whole, as written (e.g. "NASA Ocean Biology Processing Group (OBPG)", pool position 4), because "NASA" there says whose group it is, while in "NASA Technical Standards Program" (pool position 0) it is part of the name, and wording alone can't tell the two apart. So 080 should add part-of edges (OBPG part of NASA) from a known list of NASA's organizations, not from the words, so that such groups connect to the one NASA node. Groups named with a person: since 2026-10-30, rule 22 of the annotation guidelines splits "Peter Minnett and his team" (pool position 4) and drops "his team", so the ground truth has a "Peter Minnett" node that can join a "Peter Minnett" node from another record. Same name, different things: NASA often gives a mission and its spacecraft the same name (Dawn, Rosetta, Aqua). A Mission node and a Spacecraft node with the same name must stay two nodes, never joined because the names match. They may be linked (the spacecraft belongs to the mission), but only from a record that states it. What never to join, from TRACE-KG's prompt for joining (`TKG_Prompts.py`, lines 247–250): a thing and the entity class it belongs to ("Aqua" and "satellite"), a kind and a narrower kind ("sea surface temperature" and "skin sea surface temperature"), and a quantity and a measurement of it ("sea surface temperature" and "a sea surface temperature of 25 °C"). And never replace a node's words from the record with a model's: in TRACE-KG's audit, 16 of 249 joining actions (6.4%) were wrong changes to a thing's name, description, or type hint (appendix B.4.1; appendix C.3, Table 8). From: Negro, Futia, Kůs, and Montagna, *Knowledge Graphs and LLMs in Action* (Manning, 2026), section 6.1.4; 2026-10-19; 2026-10-22; TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3), for what never to join and for keeping the record's words. An example found on 2026-10-30: "ROSETTA-ORBITER" in two Rosetta records and "Rosetta Orbiter" in the ALICE record ("ROSETTA-ORBITER 67P/CAL ALICE 2 ESC1 V1.0"); rule 23 of *Writing a classed triple* picks one wording within a record only, so different records can still differ, and joining them is this item's job.
- **One name, two things (from 2026-11-02).** A record that says only "MODIS" may mean the MODIS aboard Terra or the one aboard Aqua: MODIS flies on both, and the record's text alone can't tell which. The ground truth keeps "MODIS", by rule 1 of *What counts as a fact* ("Only what the record states. Never add what you know from elsewhere"). So 080 must tell such subject instances and object instances apart, the opposite of *Joining subject instances and object instances that name the same thing into one node* (above): one "MODIS" node would wrongly join Terra's data with Aqua's. The record's other metadata fields may tell, e.g. its platform in NASA's Common Metadata Repository (CMR). From: 2026-11-02.
- **Checks of the graph:** how well nodes were joined (precision and recall of the joining), duplicate nodes, and a person checking a sample of edges. Joining is measured from both sides, as in TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3), appendix C.3, Table 8: precision over the joins made (of 249 actions checked by hand, 11, 4.4%, joined things that differ) and recall over a sample of nodes (of 120, 9, 7.5%, were still duplicates). From: the literature review of 2026-10-07; TRACE-KG (Abolhasani et al., arXiv:2604.03496v3), for the two-sided check.
- **Phase 1's maintainers:** README's table says 434 Maintainer nodes, but 020 joins maintainer spellings down to 422. Rebuild phase 1's part with 020's cleaning. From: my old to-do list.
- **More edges from the metadata fields other than the text fields** (phase 1): fields not used yet may give more edges, or properties. From: my old to-do list.

## Considered and not changed

Proposals that were looked at and decided against, or decisions to keep something as it is, so that nothing here is "fixed" back by accident. Read this before proposing a change to a rule, the ground truth, a prompt or a metric. Each entry: what was proposed, what was decided, why, when, and where it lives. A decision with a home of its own elsewhere is listed here with a pointer.

**Annotation guidelines and ground truth** (`annotations/guidelines.md`; rule numbers as of 2026-10-30)

- **Capitals, spacing, hyphens and punctuation sentences of rule 23 stay out of the model's copy of the rules** (`common/common_prompts/extraction_rules.txt`). Decided when the capitals sentences were written (commit ef16bc5); copied into the model's copy by mistake on 2026-10-30 (commit 1f82845, applying audit finding R5) and restored the same day. Why: step 070 ignores capitals, spacing, punctuation and dashes when pairing, so they change no score, and the model has fewer rules to follow. Home: rule 23's note.
- **Title-case ties in rule 23** (considered on 2026-10-30): treating a title in title case like one in all capitals, which would change four instances ("Swath" → "swath", "Weekly" → "weekly", "Daily" → "daily" twice). Not adopted: step 070's pairing ignores case, and step 080 will normalize names, so the choice changes neither scores nor the graph.
- **Different wordings of one thing across records** ("ROSETTA-ORBITER" in two records, "Rosetta Orbiter" in another; 2026-10-30): rule 23 works within a record only. Joining them is step 080's job (*Joining subject instances and object instances that name the same thing into one node*, under 080).
- **Rule 23's "first wording wins" for small differences** (approved, then withdrawn on 2026-10-30 before it was applied): it would have made an all-capitals title win ("VESTA" over "Vesta"), against the capitals sentence. Rule 23 keeps "where the record isn't typed all in capitals … the form used most often (on a tie, the first)".
- **Keeping a qualifier inside an object that describes** ("rule 19 doesn't apply to it", e.g. Aqua's purpose keeping "Earth"; rejected on 2026-10-27): every triple follows the same rules; a small exception would slip past the model; keeping qualifiers in some instances and out of others is the inconsistency a knowledge graph must avoid. Not reopened by rule 19's *A country* of 2026-10-31, which keeps a country in the instance only when the words it qualifies name no Person or Organization, in an instance of any entity class. Home: rule 19, and *Rule 19 can lose which fact a qualifier went with* (050), which also records options 1 and 3 as not adopted and option 4 as future work.
- **Rule 22's "when each item alone is a complete object"** (dropped on 2026-10-30): a judgment call, not a test. Rule 22 now splits every list, with only the collective-"and" and period-or-range exceptions. Still not adopted after 2026-10-31, when rule 22's describing words got the question "can one of the things they describe have all the describing words at once?": that question asks about one item and the words, which the annotator checks against what the record means; "a complete object" asked whether an item could stand alone, which no one can check the same way.
- **The seven-word collective-"and" test** ("between", "continuity", "combined", "merged", "ratio", "difference", "compared"; approved, then not written on 2026-10-30): it would have kept three correctly split lists whole ("Normalized Difference Snow Index (NDSI) and a series of screens", "between … NASA and … CONAE", "Combined Terra and Aqua"). Replaced by the five nouns of rule 22's exception.
- **Searching the whole catalog to shape a rule** (considered on 2026-10-30, for the collective-"and" nouns): not done. The catalog includes the ground truth candidates pool, and with it the held-out records the final scores come from; a rule shaped by their text would make those scores overstated. Any such search must leave out the whole pool (search the induction candidates only). The collective-"and" exception became a definition instead (050, *Audit of the rules and the ground truth*).
- **Inference by confidence** ("when we are 97% sure"; considered on 2026-10-30): no one can check a confidence, two annotators (or the annotator and the model) would differ, and an inferred fact has no source text. Instead, rule 1 of *What counts as a fact* allows a fact only through a written test met by the record's own statements (SUPERSEDES, comparisons).
- **The model's own knowledge in the ground truth or extraction** (considered on 2026-10-30): kept out, so that step 070 measures how well the pipeline reads the record. Home for using it: *Knowledge enrichment from the model, verified and labelled* (080). One exception since 2026-10-31: the entity class, and only the entity class, of a name an official NASA list names for the record's own mission or platform (rule 1's *Names on an official NASA list*; the model's copy, *Names you recognize*), because a person checks it against the list, not against what the model knows. Widened on 2026-11-12 (my decision): the entity class may also come from the GCMD Providers keywords or, when no NASA list names the thing, from Wikidata, and the rule's part is now called *Names in a public reference*. The reason is the same: a person checks the entity class against a reference anyone can look up, and every fact must still be stated by the record (050_annotate, *An entity class may come from a public reference*).
- **Kept after the audit of 2026-10-30:** "Version 6.1" SUPERSEDES "Version 6" (G4, with the test now in SUPERSEDES's definition); SST4 HAS_LOWER_UNCERTAINTY_THAN "skin SST" (G5, by the comparison test); "Aquarius L-band radiometer" whole (G8: the words left without "radiometer" aren't a name); the ALICE record's target on the phase ("comet escort 1" HAS_TARGET "67P/Churyumov-Gerasimenko"), with no HAS_TARGET for the dataset from the notes (rule 19's subject rule; since 2026-11-01 the dataset has one from the title's "67P": the second opinion's results, 050); rule 19's two judgment tests (R10: identical wording gets opposite answers, "Ocean Cloud Cover" vs "Ocean Surface Temperature", so no word test exists). Home: the audit entry (050).
- **"his team" as a subject** ("Peter Minnett and his team"; 2026-10-30): neither kept whole (against rule 22) nor kept as a subject of its own (it names no specific team; rule 9 of *What counts as a fact*). The four triples have the subject "Peter Minnett"; "his team" is in `annotations/left_out.md`.
- **Team as an entity class with a named example** ("the MODIS Science Team"; 2026-10-30): a named group is an Organization, so the example contradicted Team's definition; Team was removed instead.
- **The ground truth's "RELATED_TO" for "average atomic mass"** stays (Q17, answered). Home: `annotations/questions_for_experts.md`, Q17.
- **Expert questions withdrawn as settled from sources:** Q16 and Q22 (and parts of Q15 and Q23); on 2026-10-31, parts of Q1, Q2's notes, Q3, Q6, Q10, Q18, Q19, Q20 and Q28 (research B14, and the decisions of that day); later on 2026-10-31, Q1 (b), Q2 (b), Q3 (a)'s first half, (b) and (c), Q4 (b), Q5 (c), Q6 (a), Q7 (b) and (c), Q8 (a) and (b), Q18 (a), Q25 (a), and all of Q9, Q19, Q20, Q23 and Q28 (the research of 2026-10-31). Home: `annotations/questions_for_experts.md`, each question's notes.
- **"Land" in "Land Aerosol Optical Depth" stays an area the data cover** (considered on 2026-10-31, research B2): MAIAC also retrieves over water, but the record's words say "Land" and rule 19's ground-or-water test takes it out ("the aerosol optical depth of the land" is false); outside knowledge never overrides what a record says (rule 1). Noted in Q11's notes; its outside fact goes to *Knowledge enrichment from the model, verified and labelled* (080).
- **"Global Gravity" stays split** into HAS_MEASUREMENTS_OF "Gravity" and HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Global" (considered on 2026-10-31, research B3): "Global Gravity" is a value of the archive's OBSERVATION_TYPE keyword, but whether it names a kind of measurement as a whole waits on the experts' answer to Q3. Reversed later on 2026-10-31, on new evidence (the team's naming document defines the keyword as the "Mode of the executed measurement", and "Phobos Gravity" names a target, not an area): "Global Gravity" is kept whole, HAS_VARIABLE "Global Gravity" (Variable), and HAS_SPATIAL_COVERAGE "Global" is deleted (*The research of 2026-10-31 on the expert questions, applied*, under 050). Kept here so that the split is not brought back: a split by position would read "Phobos" as an area in the nine "Phobos Gravity" records.
- **"daily" of the swath snow record stays its temporal resolution** (considered on 2026-10-31, research B5): NSIDC gives the data set's temporal resolution as "5 minute", one granule, but "most locations on Earth are imaged at least once per day" ([NSIDC user guide](https://nsidc.org/sites/default/files/myd10_l2-v061-userguide_0.pdf), 2.2), so for each location the values are about a day apart (checked on 2026-10-31: exactly so for the land surface temperature record, one clear-sky value per cell a day, and only roughly for this swath record and for the aerosol record, whose daily files hold one value per pass, up to 30 a day near the poles; Q5's notes), which fits TemporalResolution's definition; the record says "provides daily snow cover".
- **"radiance data" stays one input dataset with two DOIs** (considered on 2026-10-31, research B7): the two DOIs are two products, but splitting them needs a rule 11 exception to tell two same-worded instances apart; it waits on the experts' answer to Q12.
- **SST4 ESTIMATES "SST" stays** (considered on 2026-10-31, research B9): the OBPG's SST4 document says it returns the skin sea surface temperature, but the record says only "SST" there; outside knowledge never overrides the record's words (rule 1).
- **No PDS3 title convention in rule 1's closed list** (considered on 2026-10-31, research B10): the PDS3 Standards Reference (section 6.3.2) puts the instrument host first in a data set name, but the host can be Earth-based, a lander or a rover, and the order of the other parts varies (DAWN titles drop the level; an OSIRIS title can put the mission phase where the target goes), so a convention by position would misread some titles. The entity classes of "DAWN" and "STARDUST" stay choices informed by sources; "RSI", whose entity class depends on its meaning, is left out (above). Since 2026-10-31 rule 1 reads a name's entity class from an official NASA list instead, not from a title's order: "RSI" is an Instrument by its PDS4 context product, and "DAWN" and "STARDUST" are the PDS4 instrument hosts spacecraft.dawn and spacecraft.sdu. This entry stands for the order of a title's parts.
- **Discipline and HAS_DISCIPLINE stay defined** with no ground truth triple (considered on 2026-10-31, with B4): a field of science is still a fact a record may state, and Q10 asks which list of disciplines to follow.
- **"known issues" as an Issue documented at the page** ("known issues" (Issue) DOCUMENTED_AT the page; considered on 2026-10-31): not done. It would leave the issues joined to no other instance (no predicate joins a dataset to its issues), and "known issues" names no particular issue; the record's dataset DOCUMENTED_AT the page stays, which is what `foaf:page` and `dcat:landingPage` record. Home: `annotations/left_out.md`, the item "known issues".
- **A predicate for the ground each file covers** ("1,200 by 1,200 km"; considered on 2026-10-31): not coined. The record doesn't say the size is per file (NASA LAADS does), so the fact would rest on outside knowledge (rule 1). Home: *Missing twin predicates in the ground truth schema* (050).
- **A predicate for a use** ("can be used for", Wikidata's P366; considered on 2026-10-31, for what the structure function "allows for"): not coined. Only one record needs it, so it would tailor the schema to that record; the words are left out by rule 8's *A study a record tells*. Home: *The research of 2026-10-31 on the expert questions, applied* (050), and Q9's notes. Since 2026-10-31 rule 9 of *What counts as a fact* gives the reason for every record: a use is a fact of the work that used the data, and gives no triple when the record doesn't name that work.
- **An entity class for a kind of measurement** (Q3's old option 5, for "Global Gravity", "Occultation", "Solar Conjunction"; considered on 2026-10-31): not coined. "Global Gravity" is a Variable, and no other record needs the entity class. Home: the same item, and Q3's notes.
- **A Program entity class, or PO.DAAC as an Organization of its own** (Q8's old options 3 and 2; considered on 2026-10-31): not done. The records' words give a program as a named institution and the archive as JPL's role, and the field's vocabularies split on programs. Home: the same item, and Q8's notes.
- **"Earth" taken out of "Earth observation"** (considered on 2026-10-31, rule gap 9): "Earth observation needs" gives HAS_TARGET "Earth" and "observation needs", by rule 19's target body. Keyword vocabularies keep "Earth observation" as one term (Wikidata's Q1348989), but keeping it would bring back the exception rejected on 2026-10-27 (*Keeping a qualifier inside an object that describes*), and both facts stay written. A narrow option, if it is ever wanted: a target body stays when it and the next word form a name the record uses as one (capitalized, or with an abbreviation of its own; rule 11).
- **The OSIRIS calibration steps' names as methods** ("Radiometric calibration" for the record's "radiometric calibrated"; considered on 2026-10-31): not done. They are not the record's words (rule 11 of *Writing a classed triple*), and the record's participles name no method (rule 24's *Words that name no method*); the names go to 080's enrichment. Home: the same item, and Q7's notes.

**Evaluation and pipeline**

- **An AI model as a third pair level** (judging whether two differently worded triples state the same fact): not used; every run would cost model calls, and a model tends to favor output like its own. A person checks a sample instead. Home: `070_evaluate/metrics/pairs.md`, *The same fact in different words*.
- **Inducing the schema from a larger or more varied sample, to reach the 40 facts out of reach** (considered on 2026-10-30): not done; 12 of the 13 ground truth records come from the maintainers the schema was induced from, step 040's saturation curve is nearly flat for predicates (2.8 added by the last 25 of 150 texts), and 69 of the 104 induced predicates translate to (none) because their meaning splits. Conditional rows were built instead (*Translation that depends on the entity classes*, 070). **Reversed on 2026-11-14:** 8 of the 80 finished ground truth records now come from maintainers the 2026-10-12 sample never read (checked against that schema's `made`, `maintainers`), so the sample now draws from every stratum of the pool (*The sample of texts*, under 040, done).
- **Re-attaching a predicate to another subject in the translation table** (considered on 2026-11-01): not done. A fact the two schemas attach to different subjects (a resolution stated of a variable in the current schema, of the dataset in the ground truth) never pairs; EDOAL can express such a correspondence by a path, but a row translates a predicate in place, and no column can change that. Kept as a known limit instead. Home: `070_evaluate/070_evaluate.md`, *Known limits*.
- **Credit for a broader or narrower translation in the metrics** (considered on 2026-11-01): not done. The extraction benchmarks score a triple only after canonicalization to one target or none (EDC; Text2KGBench), and the Ontology Alignment Evaluation Initiative scores non-equivalence correspondences by hand or by relaxed measures, so no standard automatic credit exists; step 070 counts by match kind instead and leaves every formula as it is. Home: `docs/terminology.md`, *match*.
- **Rerunning step 040 casually:** a new induced schema would need the translation table checked again. Home: `docs/upkeep.md`, *Translation table*.
- **The fixed numbers of step 070** (`FEW_RECORDS`, `Z` and the others) are fixed on purpose. Home: 070's guide.
- **`splits.json` is written once and kept**, so a re-harvest doesn't reorder the pool. Home: 030's guide.
- **Prompt caching:** step 060, and step 040's stage 2 (extracting triple instances, since 2026-11-14), mark the identical start of their prompts for caching; 040's other stages are not cached on purpose (small saving), and 050's drafting passes no cache mark (it was planned for a Gemini model, whose caching works differently: 060's guide, *Prompt caching, for cost*). Home: `docs/findings.md`, 2026-10-12.
- **What never travels through Git** (the key and the outputs). Home: `docs/running_on_nasa_laptop.md`.
